Baba could not disregard the sweet and loving command of the girl. He ate everything she had brought. Then he said, "Don t come again."

The girl went away. As she was going she turned again and again and looked at Baba with a mysterious smile. Simultaneously Baba felt that the suffering of his body was gone. He opened the bandage of the foot. To his surprise he found that the sore had disappeared The whole thing aroused suspicion in his mind. He went to the home of the girl and asked her mother, "Where is Lali?"

"Lali is gone to her father-in-laws."

"When did she go?

"About three months ago."

Then Baba understood everything He returned to his cottage without saying anything further for fear of publicity. But very soon it became known to everyone that Baba had been mercifully blessed by Radharani, for the sore on his foot had mysteriously disappeared and his emotional state also had undergone a sudden change He always cried, "Ha! Radhey Karunamayi (embodiment of mercy)!" and wept.

Once Baba desired to do the *patha* (reading) of Srimad Bhagavata. The Vrajabasis made necessary arrangements. Baba started reading and explaining Rasapancadhyayi (the five chapters of Bhagavata relating to RasaliIa). A *doma* child (child belonging to one of the lowest castes) came everyday and listened to the *patha* most attentively. The Vrajabasis did not like this. But Baba did not object to his coming. On the last day, when the *patha* was to be completed, the child came like one possessed by someone else and sat on Baba s lap. In the midst of *patha* he inquired, "Baba, after Rasa-IiIa, where did Krsna rest, in Seva-kunja or in Samketa-vana?"

Before Baba could reply there was a loud sound like the explosion of a bomb. His soul took it s flight through his *brahma-randhra* (the aperture at the crown of the head) and probably reached where Radha and Krsna were resting after *rasa.*

## Chapter 8 - Sripada Navakisora Gosvami and Sripada Locanananda Gosvami

Sripada Navakisora Gosvami and Sripada Locanananda Gosvami were the sons of Sripada Laksmikanta Gosvami, a descendant of Śrī Nityananda Prabhu. They lived in Dhacca. Both the brothers were learned and intensely devoted. Both renounced the world in the prime of their youth and went to Vrndavana for *bhajana.*

They were conscious of the utter necessity of a spiritual guide and wanted to take shelter under *siddha* Srikrsna Dasa Baba of Govardhana. But they thought that if they revealed their identity to him, he would regard them as worshipable being the descendants of Nityananda Prabhu; and would not accept them as his pupils. Therefore they told him that they belonged to a low caste and requested him to accept them as his servants and guide them in *bhajana.*

Krsnadasa Baba was impressed by their sincerity and keenness. He kept them in his *asrama* and began to teach them *astakalina-lila-smarana* (meditation of Krsna-IiIa as performed during the eight parts of the day). Krsnadasa Baba had become very old. He needed able and sincere persons like them to serve him. So he began to accept all kinds of service from them without any hesitation. They also began to render all kinds of service, even menial service, most faithfully.

After some time their younger brother came to Govardhana in quest of them. He went to Krsnadasa Baba s *asrama.* At that time the two brothers had gone to Manasa Ganga for bathing. When they returned they saw that Siddha Baba was red with anger and their younger brother was sitting by his side. They understood that Baba was angry because he had come to know about their lineage. They fell at his feet and asked for pardon. Siddha Baba said in a voice ringing with rage, "You are a fraud! You have usurped my right to serve and forced your service upon me by deceit. Could there be a greater treachery than this? I never imagined that Prabhu-santana (descendants of Nityananda Prabhu) could be such impostors!"

The two brothers said with folded hands and tears flowing from their eyes, "Maharaja! We are great sinners. Birth after birth we have been accumulating sins. It is on account of those sins that this time we are born in a family, in which by the very nature of our birth we are removed from *bhakti.* Our birth and lineage, on account of which we are regarded as worshipable by society, have filled us with pride and rendered us ineligible for *bhakti.* We realized that unless we came down from the high pedestal on which we have been placed by destiny and take shelter under the feet of a *mahabhagavata* *(siddha* saint), we cannot hope to attain *bhakti.* We knew that no one would accept us if we disclosed our identity.

Therefore we were compelled to adopt this course. We have undoubtedly committed an offence. You can now give us whatever punishment you think we deserve."

Krsnadasa Baba said, Your punishment is this: Both of you wash your feet with your own hands and give the *caranamrta* to me to drink. Thus only the offence you have committed by fraudulently serving me can be expiated."

The Gosvami brothers had to obey, though most unwillingly and painfully, to pacify Baba. Baba was pacified. He said, I am happy that Krsna gave me the company of two great persons like you. It is out of *vaisnava* humility that you regarded your birth and lineage as a curse. In fact it is due to your lineage that you are so humble and devoted. I do not have even a semblance of the humility and devotion you possess. You now continue to stay in this *asrama* and do *bhajana.* I shall consider myself honoured and blessed."

But the Gosvami brothers pleaded their inability to stay in the *asrama* anymore. How could they stay there to be revered by a Siddha Baba and his disciples, whom they revered themselves? They sought a solitary place in a remote village to the west of Kamyavana, called Pasupa and began to live there.

From time to time they went to kamyavana to have *darsana* of *siddha* Śrī Jaikrsnadasa Baba. Their acquaintance with Śrī Jaikrsnadasa Baba was not new. They used to go to him for his *satsanga* (holy company) even before they renounced the world. Śrī Jaikrsnadasa Baba was at that time a powerful magnet of *bhakti*, who attracted not only the *sadhakas* from different places, but also the deities. Once when Navakisora Gosvami had gone to stay with him for some time, he had also carried with him his deity Śrī Radha-Madanamohana. Śrī Radha-Madanamohana felt so much attracted by Baba that one night he told Navakisora Gosvami in a dream, "You have served me well so long.

I was happy with your service. But now I would like to stay with Jaikrsnadasa Baba Ji and accept his service. I will not go back to Dhacca. "Navakisora Gosvami had to go back alone, Radha-Madanamohana Ji remained with Jaikrsnadasa Baba. So in going to Jaikrsnadasa Baba Ji, Navakisora Gosvami used to feel double attraction, the attraction of Baba and the attraction of his own Thakura, Śrī Radha-Madanamohana.

After some time the Gosvami brothers received the information that all the other members of their family were dead and there was no one to look after the family deity. So they had to go back to Dhacca At that time the income of the family was meagre and it was difficult to carry on the service of the deity satisfactorily. The deity told them in a dream, "Do not bother about my service. In the village Fardabaja in district Cattagrama there lives a devotee, who is a big landlord. Everyone in his family will soon die. No one will be left to own his property. You take me to him. He will transfer all his property to me. My service will be performed with the income from that property."

Both the brothers then took their deity to the landlord and told him about the dream. He gladly transferred all his property to the deity by a deed and appointed the two brothers as *sevaits* (servitors). The service of the deity began to be performed in right royal manner. Samkirtans and festivals were frequently held and *prasada* was given to all and sundry.

The two brothers had renounced the world for *bhajana.* But they were brought back to the world by the will of Krsna. Did this in any way block their progress in *bhakti?* It did not. For the world in which they now lived was the world of Krsna. They lived and breathed in the world of Krsna for Krsna. Therefore they continued to rise in *bhakti* in stages till the stage of accomplishment *(siddhavastha)* was reached. This is evident from one or two episodes in the life of Locanananda Prabhu which are well known.

Once Locanananda Prabhu was invited to a *samkirtana* at the house of Śrī Kartika Adhikari, a famous physician of Fardabaja. Locanananda was dancing in the *samkirtana.* Suddenly overpowered by a fit of emotion *(bhava)* he ran out of the house and disappeared. Even after the *samkirtana* was over he did not return. Adhikari ji got the whole of Cattagrama searched, but he was not found anywhere. The second day a wider search was organised. People were sent to Navadvipa, Nilacala and Vrndavana. On the fourth day, when Adhikari Ji was sitting with a number of devotees in his drawing room, he saw that some cows had assembled around the garbage-pit in front of his house. Some of them standing at the edge of the pit were looking amazedly at something below, some seemed to be smelling something in the pit and some, intoxicated with the smell, were dancing in an exalted mood with their tails turned upwards. This aroused the curiosity of Adhikari Ji and others. They went near the pit and saw the body of a man buried in the garbage, head downwards. Whose body could it be? They pulled it out of the garbage. "Oh, Locanananda Prabhu!" they shouted in astonishment. Locanananda Prabhu had run away from *samkirtana* in a state of trance and fell into the pit. After *samkirtana* there was a feast of about 500 people, who were served on plantain leaves. The leaves with the remnants of food, which were thrown into the pit, had covered his entire body. Cows had gone into the pit to eat the leaves and the food. In the process his feet were uncovered. They started smelling and licking the feet. This filled them with such an exaltation that they went out of the pit and started dancing in ecstasy.

The body of Locanananda Prabhu was washed, but there was no sign of life in it. Everyone began to mourn his death. Everyone was sorry that a saintly person like him should have had that kind of end. But Adhikari Ji, who was not only a competent physician but also an advanced devotee examined the body carefully and said, "Prabhu Ji is not dead. If he was dead, his body could not remain fresh even after three days." He asked everybody to perform *kirtana.* As soon as *kirtana* was started, Prabhu stood up and began to dance in ecstasy as before. When he came to his own, people asked him how he ran away from the *samkirtana* and lay in the garbage for three days. He said that he knew nothing about it.

Sitakunda Candranatha, a place in district Cattagrama, is famous for the temple of Sambhu- natha and the 15 days long fair held there on the occasion of Siva-caturdasi. Thousands of *sadhus* and pilgrims attended the fair. The gate of the temple is opened at midnight for *darsana.* Once Locananahda Prabhu went there with a number of devotees. At midnight, when he was going with the devotees for the *darsana* of Sambhunatha, he met a Jogisvara near Vyasakunda and the temple of Kala-bhairava. He was tall and fat. His black body was smeared with ashes. A garland of bones hung around his neck. His long, yellow coloured matted hair touched his feet. He wore a *tripunda tilaka* on his forehead and his eyes scintillated like balls of fire. The devotees were frightened to see him. But he fell prostrate before Locanananda Prabhu and supplicated, "My Lord! I am the presiding deity of this place. My name is Kala-bhairava. I have been accepting the sins of people. Thereby I have myself become heavily burdened with sins. I have been anxiously awaiting your arrival here for my deliverance. Kindly have mercy on me." Locanananda Prabhu gave Krsna- mantra to him. He again prostrated reverentially before him, offered a gold *mohur* at his feet and disappeared.

In Fardabaja there was a river about half a mile from the house of Adhikari Ji. Locanananda Prabhu used to go to that river for bathing every morning. Once the village was afflicted with cholera. The epidemic was taking a heavy toll of life everyday. One day, when Locanananda Prabhu was returning from the river after bath, a fiendish old lady, emitting foul smell from her body, touched his feet in obeisance. He burst out with anger, "Who are you, dirty and despicable lady? You have spoiled the water I was carrying from the river for the deity by touching me."

The lady trembled with fear. She said with folded hands, "Prabhu, I am a great sinner. My only function is to kill innocent people. Besides I have committed an offence by touching your holy feet. I do not know what my fate is going to be."

"But who are you?" shouted Locanananda Prabhu.

"I am *visucika devi*" (the goddess of cholera) replied the old lady.

Locanananda Prabhu took pity on her and gave her Harinama. Then she said, "Prabhu, you are my *guru.* It is my duty to make some offering to you. What offering should I make?"

"You leave the village immediately and never come here again. This is the only offering I can accept."

The lady obeyed.

Locanananda Prabhu again went to the river and bathed. On his return he told Adhikari Ji, "Your village is now free from the scourage of cholera for all times."

Surprisingly all the cholera patients in the village recovered immediately and not once since then anyone in the village has suffered from cholera. What s more, patients of cholera from other villages also are cured on coming here.

After some time both Sripada Navakisora Gosvami Prabhu and Sripada Locanananda Gosvami Prabhu went to Jagannatha Puri at the time of Ratha-yatra. Sripada Navakisora Gosvami Prabhu left the body while having Jagannatha Ji s *darsana* at the time of Ratha-yatra. Locanananda Prabhu then sat at the feet of his dead body and said, "Prabhu! You kept me with you like the very shadow of your body since birth. Will you now leave me alone? With this he also left his body and followed the elder brother to *nitya dhama* (the eternal abode of Radha and Krsna).

## Chapter 9 - Śrī Jagannatha Dasa Baba Ji

Śrī Jagannatha Dasa Baba Ji had a very long span of life. He lived for 147 years. He used to stay for six months in Vraja and six months in Navadvipa. He was known throughout Bengal and Vraja as a *siddha purusa.*

Once when he was in Navadvipa, Gopinatha Raya and Janaki Natha Raya, two self-conceited landlords of Bhagyakula went to see him. They said to him, "Baba, we are told that you are a *siddha purusa.* Show us some miracle."

"What? Who told you that I am *siddha*?" As Baba said this he tapped his stick against the ground several times.

"Let us go. Baba is angry," said the frightened landlords and began to leave.

"No, No, I am not angry. I was only trying to drive away a she-goat, who was eating up the *tulasi* plant in Lokanatha Gosvami s *kunja* in Radha-Kunda," Baba said humbly.

The Landlords were surprised. How could Baba see and drive away an animal hundreds of miles away? Immediately they sent a reply-paid telegram to someone in Radha-Kunda to seek confirmation of the episode. The reply was that a she-goat had actually eaten up the *tulasi* plant in Lokanatha Gosvami Kunja that day.

The Landlords again went to Baba and apologized for their arrogance. When they had gone Baba said to Bihari, his disciple. "Poor souls of Kali! They would not believe without seeing. I showed them something so that they might not suffer on account of any offence against me."

The landlords were fools to have tested Baba s accomplishment as a saint by asking him to show a miracle. Baba s *siddha* state was obvious. He was himself a miracle. He. was at that time one hundred and twenty-five years old. His body was bent like a semi-circle. His long brows hung before his eyes like curtains. He could hardly walk. If he had to go anywhere, his disciple Bihari had to carry him on his shoulders. Still his stamina in *bhajana* was unique. He would do *japa* almost throughout the night. In the morning he would perform a thousand *dandavats* before the deities. He would often fast continually for three days without even drinking water. He would offer *tulasi* leaves to Giridhari with the help of his two disciples, who would stand on either side of him and lift his eye-brows with their hands to enable him to do so. In *samkirtana* he would dance and sing and while dancing jump up to four feet above the ground in ecstasy.

The great saints do not like to exhibit their power of performing miracles. But they are sometimes compelled to do so to instill faith in sceptical minds or to relieve someone s suffering out of mercy. Jagannatha Dasa Baba also had to do this sometimes.

Once the entire *Navadvipa* was flooded. Almost everybody had to quit. But Baba did not. Bihari, who was his only attendant at that time, fell seriously ill. Pyarimohana Gosvami of Mahaprabhu s temple called a doctor from Calcutta. The doctor pronounced his condition as very critical. He said, "The patient would die before day-break." Lala Babu called his Kaviraja. He also confirmed what the doctor had said. Then Baba said, "Allright, *\\* will see who takes Bihari away from me." He sat near his bed and began to do *japa.* After half an hour Bihari opened his eyes and said, Baba, I am very hungry." Baba immediately cooked some *mohana-bhoga*and gave it to him to eat. After he had eaten, Baba asked him to go and cook for the deity. Bihari took his bath and began to cook.

Bihari was a *vrajabasi.* He did not know Bengali. But Baba asked him to read Caitanya-caritamrta everyday so that he could listen. He said, "Baba, I do no know Bengali." But Baba asked him to purchase a copy of Caitanya-caritamrta, which he did. He then asked him to read. Bihari only looked vacantly at his face. He said angrily, "Don t Look at me. Look at the book." Bihari began to look at the book and to his surprise he found that he was able to 1 A kind of pudding read Caitanya-caritamrta like one, who had already learnt Bengali and studied Caritamrta well.

Similarly, Baba said to Bihari one day, "Bihari, I shall sing, you accompany me on the *mrdanga*."Bihari said, "Baba! I have never even touched a *mrdanga.* How can I play on it? "There is no harm in trying," replied Baba. So Baba started singing and Bihari took up the *mrdanga.* And Lo! He was playing on it like an expert.

The *siddha* saints sometimes perform miracles even without knowing or willing. In the state of *bhava* or ecstasy brought about by stimulation of the sentiment of divine love, miracles happen automatically, because in that state the saint is in tune with the Lord; the infinite source of inconceivable power or energy *(acintya sakti)* that transcends the laws of nature or science. This happened in the case of Baba on numerous occasions. Once a kirtana-party passed by the side of his cottage in Navadvipa. On hearing the *kirtana*, Baba asked Bihari to take him near the party. Bihari at once lifted him on his shoulders and took him there. Baba laid himself prostrate before the party in obeisance. Soon after the kirtana-song filled him with such ecstasy that he stood up and began to sing and dance with the party. He was no more the bent up old man, who had to be carried by Bihari on his shoulders. He was walking along with the party and singing and dancing with them. As he sang and danced he leapt two or three feet above the ground in ecstasy; and sometimes fell unconscious on the road. Since the road was under repairs and scattered with small pieces of stone all over, the fall bruised his body, but he was absolutely unaware of it. When he thus fell unconscious, the devotees sang *kirtana* close to his ears. Then he regained consciousness and again started walking, singing and dancing, as if nothing had happened. Bhava is contagious. Baba s *bhava* touched the hearts of the members of the party like electric current and they were also lost in ecstasy. Their movement, therefore, became slow. They took six hours to cover the distance from Baba s cottage to the *banyan* tree in Ranighata, where they stopped. Baba was all the time walking with them and dancing and jumping in a state of trance!

Baba had practised a long course of *sadhana* to attain this state. He always had stoic apathy to the world and was completely free from attachment. He would not even touch money. Once Bihari was carrying him somewhere on his shoulders. A rich man offered him a *rupee.* Baba asked Bihari to put it in his pocket. After Bihari had gone about a mile, Baba asked him to turn back and go again to the house of the man, who had offered the *rupee.* On reaching there, he called the man and said, "please take back your rupee. I am told you have thousands of rupees. I could not bear the bite of a single rupee. I wonder how you bear the bite of so many rupees." The rupee was with Bihari, but it was biting Baba, because he had touched it mentally by asking him to keep it.

Baba lived like an ascetic. His diet was strictly regulated. During the four months of the rainy season, he took in the first month only four bananas in the evening, in the second month guavas, in the third month whey, and in the fourth month boiled banana-flowers without salt.

Baba once went to Hrsikesa for *mantra-purascarana*. Bihari went with him. During *purascarana* he got up at 3 a.m., took his bath, closed the door of his room and sat down for *japa* till the evening. Throughout this period he observed silence and did not eat or drink anything. If he had to go for urination or even if he passed wind he stopped *japa* and bathed before resuming it. In the evening he took *havisyanna* (boiled rice with *ghee).* After two months one day he saw a tree laden with too many fruits. He was excited and said to Bihari, "Bihari look, how many fruits on this tree!" This broke his silence and he had to re-start *purascarana* from the beginning. After three months he had the *darsana* of Gaura-Nitai. This is how he became *siddha.* He used to say that if one wants to see Gauranga Mahaprabhu or Śrī Krsna in this life, he should do *purascarana.* Siddha saints are self-willed and fearless.

They live in a world of their own and do what they like, caring little for what others will say.

Once, when Baba was in Vrndavana he asked a sweeper to give him a *roti* to eat.

The sweeper said, "Why cut a joke with me Baba?"

"No, not a joke. I am very hungry."

"But I am a sweeper. How can you eat bread from my hand?"

"What of that? You are a sweeper of Vrndavana. A sweeper of Vrndavana is superior even to Brahmins."

The sweeper could not disobey a saint like Baba. Reluctantly he gave a *roti* to him and he ate it.

Bihari was watching this with dismay. He said, "What have you done Baba? Society will treat you as an outcast. It will be difficult for you to stay in Vrndavana." Baba only smiled.

News spread like wild fire throughout Vrndavana that Baba had eaten a sweeper s *roti.* Soon came NiIamani Gosvami, Radhika Natha Gosvami, Gaur Siromani, Gaur Sundar Raya and other dignitaries of Vrndavana in a delegation. Baba asked Bihari to give them an *asana* to sit. They seated themselves, but kept looking at each other for some time, for no one could muster courage to say anything.

Baba said, "I know why you have come. Why not speak out your mind?"

Then they said in a submissive tone, "Baba! you are the crest-jewel of the entire Vraja. If anyone says anything against you, we feel very much hurt. Now everybody is talking against you. Some say, "Baba has gone mad." Others say, "if Baba defies the age old traditions, what will happen to society?"

Baba replied, "You are all learned people. Do you not know the importance of the *raja* (dust) of Vrndavana? It is so charged with Krsna-prema that even Brahma longs to be a particle of it. Is not the sweeper of Vrndavana, who serves the *raja* and rolls and bathes in it incessantly, therefore, purer than anyone else?"

No one could say anything further.

Baba was self-willed to such an extent that at times he treated even the deities he worshipped as he liked. Once he was living in Suryakunda in Vraja. The people of Suryakunda had made a cottage for him. But Baba wanted to serve Gaura-Nitai, so they also made a temple for the deities. Bihari brought two beautiful images of Gaura-Nitai from Bengal. They were duly installed in the temple. The images were made of brass and glittered like gold. One night some robbers came while Baba was doing *bhajana.* They said, "Baba, we are robbers; you must give us all that you possess." Baba said, "What do I possess? I am only a servant. The masters are in the temple. You go to them." The robbers entered the temple. They were overjoyed to see the images. They thought the images were made of gold. So they wrapped them up in a blanket and wanted to run away. But the chief of the robbers, who was carrying the images, struck against the top frame of the door and fell down along with the images. Thinking that to be an ill omen the robbers left the deities and ran away. Next morning Baba asked Bihari to give away the deities to someone in Vrndavana. Bihari took the deities to Vrndavana and gave them to one Ma Gosvamini of Gayesapur. At present the deities are installed in a temple in Gopala Bagha in Vrndavana. They are known as *sonara* (golden) Gauranga and Nitai.

After sometime Baba again said to Bihari, "Bihari, I do not feel happy without a deity. Get me an image of Mahaprabhu from somewhere." Bihari brought an image of six-armed Mahaprabhu from Dinu Baba ji, a Manipuri Vaisnava, who lived in village Mukhara near Radhakunda. The image was duly installed and worshipped by Baba for ten years. After ten years he said to Bihari, "Go and give the deity to someone in Vrndavana. Bihari gave the deity to Baba Nityananda Dasa Ji of Gopalaguru Matha in Vrndavana. At present that deity is being worshipped in a temple in the lane adjacent to the boundry-wall of Nidhuvana in Vrndavana.

It is difficult to say why Baba twice brought the deities and sent them away. Did he not commit an *aparadha* (offence) by doing so? It is not proper to judge the behaviour of a *siddha* saint by ordinary canons of behaviour. For they are above all rules and regulations laid down in the scriptures for ordinary devotees. The loving intimacy, that develops between him and the deity he worships does not countenance any rules and regulations. In the game of love that goes on between the two, both are free to behave as they choose. Both play hide and seek with each other. Perhaps the two episodes narrated above are examples of hide and seek that was going on between the deities and Baba

Or perhaps the deities had done something to incur the displeasure of Baba. Examples are not lacking of the devotee s chastisement of the deity for his misdeed. Yasoda s chastisement of the child Krsna is very well known. The stories of chastisement of the deity by other devotees are also not uncommon. For instance, Bansidasa Baba of Navadvipa took his deities Gaura-Nitai to task for what he considered to be their connivance in the theft of the two pots, in which he used to cook for them separately. He said to them in anger, "You connived with the thieves, because the theft was committed in your presence. How could the thieves steal without your connivance? You must, therefore, suffer for your misdeed. You will not get anything to eat until the pots are brought back." So both Gaura and Nitai had to go without food, that day. Baba also had to go without food, for he ate only the *prasada* of Gaura-Nitai. Next day Nitai felt very much disturbed, perhaps not so much because he had to remain hungry; but because he could not see Baba remaining hungry on account of him. Perhaps Nitai s disturbance brought about a similar disturbance in the mind of the thief, in whose share had fallen the pot, in which Nitai s food used to be cooked. He quietly came with the pot and kept it in Baba s cottage. Bansi Dasa said to Gaura, "This is Nitai s pot. So he will get his food today, but not you. If you want to eat, you must also bring your pot back." Baba would always do what he said. So he cooked for Nitai and gave him to eat. While Nitai was eating Gaura was simply watching with a long face. How long could Gaura tolerate this? Ultimately by his wishing or prompting the second thief also brought back the second pot. Bansi Dasa Baba then cooked for Gaura.

It may be that Jagannatha Dasa Baba had ousted Gaura-Nitai from his temple, because he thought they had invited the robbers and created disturbance in his *bhajana.* But what offence had the six-armed Mahaprabhu committed to deserve his ousting? Baba alone knows. The ways of the saints, who have realised the Lord are no less inscrutable than the ways of the Lord Himself.

Once Baba said to Bihari, "Bihari, let us go to Navadvipa."

"When, Baba?"

"When? Just now."

Bihari lifted Baba on his shoulder and started for Navadvipa. As he lifted Baba, he felt that he was lifting a heavy stone. But soon after he began to feel as if he was carrying a thin piece of cloth on his shoulders. Walking all the distance on foot, Bihari reached Navadvipa within nine days. Who can say whether this was due to the miraculous power of Baba or the devotion of Bihari Dasa to the Guru?

When Bihari was passing through the forest of Bhagalapur, he suddenly stopped and began to turn back. Baba asked, "What s the matter Bihari?" Bihari replied in low voice, there is a lion sitting in front." Baba spoke commandingly, "No. No, not a lion, a *parsada* (companion) of Mahaprabhu, who has come to greet you." As he said this the lion looked at Baba and disappeared in the forest.

The last time Baba went to Navadvipa, he decided never to come back to Vrndavana. The news spread all over Vraja that Baba was leaving Vrndavana for good, Nilamani Prabhu, Radhikanatha Prabhu and other Vaisnavas, *pandits* and Vrajabasis started coming to persuade him not to go. They asked him, "Baba, why are you leaving Vrndavana in your old age?"

The question brought about a sudden change in Baba. His eyes were filled with tears, his body trembled, and the hairs of the body stood on ends. He replied in a voice choked with emotion, "I am leaving, because I want to pass the last days of my life in Navadvipa at the lotus-feet of Gaura-Nitai. I am a lowly person.

I do not know how many offences I have committed. Gaur-Nitai of Navadvipa are more merciful than Krsna of Vrndavana. They condone the offences of the *jiva,* give him a loving embrace and accept him as their *parsada* (companion or servant).

On way to Navadvipa Baba stopped at Ambika Kalna to meet his old friend and god-brother, the great saint Bhagavan Das Baba Ji of Kalna. The scene of their meeting was unique. Never had the persons, who were present at the meeting, seen such a scene before. On seeing each other, both the saints were so filled with joy that they clasped each other, and interlocked in each other s loving embrace began to roll on the ground in a fit of emotion. This continued for hours. Both seemed to be in *samadhi.* It appeared that the *samadhi* would never break. There was nothing unnatural about it, because it was not a meeting between ordinary persons; but a meeting of hearts between two devotees who had realized Krsna, and whose hearts were overflowing with Krsna-prema. Prema is so powerful that it makes not only the devotees, but even Krsna rock and roll and dance in ecstasy *(C.C. 3.18.17).*

The disciples of the two saints, however, became very anxious. Visnudasa Baba, the foremost disciple of Bhagavana Dasa said to Bihari, "It is now 11 P.M. We must do something to bring them back to consciousness. Jagannatha Dasa Baba must be very tired and hungry after his long journey. He must have something to eat."

Bihari said, "Yes, Baba has not had even a morsel of food for the last three days." As he said this, he lifted Jagannatha Dasa Baba in his lap and began to massage his chest. After sometime Baba regained consciousness. Looking at Bihari he said,

"Bihari, have you taken food?"

"How can I Baba, until you have eaten? You have not yet taken anything and it is now 11 o clock at night."

"Night! No, no, it is evening."

Everybody laughed. Baba stayed with Bhagavana Dasa Baba for ten days and then went to Navadvipa.

On reaching Navadvipa Bihari asked, "Baba, in which *asrama* would you stay?"

Any *asrama* in Navadvipa would have gladly welcomed Baba. But Baba said, "I will not go to any *asrama.* I will stay under a tree." So he stayed under a tree. After sometime Bihari, with the help of Śrī Kedaranatha Dutta, known as Bhaktivinoda Thakura, purchased a piece of land near the tree and built two thatched cottages on it. Later three *pucca* cottages were built and a boundry wall erected round them by Śrī Banamali Raya Bahadura of Tarasa. Baba lived here for 32 years.

He left the body at the age of 147.

There was a *keli-kadamba* tree in Baba s *asrama*, under which he used to sit for meditation and chant Hare Krsna. After Baba s disappearance, the tree began to dry up and it s bark fell off On it s naked body appeared the words "Hare Krsna" which, though not very distinct, could easily be read.

Four or five days before Baba left the body he said to Bihari, "Bihari, you have rendered so much service to me, but I have not yet been able to do anything for you. Today I will give you four or five cart-loads of wealth."

Bihar! said, "Well said Baba. You do not have anything except a broken *karava* and you will give me cart-loads of wealth!"

Oh Bihari, you do not understand. I will ask Mahaprabhu and he will easily arrange. But you let me know whether you want me or wealth."

"I want you, not wealth, Bihari replied quickly.

Baba was happy to hear this. He said, "Very good, Bihari. By opting for me you will have me, but not wealth. At the same time you will not have to suffer for want of anything. You will live for a hundred years. Always chant Harinama. Kali will not be able to do any harm to you. You will be blessed with the vision of Gaura Nitai."

Nothing is known about the early life of Jagannatha Dasa Baba, except that he had taken *diksa* from Śrī Jagadananda Gosvami of Srngaravata of Vrndavana and Vaisnava Sannyasa from *siddha* Śrī Krsnadasa Baba of Govardhana.

Baba had a large number of disciples, some of whom became *siddha.* Among his *siddha* disciples the names of the following may be mentioned:

1\. Śrī Bihari Dasa Baba Ji,

2\. Śrī Bhagavan Dasa Baba Ji,

3\. Śrī Gaurahari Dasa Baba Ji of Navadvipa,

4\. Śrī Ramahari Dasa Baba Ji of Vrndavana,

5\. Śrī Nityananda Dasa Baba Ji of Barasana, and

6\. Śrī Krsna Dasa Baba Ji of Kadambakhandi.

Baba used to give valuable advice to the *sadhakas.* For instance he said:

1\. You must avoid the company of women, or the company of a man who keeps the company of women, or even the company of a man who is in any way associated with the person keeping the company of women.

2\. If you want to realize the Supreme End, you must repeat Harinama regularly and steadfastly. Regularity is important. It should be maintained even at the cost of one s life.

3\. You should not forget Gaura, for he is even more benevolent and merciful than Krsna. Krsna is like a just ruler, who takes into account your offences in his administration of justice. Gaura does not take into account your offences. While Krsna is interested more in dispensation of justice, Gaura is interested more in dispensation of mercy. From this point of view, Gaura-kirtana also is more useful than Krsna-kirtana.

Gaura-kirtana is, for example, Śrī Krsna Caitanya Prabhu Nityananda, Śrī Advaita Gadadhara Srivasadi Gaura Bhaktavrnda.

4\. Krsna is the *avatara* of Dvapara, Gaura is the *avatara* of Kali. We should sing the name and the hymns of the *avatara* in whose age we live, just as we sing the praises of the king in whose kingdom we live.

## Chapter 10 - Śrī Gaurakisora Siromani

Śrī Gaurakisora Siromani s mother-in-law was a chaste lady. When her husband died she expressed her desire to become *sati.* Her relations tried to dissuade her by telling her about the pain and torture a *sati* has to suffer. Then she dipped her finger in *ghee* (clarified butter) and put it on the flame of a lamp. The finger began to burn like firewood. Showing the finger to her relations she said, See, how it burns, but it does not give me any pain." Then after circumambulating seven times the burning pile on which her husband s corpse was laid, she jumped into it. Even today the place near Guskara station in Vardhamana district, where she became *sati* is known as *sati danga.* Before jumping into the burning pile she said to her people, When my daughter Ramadasi comes of age you marry her to Gauracandra Siromani, the son of Dhanakrsna Cattopadhyaya of village Citahati in *tahsila* Katoya.

At that time Ramadasi was one and a half years old and Gauracandra was a young boy, who went about seeing theatrical performance and had little to do with reading or writing. Obviously, Ramadasi s mother had by her supernatural power seen in Gauracandra a *siddha* saint in the making. Ramadasi was married to him when she came of age. Up to the age of twenty, Gauracandra had, not even learnt alphabets. One day one of his neighbours said, "Gaura, it seems you will remain illiterate and a disgrace to your family." From that very day he became restless for learning. After learning the rudiments of the Bengali language from some *pandita* he went to Katoya and joined the *tola* (a *sanskrta* school) of Pancanana Tarkaratna. Later he learnt all the *bhakti-sastras* from Sakhicarana Dasa Pandita Baba of Katoya, and became famous as a *pandita*, well versed in the *sastras.* He began to live in the *natya-mahdira* of Mahaprabhu s temple in Katoya and started teaching.

He also took *diksa mantra* from *siddha* Śrī Caitanya Dasa Baba of Navadvipa and started *bhajana.* From early morning till 2 o clock in the afternoon he spent his time in *kirtana*, *nama-japa* and *IiIa-cintana* (meditation on lil*a).* After that he cleaned the path to Ganga and the place which was used by the *vaisnavas* as urinal. Then he bathed in Ganga, went to Mahaprabhu s temple for *darsana*, made obeisance to the *gosvamis* and *vaisnavas* assembled in the temple and took a particle of their *prasada* thrown in a pit outside the temple. As for his meal he boiled four handfuls of rice, added a little salt to it and ate after offering it to Mahaprabhu. In the evening he taught Srimad Bhagavatam and other *sastras* to students. Once commenting on the *janmadasya sloka* of Srimad Bhagavatam in an assembly of the great *vaisnava pandits* of Katoya, Siromani Mahasaya spoke for 35 days and his explanation was throughout in support of the concept of Śrī Gauranga and his philosophy. The *pandits* with one voice acclaimed him king of the *pandits* of Katoya and the surrounding area.

The *tantrika pandits*of Kagrama of Mursidabada district got jealous of him. They invited him to Kagrama for *Bhagavata patha* with a view to humiliate him. The *patha* was arranged near the Visnu-temple of Kagrama. On the third day of the *patha*, the *pandits* arranged for *pasu-bali* (animal sacrifice) for Kali-puja instead of *patha* at the same place, where *patha* was held. After Kali-puja, when they began the sacrifice, every time they had to strike the animal with the sword twice. This was an evil portent. So they tried to propitiate the goddess. At night the goddess told the *pujari* in a dream, "You have insulted a *visnu-bhakta.* I shall kill you!"

The *tantrika pandits* were very much frightened. They went to Siromani Mahasaya, apologized for their misdeed and requested him to do something to save them. Siromani Mahasaya asked them to go and bathe in Ganga and chant Harinama. The *pandits* obeyed. They bathed in Ganga and pleased the goddess by taking initiation in Harinama from Siromani Mahasaya.

After some time Siromani Mahasaya went to Vrndavana and began to live in a house near Kesighata, which even today is known as Siromani Kunja. In Vrndavana he used to teach Bhagavata to the *vaisnavas* in the afternoon. An old *vaisnava*, whom nobody knew, came to attend the Bhagavata *patha* everyday just when the *patha* began and went away immediately after the *patha*, without giving an opportunity to anyone to inquire who he was and where he came from. But he listened to the *patha* with utmost attention. One day, when Siromani Mahasaya was describing the *daksa-yajna* mentioned in the Fourth Canto of Srimad-Bhagavatam, one of the *vaisnavas* asked, "How could Sati burn her body by yogic power?" Before Siromani Mahasaya could say anything in reply, the old *vaisnava*, who never spoke a word, said, "Do you want to hear or to see how she did it!"

The other *vaisnavas* said, "Who would like to hear if he can see?"

The old *vaisnava* then said, "All-right, *see."* He sat cross-legged facing the north, and said, "Jai Gaur!" and began to meditate. After a little while fire blazed out of his great toes. Slowly it began to spread all over his body. "Oh! What a tragedy!" The *vaisnavas* tried to extinguish the fire by pouring water, but they could not stop the flames and the body of the old *vaisnava* was soon reduced to ashes.

We have said before that in Vraja many *siddha mahatmas*, who left their physical body hundreds of years before, still live there in their spiritual body and help remove the doubts and difficulties of the *sadhakas.* The old *vaisnava* might have been one of them.

Siromani Mahasaya bowed down to everyone, considering him to be superior to him in devotion. It was difficult to walk along with him on the road, because he would bow down to every pilgrim, who came to Vrndavana. When anyone asked him why he bowed to the pilgrims, he replied, "You don t know. Your devotion to Vrndavana has become still because of your nearness to it. There is little enthusiasm or zest in it. But these people live in far off places. Some come from East Bengal, some from Gujarata, Maharastra, Madras or Multauna, inspite of all the trouble and tribulation they have to suffer on the way. Their yearning and enthusiasm for Vrndavana are much more adorable. So my head naturally bows down to them."

Siromani Mahasaya, as far as possible, never allowed anyone to do *dandavat* to himself.

Anyone who wanted to do *dandavat* to him found him lying prostrate at his feet even before he made obeisance to him. Once Sridhara, a disciple of Vijayakrsna Gosvami, went to him for *darsana.* At that time he was asleep. Sridhara performed *dandavat* at his feet. As soon as he got up after performing *dandavat* he saw that Siromani Mahasaya s feet had automatically turned in another direction. He performed turned in another direction. He performed *dandavat a* second time. This time also he saw that his feet had turned in another direction. In this way he performed *dandavat* three times, every time from a different direction according to the direction of Siromani Mahasaya s feet and every time he found that sleeping Siromani Mahasaya s feet had automatically turned to avoid his *dandavat.*

After living in Vrndavana for some time Siromani Mahasaya fell seriously ill. Thinking that his life would soon end he took *vesa (vaisnava samnyasa*) from *siddha* Śrī Nityananda Dasa Baba Ji of Madanamohana Thaura. After *vesa* he was given the name Śrī Gaurakisora Siromani. By the grace of *guru* he slowly recovered from illness. Siddha Śrī Balarama Dasa Baba Ji then advised him to go and live in the *asrama* of Śrī Nityananda Dasa Baba Ji. He started living there. Even before his *vesa* Gaurakisora Dasa Baba ji was known as a *siddha mahatma.* After his *vesa* Nityananda Dasa Baba Ji became more famous, and people began to come to him in larger numbers for *darsana* and advice regarding *bhajana.* Seeing that this created disturbance in his *bhajana* Gaurakisora Siromani himself sat at the gate of Nityananda Dasa Baba Ji s *kuti* and removed the doubts and difficulties of the people, who came to see Nityananda Dasa Baba, to their satisfaction. If anyone insisted on having the *darsana* of *Nityananda Dasa* Baba, *he* asked *him to come in* the evening when Baba came out to listen to the turned in another direction. He performed *dandavat* a second time. This time also he saw that his feet had turned in another direction. In this way he performed *dandavat* three times, every time from a different direction according to the direction of Siromani Mahasaya s feet and every time he found that sleeping Siromani Mahasaya s feet had automatically turned to avoid his *dandavat.*

After living in Vrndavana for some time Siromani Mahasaya fell seriously ill. Thinking that his life would soon end he took *vesa (vaisnava samnyasa)* from *siddha* Śrī Nityananda Dasa Baba Ji of Madanamohana Thaura. After *vesa* he was given the name Śrī Gaurakisora Siromani. By the grace of *guru* he slowly recovered from illness. Siddha Śrī Balarama Dasa Baba Ji then advised him to go and live in the *asrama* of Śrī Nityananda Dasa Baba Ji. He started living there. Even before his *vesa* Gaurakisora Dasa Baba ji was known as a *siddha mahatma.* After his *vesa* Nityananda Dasa Baba Ji became more famous, and people began to come to him in larger numbers for *darsana* and advice regarding *bhajana.* Seeing that this created disturbance in his *bhajana* Gaurakisora Siromani himself sat at the gate of Nityananda Dasa Baba Ji s *kuti* and removed the doubts and difficulties of the people, who came to see Nityananda Dasa Baba, to their satisfaction. If anyone insisted on having the *darsana* of Nityananda Dasa Baba, he asked him to come in the evening when Baba came out to listen to the *daily* patha.

On account of the service he thus rendered to Śrī Gurudeva, however, his own *bhajana* began to be disturbed. But this did not dissuade him from the service of Gurudeva, because he considered service to Gurudeva of much greater importance than his own *bhajana.* Besides, in course of time, he adjusted himself to the new situation and could carry on his *bhajana* undisturbed even while engaged in the service of Gurudeva. He used to tell the *tyagis* (those who had renounced the world) that in their *bhajana* they should be like the pigeon of Naubatakhana.

Sometime after Gaurakisora Siromani had taken *vesa* from Nityananda Dasa Baba Ji, Pandita Ramakrsna Dasa Baba Ji also took *vesa* from him. Nityananda Dasa Baba Ji told Ramakrsna Dasa Baba Ji, "Although Gaurakisora is your god-brother, you should regard him as your *guru."* This speaks of the high esteem in which Gaurakisora Siromani was held by *siddha* Nityananda Dasa Baba Ji. All other *vaisnavas* also held him in high esteem.

At that time there lived in Vrndavana, besides Nityananda Dasa Baba and Pandita Ramakrsna Dasa Baba, a number of other *siddha mahatmas* and *vaisnavas* like Siddha Balarama Dasa Baba, Śrī Brahmananda Gosvamipada of Srngaravata, Gosvami, Śrī Nilamani Gosvamipada, Śrī Haracandra Gosvamipada, Śrī Gangaprasada Raya of Tarasa and Śrī Hajara Mahasaya. Gaurakisora Siromani was highly respected by all of them.

These *siddha mahatmas* kept an eye upon all *sadhakas.* If they found anyone acting against the principles of behaviour laid down in the *sastras* for *sadhus* and *vaisnavas* they castigated him.

Once they had to castigate even Gaurakisora Siromani Baba for one of his lapses. The Rani of Hetamapura came to Vrndavana. She was very much pleased to hear the Bhagavata-patha of Gaurakisora Baba Ji. After the *patha* she sent some money, fruits and flowers etc. to him in the form of *daksina.* He accepted them, because he did not want to hurt the Rani by refusing them. The *vaisnavas* reprimanded him for this. He realized the gravity of his offenceand asked for punishment. The *vaisnavas* ordered that he should do Bhagavata-patha in each of the temples of Vrndavana within a year. The order was difficult, because the number of temples in Vrndavana was large. Yet Gaurakisora Baba carried out the order by doing several *paths* a day.

Siromani Mahasaya. always swam freely in the ocean of *bhava* and the slightest stimulous made him dive deep into it. Once he saw a dancing girl returning from somewhere after a performance. The girl reminded him of a Vraja-gopi and he was so overwhelmed with *bhava* that he fell unconscious on the ground.

One night Siromani Mahasaya heard his *guru* Śrī Caitanya Dasa Baba Ji saying to him in a dream, Siromani, I am coming to Vrndavana!" He understood that Gurudeva had left the body to participate in Krsna-IiIa in celestial Vrndavana. He was shocked. Soon after, in the year 1890, he also followed his Gurudeva.

## Chapter 11 - Śrī Gauracarana Dasa Baba Ji

Siddha Śrī Gauracarana Dasa Baba Ji was a descendant of Śrī Lokanatha Gosvami, an associate of Sriman Mahaprabhu. He was born in Talkahari village in the district of Jessore (Bangladesh). At a very early age he left home and went to Navadvipa. There he took initiation from *siddha* Śrī Caitanya Dasa Baba Ji. After remaining in the service of *gurudeva* for a long time, he requested permission to go to see holy Vraja and return. Gurudeva said, "Go, but will you be able to return? Having gone there you may find yourself in the love-trap of Krsna and Balarama. They may not let you come."

Gauracarana said, "No *gurudeva*, you may rest assured, i will return quickly after making the *84 kosa parikrama* (circumambulation) of Vraja."

Gauracarana went to Vrndavana. He performed the *parikrama* of Vraja. At the conclusion of the *parikrama* he went to Mahavana and had the *darsana* of Dauji (the image of Balarama). He resolved to pass the night there and at dawn to set out for Navadvipa. During the night, while he was asleep, Dauji called him by name and said, Look, you are very dear to me. So remain here and do *bhajana* in the cave you see over there. Here you will attain all that you want."

The devotee worships Bhagavan, because he loves Him. But when Bhagavan begins to love the devotee, what remains for him to achieve? What remains is the sweet game of hide and seek that goes on between the two. Gauracarana Baba had *sakhya bhava* or the *bhava* of friendly relationship towards Bhagavan. Friends often have love-quarrel between them. It was not unnatural, therefore, that Gauracarana should have quarreled with Dauji. So, protesting against what Dauji said, he replied, No I shall go to Navadvipa according to the instructions of my Guru. I will not stay here."

"I will not let you go," said Dauji, with a playful smile on his face.

Gauracarana found himself in a predicament. He did not know whether he should obey his *guru* or Dauji. After reflecting for a while, he said, "No, no! I shall go. I shall leave this very moment."

Even as he said this he sprang up, slung his bag over his shoulder and set out at midnight. Gauracarana was marching fast. As he was marching on, there rang in his ears sometimes the words of *gurudeva*, "Go, but will you be able to return!" and sometimes the words of Dauji, "I ll not let you go." The smiling face of Dauji seemed to draw him back forcibly. Still he was pushing forward with long strides. Every little while he turned his face back to see whether Dauji was coming right behind. He was determined to stop a while and take rest only after crossing the perimeter of Vraja. After going a long distance he thought, "By now perhaps, I have crossed the border of Vraja." But as the day dawned he was amazed to find where he was. He exclaimed, "Oh! Have I, like a fool, been only going round and round Baladeva Kundaall night long?! This illusion must have been manoeuvred by Baladeva."

The words of gurudeva came true. Gauracarana realized that it was not possible for him to escape the grip of Dauji. What could the helpless Baba Ji do? He began to dwell in the cave pointed out by Dauji and do his *sadhana* there.

He would get up very early in the morning, take his bath and start chanting "RamaKrsna" with the aid of a large rosary. He would go on chanting till the afternoon. Then he would come out of the cave, again take bath and sit down for reading Srimad Bhagavatam and other scriptures.

At dusk he would go begging for food. He would converse with no one. Nor would he go anywhere else. For twenty years he did *bhajana* in this manner in the cave. His absorption in chanting was so deep that even while asleep he could be heard chanting "Rama-Krsna!"

Gauracarana Baba had the conceit of being the younger brother of Sridama and the elder brother of Radharani. It is believed that Dauji used to go to his cave and feed him with butter and sugar candy.

After some time Gauracarana s reputation as a *siddha mahatma* spread far and wide. A devout person of the *saha* caste came from Bangladesh to get initiation from him. Everyday when Baba went out for begging, he saw him sitting outside his cave. But Baba said nothing to him. In this way one year passed. Then one day he asked, him, "Who are you? What do you want?" Saha Mahasaya introduced himself and said, "I desire your grace (i.e. initiation)."

"What is that under your arm?" asked Baba.

"This is *Caitanya-caritamrta,"* he replied.

Baba took him inside the cave and asked him to recite *Caitanya-caritamrta.* From that day for about a year Saha Mahasaya recited to him *Caitanya-caritamrta* everyday. During this period Baba gave him *diksa.* Later he gave him *vaisnava samnyasa* and the name Śrī Dayala Dasa. Dayala Dasa was the first disciple of Śrī Gauracarana Dasa Baba Ji. After him many others took initiation or *vesa* from him.

At the time of hearing the recitation of *Caitanya-caritamrta* Gauracarana Dasa Baba used to be overwhelmed with *bhava.* So profuse used to be the flow of tears from his eyes that his clothes got completely drenched. Listening daily to the recitation he became so engrossed in love for Gaura that instead of chanting "Rama-Krsna", he began to chant the names of Gaura and his associates: "Śrī Gauranga, Nityananda, Śrī Advaitacandra, Gadadhara, Srivasadi Gaura Bhaktavrnda."

How is it that Gauracarana Baba, who was intensely devoted to Rama and Krsna, became a devotee of Gaura? Was it because the *madhurya* (sweetness) of Bhagavan in the form of a *bhakta* charged with *Mahabhava,* the sentiment of Radha s love for Krsna, the highest love sentiment, is even more charming than the sweetness *(madhurya)* of Krsna, the highest embodiment of all rasas?

A year later Dayala Dasa Baba Ji expressed to *gurudeva* his desire to go to Navadvipa to have the *darsana* of his *parama-gurudeva*,Gauracarana Dasa found himself in a predicament. He did not want to be deprived of the company of a loving devotee like Dayala Dasa. But after reflecting for a while he said with a deep sigh, "All right! Go. I could not go back to serve *gurudeva.* You go and serve him."

Dayala Dasa went away. But Gauracarana could not bear his separation. He began to think that Śrī Gauranga Mahaprabhu Himself had come to him in the guise of Dayala Dasa to dispense Gaura-bhakti to him. He went about from grove to grove shouting "Dayal! Dayal!" like a madman.

Did Gauracarana Baba, in consequence of his love for Gaura, forget all about Rama and Krsna? The question does not arise. For Gaura is not separate from Krsna. He is only a different form or manifestation of Krsna. He is Gaura-Krsna or fair-complexioned Krsna, having the form and conceit of a devotee: He appears in this form in order to relish the *madhurya* of his own Krsna form and also to relish Krsna-prema or the bliss of loving devotion to Krsna.

There was no change in Gauracarana s *sakhya-bhava* towards Rama and Krsna. Under the maddening spell of this sentiment one day he went to the temple of Govindaji in Vrndavana. Perchance he was reminded of Krsna s own saying in Srimad-Bhagavatam: "I follow close behind my devotee so that the dust of his feet may settle on my body and I may be purified." He went before the image of Govindaji and said, "Take the dust of my feet. The priest of Govindaji thought he was mad. They pushed him out of the temple. While going out of the temple he turned his face towards Govindaji and said, "All right, you push me out. But remember my name is Gauracarana. When you come out to play in the evening I shall make you take the dust of my feet. Unless you do so I will not play with you!"

Gauracarana Baba s behaviour towards Govinda may arouse our indignation. But, surprisingly, Govinda enjoyed it. He always enjoys the devotee s outburst of anger in love more than the praises or glorification of a devotee conscious of his *aisvarya* (lordliness). This is an extreme example of *raganuga-bhakti*, or spontaneous outflow of devotion, free from the stress and strains of the moral imperative or the injunctions of the scriptures, which is prevalent in Vraja. But it highlights the importance of the total absence of *aisvarya bhava* in *raganuga* bhakti.

After some time Śrī Gauracarana Dasa Baba went to Kunjara village in Vraja and began to reside there. He lived very long. In his last years he resided in Vrndavana with his disciple Śrī Krsna-Caitanya Dasa (Raya Saheb Śrī Kailasa Dasa) in the Manipuri Kunja, where he left his physical body to join Śrī Krsna in celestial Vrndavana and participate in his *lila.*

## Chapter 12 - Śrī Lala Babu

It was evening. The sun was about to set. Lala Babu was out in a palanquin for a pleasure drive in Calcutta. In front of the palanquin were some gunmen and a number of sepoys and servants trailed behind. The palanquin was brought down and kept in the midst of a beautiful grove surrounded by trees and creepers, laden with fruits and flowers of different hues.

A hookawith a long pipe having a silver mouth-piece was soon placed before Lala Babu. Resting on one side on a large pillow with a velvet cover he held the pipe close to his mouth and started smoking. As he was smoking a cool evening breeze was blowing, the birds returning to their nests were twittering, the waves of Ganga seemed to be dancing to their tune and the crimson rays of the setting sun added their own charm to the scene. Lala Babu s heart was swinging with joy to see what appeared to him to be nature s entertainment for him. He felt that there was no one in the world happier than him.

Suddenly a spark disturbed him. The spark came from a cottage nearby. It was the cottage of a fisherman. The fisherman was asleep. His daughter was trying to awaken him by saying, "Baba, awake! The day is about to close. The sun is setting."

The words of the girl kept ringing into Lala Baba s ears for sometime. He felt that he had heard a cal! from Vrndavana, a call from Radharani, who was saying. "Baba, how long will you continue to sleep? Life s term will soon be over. So wake up and do what you can while there is yet time to conquer death.

The call came like an electric shock. It shook the entire frame of Lala Babu s body. The pipe fell from his mouth. The hubble bubble of the *hookah* stopped. There was a sudden change in him. He was no more the Lala Babu, who considered himself to. be the happiest man of the world. The dream-world of happiness in which he had so far lived had no attraction for him now. He had realized that death was more real than power and pelf, the majesty and grandeur he had been enjoying. He knew that when death came everything would melt into nothingness like every puff of smoke he had emitted while smoking and watched curling and curling till it melted into the sky. The call had awakened him from his slumber. He had decided not to sleep anymore. He had determined to renounce everything and to go to Vrndavana to devote the rest of his life to the service of Radha-Krsna.

With this determination he went home. He passed the whole night weeping and praying to Radharani so that She might give him the courage to act immediately in response to Her call. The next morning he called his wife and children and said, "I have heard Radharani s call and I am going to Vrndavana." His wife and children started weeping and wailing. Other relatives and friends used all their wit and strength to move him from his determination. But he ignored their persuasion and the weeping and wailing of his wife and children and started for Vrndavana.

Lala Babu s real name was Krsna Candra Sinha. He was born in 1775 with a silver spoon in his mouth. His grandfather Ganga Govinda Sinha was the governor of Bengal and Bihar, when Warren Hastings was the governor general of India. He had by his extraordinary ability made a huge fortune and had become the owner of a large estate. His brother Radha-Govinda was also fabulously rich.

Krsna Candra Sinha s father Pranakrsna was the only heir to the property of the two brothers. He, therefore, became the richest and the most respectable man of Eastern India of his time. Krsna Candra was his only son. Ganga Govind Sinha used to call him Lala" out of affection. The servants in the family and the other people called him "Lala Babu" out of respect. Therefore he became known as Lala Babu all over India.

Lala Babu s intelligence was extraordinary. His father appointed the best teachers to teach him English, Sanskrit and Persian. Within a short time he acquired mastery over these languages. He loved to read Bhagavata. His study of Bhagavata was so thorough that he could explain the deeper meaning of any of it s *s/okas* with surprising acumen and skill.

He was also very kind-hearted and generous. His heart melted to see the suffering of the poor and the needy. Once a poor man, who could not marry his daughter for want of money, approached him for help. Immediately he asked the treasurer to give him a thousand rupees. The treasurer sought the permission of Lala Babu s father. He said, "Since Lala has already promised this sum to the man, you give it to him. But tell Lala that he should give money in charity in this manner only when he himself earns something or increases the earning of the estate."

Lala Babu felt this very much. He said to himself, "The grandson of multimillionaire Ganga Govinda Sinha does not have the authority to do even a small service to anyone! Very well then he will stand on his own legs before he does any such thing." Much against the wishes of his parents he went to Vardhamana and accepted the post of Saristedara (head of a department) in Vardhamana collectorate. On account of his extraordinary ability he began to rise rapidly from one position to another. At this time he also got married and had a son.

In 1803, Orissa also came under British rule. He was then appointed governor of Orissa. He got an opportunity to live in Puri, the place associated with the divine Lila of Mahaprabhu Śrī Krsna Caitanya. This gave a fillip to his innate tendency towards *bhakti.* After his official work he began to devote the rest of his time to the study of religious books, *nama-japa* and *kirtana.*

After some time he got the news of the death of his father. The news filled his heart with great pain and remorse, because after he had come out of home he never went back and his father died without fulfilling his cherished desire for seeing his son and grandson.

He relinquished the post of Governor and went back to Calcutta because the burden of managing the estate of his father had now fallen upon him. He began to look after the affairs of the estate. But he often asked himself the question: Does this estate together with all the wealth, of which I am now the master, serve the real purpose of life? If not, why should I not seek the path of Bhakti, which promises not only eternal peace and happiness, but also the eternal company and the loving service of the twin lords of my heart, Radha and Krsna." Radha and Krsna seemed to attract him to Vrndavana. But the thought of his duty towards his widowed mother, wife and children and perhaps a desire that still lurked somewhere in his mind, the desire of enjoying at least for the time being the opulence at his command, pulled him in the opposite direction. The conflict continued in his mind until he heard the call of Radha rani. With the call came the final decision, which took him to Vrndavana.

In Vrndavana he lived the life of a recluse. From morning till evening he spent the day in *bhajana.* In the evening he went out for *madhukari* and contented himself with eating whatever he got.

His heart wept to see the condition of some of the old temples and their deities, who were not being properly served. He wished that he could do something to improve their condition. But he could only wish, because he was now a pauper and a beggar. However, a thought came to his mind. He said to himself, "I am a beggar, but not my lord. My lord is the owner of the big fortune I have left in Calcutta. Out of that I can give to my wife and children their legitimate share and spend the rest in the service of the lord, while I continue to live only on what I get in alms."

He started working on this plan. He got twenty-five lacs of rupees from his Jamidariin Bengal and Bihar. With this money he started purchasing extensive land and property in Vraja. Altogether he purchased seventy-four *paragana*of land. He used the income from the land in repairing old temples and constructing new temples and rest-houses and making arrangements for proper service of the deities in the temples.

He also planned to build a beautiful temple of Radha-Krsna in Vrndavana with arrangements for providing food to hundreds of *sadhus* and the poor everyday.

The construction of the temple was started. Lala Babu had often to go to Rajasthan to purchase high class stone for the temple. On the way while going and coming, he had to stop at Bharatapur for a day or two to stay with the Maharaja of Bharatapur, who was his old friend.

On account of his intimacy with the Maharaja he once got into great trouble. At that time East India Company was having negotiations for a treaty with the Rajas of Rajasthan. The Maharaja of Bharatapur s role was important in the treaty. The Maharaja for some reason refused to sign the treaty. Some people told Sir Charles Metcalf, East India Company s resident in Delhi, that the Maharaja was willing to sign the treaty, but he changed his mind on account of the advice of Lala Babu. Metcalf, therefore, got Lala Babu arrested and brought to Delhi for legal proceedings.

The news of Lala Babu s arrest spread like wild fire in the whole of Vraja. Thousands of Vrajabasis rushed to Delhi to protest against his arrest. Metcalf was surprised and also somewhat worried to see that Lala Babu wielded so much influence in Vraja. He postponed the proceedings against him and sent his spies to Vraja to make necessary investigations and report about his past history and character. Their report opened the eyes of Metcalf. He set Lala Babu free and apologized to him. He also offered to give him the title of Maharaja, which he declined with thanks.

In due course Lala Babu s grand and beautiful temple was built in Vrndavana in the area known as Brahma-Kunda Beautiful images of Śrī Krsna and Radha were installed. Prana-pratisthaceremony was also performed. The images of Śrī Krsna was named Śrī Krsna Candrama. Arrangements were made in the guest house for feeding hundreds of people with *prasada* every day. But Lala Babu continued to beg food for himself as before from the Vrajabasis.

One day in the coldest month of January, as Lala Babu was looking at the handsome figure of Śrī Krsna Candrama, a strange thought came to his mind. He asked the *pujari* to keep a small lump of butter on the head of the deity. The *pujari* looked at Lala Babu with surprise. Lala Babu said, Yes, yes, do as I say. I want to see whether the deity is really alive after *prana-pratistha.* If alive, the butter should melt on account of the heat of the head."

The *pujari* had to obey. A small quantity of butter was kept on the head of Śrī Krsna Candrama After some time the butter actually melted and began to flow down the cheeks of the deity. The *pujari* and the other devotees present in the temple on the occasion cried aloud with tears of joy in their eyes, "Śrī Krsna Candrama Ki Jaya, Śrī Krsna Candrama Ki Jaya!!" Lala Babu was so overwhelmed with *bhava* that he fell unconscious on the ground.

Once again Lala Babu wanted to test the deity. He thought that if there was heat on the head of the deity, there must also be breath in his nose. Why not test that also? He gave a piece of cotton to the *pujari* and asked him to hold it close to the nose of the deity. Laughingly the *pujari* held the cotton close to the deity s nose. The cotton began to vibrate on account of exhalation. Lala Babu was again overwhelmed with *bhava.* Not being able to contain the *bhava* he began to roll on the ground. He was satisfied that *prana-pratistha* was successfully done.

No one should, however, think that *prana-pratistha* alone is sufficient to infuse life into the deify. What is of greater importance is the *bhava* of the devotee. The deity lives and moves and appears or disappears according to the *bhava* of the devotee. It was Lala Babu s *bhava* that enabled the deity to pass the two tests successfully. The deity always responds to the heart of the devotee.

One day Krsna Candrama told Lala Babu in a dream,

"I am pleased with your service. But I beg of you one more thing."

"Beg! Prabhu, you have only to order this humble servant. Kindly let me know what more I can do for you?"

"You do not know Lala Babu, that I am a beggar by birth. I go about asking for alms from my devotees. Though there is nothing that I do not possess and there is no place where I do not live, I prefer to eat what my devotees give and to live in the temples they build for me. You have made a temple for me in which I live with pleasure. But I want that you should build one more temple."

"One more temple? You know Prabhu that I am a pauper. All the money I got from Jamidari has already been spent. How can I build another temple?

"I know that you have renounced everything and there is not a shell left that you can call your own. But the kind of temple I want you to build is built only when a man has renounced everything. When a man renounces everything he builds a temple in his heart. I love to live in that temple more than even in Vaikuntha. You have made so many temples outside, make one within, so that I may live there eternally. I am standing at your door today and asking for this *bhiksa*."

"Then Prabhu, kindly let me know what I should do to make my heart a temple for you"

"You first go round Vraja. See all the different places associated with my lila*.* Then do *bhajana* in Govardhana. As you advance in *bhajana* you will yourself come to know what you have to do to make your heart a proper dwelling place for me."

Lala Babu went out on pilgrimage in Vraja-mandala. He saw all the holy places associated with Krsna-IiIa and then began to live in a cave in Govardhana and do *bhajana.* Every morning he went on *parikrama* of Giriraja. After *parikrama* he did *bhajana* in the cave throughout the day. Late in the evening he went out for madhukari.

One day while he was out on *parikrama* the *pujari* of the temple of Giriraja said, "Baba, do not go for *madhukari* today. I shall myself go to your cave this evening and give you the *prasadam* of Giriraja."

In the evening it started raining and continued to rain till night. The rain was so torrential that it was impossible for the *pujari* to go out. There was no end to his anxiety for Lala Babu, who, he thought, must be very hungry, because he took *prasada* only once after the day s *parikrama* and *bhajana.* When the rain slowed down he thought of going to Lala Babu with *prasada.* He had already kept a *thala* (plate) filled with *prasada* for him inside the temple. When he went inside the temple, he was surprised to find that the *thala* was missing. There was no time to think how the *thala* could have disappeared. Quickly he arranged for another *thala* of *prasada* and proceeded towards the cave.

As soon as he reached there Lala Babu said, "What is this *pujari?* What more have you brought? I have not been able to eat all that you brought before. See, it is still lying there."

The *pujari* was surprised to see the missing *thala* and *prasada* in Lala Babu s cave.

"Who brought it?", he shouted at once.

Lala Babu looked amazedly at the *pujari* and said,

"What'?! Do you mean to say that you did not bring it!"

"I neither brought it, nor sent it through anyone."

"No, No, you brought it. I reprimanded you for coming all the way in rain and getting badly drenched; but you only smiled and went away. I wonder how you have forgotten everything so soon. Have you taken *bhanga?*

The *pujari* knew that he had not taken *bhanga.* He kept looking at Lala Babu for some time with tears flowing profusely from his eyes and then suddenly stretching his hand to touch his feet he said, Baba! You are fortunate. You have so won over Giriraja by your love that he could not see you hungry and himself came to you in my form in the rain with *prasada."*

On hearing this Lala Babu s entire body was filled with *asta-sattvika bhavas* and he fell unconscious on the ground. On regaining consciousness he began to say with tears flowing from his eyes, and throat choked with emotion, " Prabhu! You took so much trouble for this undeserving servant of yours, who could not even recognize you. How can anyone recognize you, if you do not want to be recognized? But if you can be so kind to an undeserving servant why come in disguise? Is that not to intensify his suffering in separation?"

For many years Lala Babu had mentally accepted *siddha* Krsnadasa Baba Ji of Govardhana as his *guru.* One day he requested him for *diksa* (initiation). He replied, "The time for your *diksa* has not yet come. Some subtle *samskaras* of your previous worldly life are still vitiating your *bhajana.* You must burn them in the fire of *vairagya* (ascetic detachment from the world). When that is done, I shall myself come and give you *diksa,* you do not have to come to me.

Lala Babu continued his *bhajana* with renewed vigour. Although not yet initiated, he lived like a Baba Ji. He wore *kaupina* and *kantha* and lived on *madhukari* like the Baba Jis of Vraja. Quite some time passed like this. Still *diksa* seemed to be far off.

Lala Babu often used to go to Vrndavana to see whether the service and the distribution of *prasada* in the temple of Śrī Krsna Candrama was going on satisfactorily. This time when he went there he wept before the deity and said, Prabhu!

I know that I have not yet been able to build the temple you had asked me to build in my heart. My *guru* told me that I still have some *samskaras,* which make my heart impure. But unless I know what those *samskaras* are, how can I try to remove them? I am blind. Unless you help me I will never know them. I will always remain impure and the temple you want shall never be built. "

That night Lala Babu could not sleep. He passed the whole night weeping and praying. He had never wept so much before. The tears washed away his impurity. When he got up in the morning his vision was clear. He could clearly see the *samskaras* that had been vitiating his *bhajana.* He said to himself. "I have renounced the world and all its pomps and vanities, but have I renounced the vanity of vanities, the vanity of renunciation? Does not the subtle pride of renunciation still lurk in my mind? Do I not in my heart of heart regard myself as superior to the people, who have not renounced? Is my mind completely free from hatred or malice? No, it is not. At least there is one person to whom I still bear malice, and that is Seth Laksmicanda. If I did not bear malice to him I should have gone to him for *madhukari,* just as I have gone to other people living close to him. But I have always avoided him. Is that not a clear proof that I still bear malice against him?"

Seth Laksmicanda was the builder of the famous temple of Ranga Ji in Vrndavana. He was a rival of Lala Babu and his competitor in all such activities as the construction of temples, alms-giving and the service of *sadhus.* Lala Babu had also filed a suit against him in connection with a dispute regarding the ownership of some land in Vrndavana which he had won. But the old malice against him was still lurking in his mind. As soon as he became aware of it, he decided to amend it.

One day there was a big crowd of beggars in front of Ranga Ji s temple. In the crowd there also stood a handsome looking beggar, with the beggar s bag hanging from his shoulder. He was beating *karataias* and singing Harinama and tears were flowing from his eyes. He was easily recognized by the guard at the door. At once the guard went in and told Seth Ji, "Lala Babu is standing at your door for *bhiksa."*

Seth Ji was stunned. But in a few moments he collected himself and ordered for a *thala* with one hundred gold *mohours,* flour, rice, pulses and fruits. He himself went out with the *thala,* respectfully bowed down before Lala Babu and said, Babu Ji, I am extremely grateful to you for kindly coming and sanctifying this place by your holy presence. Kindly favour me by accepting this *thala"* Lala Babu said, "Seth Ji, I came to you for *bhiksa.* But what you are giving does not look like *bhiksa."*

"You are correct," retorted the Seth. "What I am giving is not *bhiksa.* You are Raja though outwardly a beggar. I am not capable of giving *bhiksa* to a Raja. I had the foolhardiness to enter into a conflict with you, in which I was defeated. This is my second defeat. In the first defeat you had captured what I supposed was my land. In this defeat you have captured my heart. You are now the Raja, the sovereign of my heart. What I am giving you, therefore, is not *bhiksa*, but *nazarana* in recognition of your sovereignty over my heart."

"No, No, Seth Ji, a pauper is always a pauper. He does not deserve this present. Kindly give me only a handful of rice from the *thala.* I ask for one more *bhiksa.* If knowingly or unknowingly I have ever committed any offence against you, kindly excuse me and bless me, so that my heart is purified and I become worthy of the mercy of Krsna." Lala Babu was in tears when he said this. Seth Ji also began to shed tears. Both were overwhelmed with emotion. Both clasped each other and drowned each other in each other s tears.

When Lala Babu was returning to his temple, on the way he saw Mahatma Krsna Dasa coming towards him, with a meaningful smile on his face. Soon after Lala Babu had done *dandavat,* he patted him on his back and said, "My son, the time for your *diksa* has come!"

On hearing this there was no end to Lala Babu s happiness. On an auspicious day he was initiated. After initiation the Guru said, "Now you go back to your cave and practice *sadhana.* Do not come out of the cave and do not see the face of any man until you have the *darsana* of Krsna."

Lala Babu began to do *sadhana* strictly according to the advice of his Guru. After some years he was blessed with the *darsana* of Śrī Krsna. His heart was also turned into a temple for Śrī Krsna and Krsna-LiIa always unfolded itself to him on the screen of his heart.
