Soon after Lala Babu became famous as a *siddha mahatma* and people from different places began to come to him for *darsana* and advice. Once Maharaja Scindia came to him. He laid himself prostrate at his feet and prayed for *diksa.* Lala Babu said, "Maharaja! *diksa* alone will not do. Krsna wants that you should stretch both of your hands towards Him. If you hold the world with one hand and try to touch Krsna s feet with the other, you will never be able to touch Him. Are you prepared to stretch both hands towards Krsna?"

Maharaja was not prepared to stretch both hands. He said, "Maharaja, what you say is correct. This path is not meant for worldly persons like me." He performed *dandavat* at the feet of Lala Babu and left.

Slowly Lala Babu s life-stream became leaner and leaner. One day he asked the people, who surrounded him to take him to the bank of Yamuna. There he left his body, chanting the name of Krsna and seeing Krsna-IiIa.

## Chapter 13 - Śrī Sanehirama Ji

Everybody in village Mata in Vraja remembers Sanehirama Ji with reverence and sings his songs of divine love with joy and devotion. Born in Mata in 1842 he was devotionally inclined from his childhood. His elder brother was a farmer. He often sent him to the field to work. But he went there and sat down to meditate. This annoyed the elder brother, who thought that he was hypocritical and lazy and wanted to avoid work by pretending to be a devotee.

Once the elder brother took Sanehirama with him to the field for sowing. He started ploughing the field and asked Sanehirama to sow the seeds. Sowing went on till it was time for lunch. Sanehirama saw his sister-in-law coming to the field with the lunch-box over her head. He was afraid that his elder brother would start eating without offering the food to the Lord and he would also have to take the unoffered food. So he began to meditate. In meditation he speedily arranged bread, *karhi* (porridge) and vegetables in plate, scattered *tulasi* leaves over it and began to offer it to Radha and Krsna with *mantras.* While he was doing this the work of sowing stood still. When the elder brother saw this he dealt a strong blow on his hand with a stick and said, Have you come here to work or to ape the great saint with your eyes closed like a hypocrite?"

Sanehirama said, "Brother! What have you done? I was offering food to the Lord and you struck me. Everything got scattered."

"You imposter!" shouted the elder brother and was going to strike him again, when his wife cried, "Oh! What is this?" and began to stare wide-eyed at the things contained in the lunch-box all scattered in the furrows of the field. His attention was drawn in that direction and he too was amazed to see the edibles lying scattered on the field. He realized that his brother was not an imposter, but truly a saint. His heart was ablaze with penitential fire. He clasped the feet of his brother and begged his pardon. From that day he freed him from all work at home and in the field.

Sanehirama Ji was a great devotee of Bihari Ji! Since Bihari Ji s temple is in Vrndavana and Mata is on the other side of Yamuna. One has to cross the river for going from Mata to Vrndavana. Still Sanehirama went to Vrndavana every evening to have the *darsana* of Bihari Ji. Neither rain nor storm would thwart him. He took his evening meal only after having the *darsana* of Bihari Ji.

One day he remained seated in meditation till late at night. When meditation was over it was 2 A.M., but he thought that night had just set in.

He started out for *darsana* of Bihari Ji. On reaching the bank of Yamuna, he saw in moon-light that all the boats were moored at the bank and no boat-man was there. He was filled with anxiety. He thought that perhaps he had committed some offence on account of which Bihari Ji did not want to give him *darsana* that day.

But soon he saw a boatman coming from the other side. As he came closer he recognized him. He was the same boat-man, who took him across the river and brought him back everyday. With a sigh of relief he stepped into his boat. When he reached the temple he found it open as usual. He had the *darsana* of Bihari Ji to his heart s content and went back to the bank. The boat-man, who was waiting for him, ferried him across the river.

Disembarking from the boat, Sanehirama was startled to see a number of villagers coming to bathe in the Yamuna. He asked the boatman, Is it morning?"

So it is, sir. It was already 2 o clock when I took you to Vrndavana."

"How is it that you were here so late at night?"

"Oh sir! Don t ask about that. Yesterday I had a quarrel with my wife. So I did not go home. I lay down on the boat, but could not sleep."

But how is it that the *darsana* of Bihari Ji was available even so late at night?" Sanehirama began to reflect. "Was it all a dream?"

The next day when he went for *darsana* at the usual time, the boatman who took him across everyday was by the landing. He asked, "Sir, did you not go for *darsana* yesterday?"

"Of course I went. Have you forgotten?" said Sanehirama, as he looked at him like one who was dazed.

When did you go? I didn t see you. Someone else must have taken you across, because I went home somewhat early yesterday."

Sanehirama then realized that it was Bihari Ji Himself, who had carried him across in the guise of the boatman. The realization brought tears in his eyes. Asta-satvika-bhavas like trembling and sweating also began to appear on his body. He wondered and wondered why Bihari Ji should have taken all that trouble for him. But there was nothing to be wondered at in this. For Bihar! Ji always yearns for an opportunity to serve his devotees in whatever way possible. He has no other work to do. He says.

*madbhaktanam vinodartha karomi*  
*vivid hah kriyah*

**"I perform different kinds of activity only to please my devotees." Padma-purana**

## Chapter 14 - Pisi Ma Gosvamini &amp; Śrī Gopesvara Gosvami

There is a small temple of Gaura-Nitai in Vrndavana near Banakhandi. The deities in the temple were originally worshipped by Murari Gupta, a close associate of Śrī Caitanya Mahaprabhu. Murari Gupta s name is engraved on the pedestal. There is an interesting story relating to the discovery of the deities and their arrival in Vrndavana. After Murari Gupta, the deities were worshipped by his brother and his descendants for several generations in Sarkara Rayapura village in district Siuri of West Bengal. At one time no one in the line of descendants of Murari Gupta remained to serve the deities. An epidemic and the constant scourge of malaria compelled the people of the village to go and settle somewhere else. The desolate village was in course of time turned into jungle. The temple of Gaura-Nitai fell down and the deities were covered by the debris. The cowherds of the adjoining villages began to use the jungle as pasture.

A cowherd, who used to go to the jungle for tending cows noticed that everyday one of his cows strayed from the flock and went and stood at the site of the temple, spraying milk from it s teats. He spoke to the villagers about this. They were mystified. To solve the mystery they dug the spot. On digging the deities came out. The villagers reconstructed the temple in Siuri and made proper arrangement for the service of the deities. After sometime Balarama Dasa Baba Ji, a *siddha* saint of Orissa began to serve the deities according to a message he had received from them in a dream.

Once Candrasasi Gosvamini, a young lady belonging to the famous Mukhopadhyaya family, the landlords of Delagrama in the district of Nadiya, came to Siuri and stayed in a house near the temple of Gaura-Nitai. She felt very much attracted by Gaura-Nitai and developed motherly affection for them. She started offering them *khira* made from 40 kgs. of milk everyday. Once, they said to her in a dream, "Ma, we are very hungry and we want to eat *khira* prepared by you." When she told Balarama Dasa Baba Ji about this, he said, "The scriptures forbid the cooking of food for the deities by a person who is not duly initiated." She, therefore, got herself initiated by Balarama Dasa Baba and offered to the deities *khira* prepared by her own hands. The same night she had another dream in which she saw both Gaura-Nitai holding her *ancala* and saying, "Ma! You do not go away from here. If you go away, who will cook *khira* for us. Besides Ma, you are our mother and we are your children. How can the children live without the mother?" Candrasasi, like a good but helpless mother, lovingly pleaded her inability to stay on in Siuri indefinitely and asked them to leave her *ancala.* But they would not leave it. In the tug of war that ensued a piece of her *ancala* was torn off and remained in the hand of Gaura. When Candrasasi woke up from the dream, she saw that a piece of her *ancala* was actually torn off. Immediately she went to Balarama Dasa Baba and told him about it.

The day had just dawned and the door of the temple was not yet opened. Balarama Dasa Baba went and opened the door Both he and Candrasasi were surprised to see the torn off piece of the *ancala* in the hand of Gaura.

Candrasasi was overwhelmed by a strong current of devotion that in a moment swept away her attachment with home and all its glory and opulence. With determination never to go back she began to live in the temple and serve Gaura-Nitai with motherly affection.

Once while she was cooking for Gaura-Nitai, she began to menstruate. A woman in menses is not allowed to cook for the deities. So she came out of the kitchen and lay in the veranda in front of Gaura-Nitai, looking at them with utter helplessness and bemoaning that they would not be able to eat food cooked by her that day. As she was lamenting she saw, as if in a dream, that both Gaura and Nitai came to her and said, "Ma! why are you lamenting? You are our mother and we are your children. Do what ordinarily a mother would do in this condition for her children. Thereby you will not commit any offence. Go, bathe and cook and give us to eat. We are very hungry. Henceforth you will be free from this malady." Candrasasi never had menses after that.

Candrasasi was only twenty years old at this time. Her staying alone in the temple with a Baba Ji aroused suspicion in the minds of the people. They began to talk slanderously of her and the Baba Ji. This pained her very much. One night before going to sleep, she wept before Gaura and Nitai and complained to them of her plight. The same night she had a dream in which Gaura-Nitai lovingly threw their arms around her neck and said, "Ma! Let us go to Vrndavana."

So Candrasasi and Balarama Dasa Baba took them to Vrndavana. They made the journey by boat. When the boat reached Vrndavana, a devoted lady of Nadiya district, named Bhakta was bathing in the Yamuna. She was very happy to see Gaura-Nitai. She took them along with Candrasasi and Balarama Dasa Baba to her home in Banakhandi. Candrasasi lived there happily and served Gaura-Nitai with all her heart and soul. Bhakta assisted her in service. The residents of Banakhandi called Bhakta Pisi Ma (father s sister). Bhakta called Candrasasi, *didi* (sister). Therefore Candrasasi also began to be called Pisi Ma.

Gaura-Nitai were very restive and frolicsome. They were full of pranks. They always needed something or the other to satisfy their fancy and pestered Pisi with their never ending demands. When Pisi Ma failed to fulfil their demand, they did not hesitate to go and beg for things from whomsoever they liked.

Is it not strange that Gaura and Nitai, the lords of the universe, whose partial manifests are Brahma, Visnu, and Siva, and who create, govern, and destroy the universe at will, should go about begging? It is. But the world of love, in which they love to live and revel, is different from the rest of the universe. Here they are not the great lords, who control and govern, and give reward or punishment. Here they are themselves governed by their devotees, who love them with all their heart and who want nothing from them, but give them all they have. Here they are not the givers but beggars, for although there is no limit to their possessions and power to create new things; there is one thing, which they neither have nor they can create, and that is the love-offering of their devotees, which they cherish above everything else.

Gaura-Nitai did not have *kharaun* (wooden sandals). They did not ask Pisi Ma for it, for they knew that if they did, she would say, "What will you do with the *kharaun?* You do not have to go anywhere." She did not want that they should part from her even for a moment. Therefore they waited for the arrival of another devotee. In the month of *sravana* a devoted lady of Serapura Baguna in West Bengal came to Vrndavana for *darsana.* She stayed in Ciniyakunja, near the temple of Gaura-Nitai.

One day, while it was drizzling, Pisi Ma was sitting in the verandah of the temple. She was pulling the string of the fan for Gaura-Nitai with her left hand and counting beads with her right hand. She became drowsy. In drowsiness she saw that Nitai came out of the temple and got down into the courtyard. Gaura followed. She cried out, "Gaur-Nitai! Where are you going in the rain? You will catch cold." She felt as if she had seen a day-dream. She again began to pull the fan and again became drowsy. Meanwhile Nitai-Gaura went to Ciniyakunja. At that time the above mentioned lady of Serapura Baguna was sleeping. Nitai-Gaura shook her head and said, "Get up, you have not come here to sleep." The lady seemed to have a dream. In her dream state she said, "Who are you?"

"Our names are Gaura-Nitai. We are the sons of Pisi Ma of Banakhandi."

"Why have you come here?"

"We have come to say that we do not have *khandaun.* You give us one. See, how our feet are soiled without it."

The lady was charmed to see the exquisitely beautiful faces of Gaura-Nitai. She had never seen such beauty before. On waking she was still thinking of the two boys and she did not know why tears were constantly flowing from her eyes. In that state she went out in search of them. It was still raining. She asked someone about the house of Pisi Ma of Banakhandi. He pointed towards the temple of Gaura-Nitai. She went inside the temple and asked Pisi Ma, Is this the house of Pisi Ma?"

"Yes, this is the house of Gaur-Nitai, the sons of Pisi Ma, replied Pisi Ma and asked, why are you weeping?"

Where are your two sons? I want to see them," replied the lady.

Pisi Ma said, Oh! They are here," and she opened the door of the temple.

The lady was surprised to see two boys exactly like the Gaura-Nitai of her dream standing before her. She was so overwhelmed with emotion that she fell senseless on the ground. On regaining consciousness she narrated her dream to Pisi Ma and Pisi Ma told her about what she had seen in her drownsiness. Both embraced each other and continued for sometime to shed tears of joy and love.

The fortunate lady presented two pairs of silver sandals to Gaura-Nitai, which they use even now.

Another lady of Serapura Baguna named Prasannadasi, who lived in Vrndavana, once saw Nitai-Gaura in a dream. She saw that they had gone to her, decorated with all sorts of ornaments, and were saying, "See we have all the ornaments, but not *nupura* (jingling anklets). You give us *nupura."* The lady was overwhelmed with love and presented them golden *nupuras.*

A Baba Ji, who had for some time been assisting Pisi Ma in the service of Gaura-Nitai, one night ran away with all their gold ornaments. The next morning, when Pisi Ma opened the door of the temple, she was shocked to see them without ornaments. In her grief and anxiety, as she lay in the verandah of her temple, she slipped into drowsiness. In that state she said to Gaura-Nitai, "Boys! Why don t you tell me who robbed you of your ornaments?"

They replied, "Ma, that Baba Ji who serves us is very poor. We have given the ornaments to him, because he gave us *rabari*to eat many times. Do not say anything to him.

Then what could Pisi Ma do? She could not purchase new ornaments, because Gaura-Nitai had made her a pauper. She only laughed and said, "Very well, do what you like. Give your ornaments to whomsoever you like. I know that if you want them again, you will get them from someone. You do not feel shy in begging, since you are the sons of Brahmins."

Once Gaura-Nitai said to Pisi Ma in a dream, "Ma! Let us go for *parikrama* (circumambulation) of Vraja." So the next day she seated Nitai-Gaura in a palanquin and started for *Vraja-parikrama with two Vaisnavas,* whose names were Mathuradasa and Visnudasa. When they reached Mathura, Pisi Ma and Visnudasa went ahead into the city to make some purchases and left Mathuradasa behind with the palanquin. At that time some British soldiers were stationed in the city. Their curiosity was aroused to see the palanquin covered with a red cloth. One of the soldiers asked, "What is there inside it?"

Deities" , replied Mathuradasa

Show the deities to us!

Mathuradasa was afraid that the soldiers might do some harm to the deities. He said apologetically, Sir, this is not the time for their *darsana"*

The soldiers started using force. Then a strong glow of lightning came out of the palanquin, which dazzled their eyes. They cried, Oh God!" and ran away.

When Mathuradasa told Pisi Ma about this, she said, "I am happy to know that my children are now capable of self-defence. So I need not worry about their safety."

Pisi Ma bathed in the Yamuna three times a day and served Gaura-Nitai with piety and devotion. When she became hundred years old it was not possible for her to serve them anymore. She then deputed Sripada Gopesvara Gosvami, a descendant of Nityananda Prabhu, for their service. Gopesvara Gosvami was a devoted person. His attitude *(bhava)* towards the deities was of the *sakhya* (friendly) type. He wanted to serve Gaura-Nitai to his utmost satisfaction according to his *bhava.* But the difficulty was that the deities were small in size and looked more like Bala-Gauranga and Bala-Nitai (child-Gauranga and child-Nitai) rather than like young Gauranga and Nitai. Therefore they suited the *vatsalya bhava* or parental attitude of Pisi Ma, but not the *sakhya-bhava* or friendly attitude of Gopesvara Gosvami. He told Pisi Ma that the small sized deities failed to arouse his friendly sentiment, therefore he could not serve them with love and devotion. Pisi Ma solved his difficulty She once told Śrī Haridasa-dasa, the author of Gaudiya Vaisnava Jivana that she entered the temple, held the chins of Gaura-Nitai with her hands and pushed them up. Immediately they assumed their present figures, which are taller

But Gaura-Nitai, who were accustomed to the motherly affection and service of Pisi Ma, perhaps could not adjust themselves immediately to the friendly attitude and service of Gopesvara Gosvami. Pisi Ma used to bathe them in warm water during winter. Gopesvara Gosvami bathed them in cold water. So they caught cold.

Pisi Ma now lived in a room on the first floor of the temple and seldom came down. But whenever anything happened, which caused displeasure or discomfort to Gaura-Nitai, its vibrations touched her heart. She came to know about the cold they were suffering from. She came down to see them. She saw that their eyes were red and mucus was flowing from their nose. She wiped the mucus with a corner of her *ancala.* She also touched their body and found that they had some temperature She then called Gopesvara Gosvami and said with tears in her eyes, "What have you done? You have bathed my children in cold water and made them ill. See what severe cold they have caught and how their nose is running." As she said this she showed him the corner of her *ancala* stained with Gaura-Nitai s mucus. Gopesvara Gosvami did not believe. Pisi Ma in anger held the other corner of her *ancala* near the nose of Gaura and said, "Baba, sneeze a bit." Gaura sneezed. Again mucus came out of his nose and the temple was filled with its supernatural fragrance. Gopesvara Gosvami fell down at the feet of Pisi Ma in penitence.

On account of Pisi Ma s over-indulgence, Gaura-Nitai had become somewhat naughty, self-willed and self-assertive. If any one in the temple did anything against their wish, they became wild and did not hesitate to show their temper. This happened once on the day of Kujagara Purnima. On that day Gaura-Nitai used to be brought out into the veranda and a lamp with ten wicks used to be lighted at the gate. But this time Gopesvara Gosvami did not bring them out into the veranda and he went out somewhere early at night after putting off the ten wicks lamp to save oil. This enraged Gaura-Nitai. Suddenly Pisi Ma heard a loud sound and the temple was engulfed in darkness. Gaura had thrown away the lamp inside the temple along with it s stand. Pisi Ma understood what had made Gaura angry. When Gopesvara Gosvami came, she said, "Gopesvara! You did not bring out Gaura-Nitai into the veranda today. You also put off the ten-wicks lamp. See, how angry is Gaura. He has thrown away the lamp and is sitting in darkness! Why do you do like this?"

Pisi Ma was now 106 years old. She called Gopesvara Gosvami and told him about the particular day and time when she would leave the body. That day and at that particular time she was sitting in the veranda of the temple without any illness or indisposition, when she left her physical body to serve Nitai-Gaura and Radha-Krsna in her spiritual body *(siddha-deha).*

After the passing away of Pisi Ma, Gopesvara Gosvami once had a severe attack of small-pox. The disease took a serious turn and he remained unconscious for several days. In that state he saw that a woman looking like a monstress came to carry him away. Just then Pisi Ma came with Gaura-Nitai. On seeing them the woman disappeared Nitai said to Gopesvara Gosvami caressingly, "Oh, get up, if you keep on lying like this, who will give us to eat! Get up, we are very hungry!" Immediately Gopesvara Prabhu regained consciousness. He discharged a large quantity of phlegm accumulated in his chest and became allright.

After Gopesvara Gosvami had served Gaura-Nitai with devotion for a number of years, he developed a feeling of disappointment. He said to himself, "I have served Gaura-Nitai so long. But what have I got? If I had done *bhajana* in solitude, I must have attained something." He appointed another person for the service of Gaura-Nitai and himself went to Kusuma-sarovara for doing *bhajana.*

The third day, at mid-night, when he was sitting on the bank of the beautiful pond called Kusuma-sarovara under a bakula-tree, absorbed in *bhajana*, he saw a light, very soothing to the eye and the heart, appearing in the middle of the pond. Slowly the light came nearer. In a few moments he saw Gaura-Nitai themselves standing before him under the tree. They said, "Dada! For three days we have not taken any food or water. Why have you gone away? Will you not come to us again?" What objection could Gopesvara Gosvami have in going back to them, since he had now attained what he had wanted?

## Chapter 15 - Gaura Dasa Baba Ji

About the year 1893 there lived in Nandagrama in a hut on the bank of Pavana-sarovara, where once upon a time Śrī Sanatana Gosvami used to do *bhajana,* a Baba Ji, whose name was Gaura Dasa He was a *siddha* saint. The main part of his *sadhana* consisted in offering garlands of flowers from Gazipura, a place at some distance from Pavana-sarovara, and weave garlands He rendered this service to Krsna with devotion continually for five years in the hope that Krsna would one day be pleased to give him *darsana* But after five years of service he began to feel that he was hoping against hope Krsna was not as kind as he had thought. If he had rendered the same service to Radharani, she must have shown mercy upon him long before, because, unlike Krsna, she was merciful beyond description. Her body and soul were constituted of mercy and mercy alone.

So he decided to leave Nandagrama and go to Barasana to serve Radharani instead of Nandalala. One day he actually flung his *kantha* (quilt made of rags) on his shoulder and started for Barasana. He had gone only a mile, when he saw some cowherds returning to Nandagrama after pasturing the cows. One of them, with an attractive and lustrous look, paused to see him and asked, "Baba where are you going?"

"Lala,I am going to Barasana," said Baba with tears trickling down his cheeks. The boy looked at Baba with deep concern and said in a loud tone, "Baba don t go."

Baba said, "No, no, Lala, I must. What have I gained living here all these years?"

The boy stretched both of his hands to block the way of Baba and said, "Baba, don t, I will not let you go.

Baba said angrily, "Get away, you mischievous boy. Don t block my way."

The boy exclaimed with eyes swimming in tears, "Baba, if you go away, who will do my *phula-seva* (service with wreaths of flowers)?"

Baba was surprised. He said with a start, "Who are you boy?!" And as he said this the boy, the cows and the cowherds all disappeared.

Now there was no end to Baba s grief. He began to roll on the ground crying, "Krsna, Oh Krsna!" and lost consciousness. On regaining consciousness he again started wailing, "Oh Krsna! How deceitful you are! How you have tricked me! You gave me *darsana*, but I could not recognize you! How could I, if you did not want to be recognized? You did not even stop for a while to let me look at you to my heart s content. Was it because you are so parsimonious in mercy? But no, the fault is not yours. The fault is mine, for I do not have the *bhakti* to recognize or retain you."

Gaura Dasa could not proceed further. He returned to Nandagrama. Nandalala enjoined the *pujari* to see that Gaura Dasa did not give up his *phula-seva.* He said that He would not accept the *phula-seva* from anyone else.

## Chapter 16 - Śrī Radharamana Ghosa Bhagavata-Bhusana

Śrī Radharamana Ghosa was the son of Śrī Krsna Govinda Ghosa, a resident of Dhacca (East Bengal). After graduation he became the manager of the estate of Maharsi Devendra Natha Thakura. Later he was appointed private secretary of Maharaja Viracandra Bahadura, the ruler of Sripura.

Maharaja Viracandra Bahadura was a great devotee of Śrī Caitanya Mahaprabhu Radharamana Ghosa was a man of strong character, will and extraordinary intelligence. But he was proud of it all Besides he was prejudiced against the *vaisnavas* because of their insistence on humbleness.

The Maharaja often went to Vrndavana He stayed there in his house and passed most of his time in the company of the *vaisnavas.* One day he was sitting in a room on the first floor of his house and was busy talking with the *vaisnavas* about things celestial. Outside in the veranda were spread out a number of things of which the most valuable was a beautiful shawl On a tree in front of the verandah sat a monkey He jumped into the veranda and ran away with the shawl. Radharamana and the other employees of the Raja tried to rescue the shawl from the monkey, but their shouts and threats were of no avail. They started throwing bread and fruits etc. towards him. But the monkey did not even look at them. Notwithstanding his greediness and natural liking for fruits, it seemed that for the moment he was interested only in tearing the shawl into pieces. The noise created by the episode drew the attention of the Maharaja. He came out into the veranda and began to look at the monkey, sitting at the top of the tree and tearing the shawl with his teeth, absolutely unaffected by the allurement of so many tempting things to eat. The Maharaja kept on looking at the monkey. Radharamana Ghosa and the attendants saw that he was in a thoughtful mood and tears were trickling down his eyes. Obviously the Maharaja was not weeping for the shawl. But no one had the courage to ask him what had actually transpired within him.

The attitude of the Maharaja particularly aroused the curiosity of Radharamana. He began to look for an opportunity to ask him about it. In the evening he went into his room, when he was sitting alone. After salutation he said to him supplicatingly, "Your highness! We are all very much ashamed that on account of our inadvertence you had to suffer the loss of the precious shawl this morning. But we were surprised that you did not say anything to us. On the contrary, we noticed that the scene of the monkey tearing the shawl aroused some noble sentiment in your heart, of which the signs were visible on your face. I pray that you kindly divulge to me the secret of it all."

The Maharaja laughed and said,"Radharamana, it was not a monkey that tore my shawl. It was a saint, who came in the form of a monkey and went away after teaching me a valuable lesson. The lesson was that I should learn to be humble, if I want to gain anything from my visit to Vrndavana. I should not come here as a Maharaja with my retinue and all the luxurious things I possess. I should come as an ordinary devotee, who owns nothing and wants nothing except what is necessary to sustain life and is helpful in *bhajana.* My heart was filled with gratitude towards the monkey for the lesson he gave me by tearing my shawl and a few drops of tears that fell from my eyes were only an outward expression of what I felt in my heart. Henceforth I shall leave behind the Maharaja in me, whenever I come to Vrndavana."

This touched the heart of Radharamana. A new realization came to him. His pride was subdued and his prejudice against the humble *vaisnavas* was gone. He looked like one dazed and disillusioned. The Maharaja, who had sensed his pride and prejudice long before, now found a suitable opportunity to strike at their root. He said, "Radharamana, it is difficult to be a true *vaisnava.* The essence of *vaisnavism* is humility. Mahaprabhu Himself was an incarnation of humility. He came to show that the mercy of the Lord flows freely towards the humble and lowly, like rainwater that always seeks the lower level. The people, who sit on the mountain top of pride, remain untouched by the stream of mercy. I am a Maharaja. There is no end to my pride. I wonder if the Lord will ever show mercy on a self-conceited person like me?!" As the Maharaja said this tears streamed out of his eyes.

Several times before people had argued with Radharamana Ghosa to convince him that humility was a heavenly quality, and no one, who was not humble, could make any progress in spirituality They were not able to convince him. But the living example of humility he saw in the Maharaja had a powerful impact upon him. He had an intuitive perception of the inner state of equilibrium, self-surrender and self-effacement, necessary for receiving divine grace, which the quality of humility had brought about in the Maharaja He was convinced that humility was heavenly and the religion of Śrī Caitanya, which attached the greatest importance to humility, and which could convert the power-conscious and self-conceited people like the Maharaja and the criminals like Jagai and Madhai into saints, was the highest religion. He decided to become a *vaisnava* of the Caitanya Sampradaya and follow the path of devotion prescribed by Śrī Caitanya.

Radharamana Ghosa was not now the Radharamana, who was proud of his character and considered humility as an infirmity and the *vaisnavas*, who cultivated humility, as inferior sort of persons. He was now himself a humble and devout *vaisnava,* earnestly aspiring for the *darsana* of the Lord. If purity of heart is the condition for the *darsana* of the Lord, as the scriptures say, he now deserved to have His *darsana. For* pride and prejudice, the only two black spots in his heart were now removed. The Lord did bless him with His *darsana* in a manner which was both mysterious and unprecedented.

He now used to pass most of his time in meditation in the forests of Vraja One day, as he was wandering in a forest, he saw a *vaisnava* saint sitting under a *tamala* tree, facing its trunk and reading aloud *Srimad-Bhagavatam.* He was the *vakta* (the person who spoke) and Śrī Krsna, whose presence he imagined in the tree, was the only *srota* (person who listened) Tears of love were constantly flowing from his eyes. Thus he read out *Bhagavata* to Śrī Krsna everyday Radharamana sat behind the saint and listened to the *patha* of *Bhagavata.* When the *patha* was over, he quietly came away, and the saint did not know anything about his presence.

The next day he again went to the same place at the same time and sat behind the saint, listening to his *patha* This continued for a number of days. One day the saint noticed him He asked him reverentially to sit by his side and listen to *patha* everyday till it was completed He also told him how he would be the second listener of the *patha*, the first being Śrī Krsna. Radharamana not only implicitly believed what he said, he was elated with the thought that he would listen to the *patha* in close proximity with Śrī Krsna On the last day of the *patha* a miracle happened. The trunk of the *tamala* tree burst. The hollow thus created in the trunk was aglow with a blue radiance. In the midst of the radiance appeared Śrī Krsna, standing cross-legged with the flute held in His two hands near the mouth and the peacock feather swaying on his crown, and looking tenderly at the two devotees with a smile that was bewitching. Radharamana had the *darsana* of Śrī Krsna, but the very next moment he fell senseless on the ground. When he came to his senses, he saw that he was lying with his head on the lap of the saint. He got up and prostrated himself at his feet. The saint tenderly passed his hand over his head and said, "Radharamana, you have been blessed with the *darsana* of my Radharamana. It is now my injunction that you read out *Bhagavata* to Śrī Krsna everyday. Krsna will be pleased to shower His blessings upon you ever and ever more."

From that day Radharamana began to read *Bhagavata* to Śrī Krsna everyday, either at home or outside under some tree. The saint had told him that in order to read *Bhagavata* to Śrī Krsna it was not necessary to invoke Him, for He loved to hear *Bhagavata* so much that He Himself came running to the place, where *Bhagavata* was read, like a cow that ran after it s calf, and sat in the heart of the reader and the listeners like a prisoner. *(Bh.* 1.1.2) So he read *Srimad Bhagavatam* with the confidence that Śrī Krsna was his lone listener. He not only read out the *slokas,* he explained them as well. His explanations were always Gauranga oriented. They were so masterly and attractive that other people also began to attend his *patha.* He became famous as an exponent of *Srimad Bhagavatam* and *the panditas* conferred upon him the title *Bhagavata Bhusana.*

It was due to the effort of Radharamana Ghosa and the financial aid of Maharaja Viracandra Manikya that the Barahamapura edition of *Srimad Bhagavatam,* with four commentaries and their translation, edited by Śrī Ramanarayana Vidyaratna, and many other scriptures were published.

## Chapter 17 - Śrī Jagadisa Dasa Baba Ji

Not very far from the famous Madan Mohan s Temple in Vrndavana is Kaliyadaha, the part of river Yamuna, where Krsna once danced over the head of the multiheaded cobra called Kaliya. On the bank of the river is a beautiful spot surrounded by trees. Imagine that in the midst of the trees in front of an old cottage is seated an old *mahatma.* Though old, he is tall and well-built. The lustre of his naked body seems to penetrate and dispel the growing darkness of sunset. The slow movement of his lips and the *tulasi* rosary in his hand indicate that he is engaged in *japa.* But his raised neck, fixed gaze and the glowing smile on his face indicate that he is completely lost in the enjoyment of a scene of transcendent beauty. By his side is sitting a very handsome young boy, wearing only a loin-cloth of jute. He is also engaged in *japa.* He looks at the face of Baba and then in the direction in which his gaze is fixed. But not being able to see anything in that direction he keeps on looking at Baba's face with curiosity.

Baba suddenly exclaims, See, Gopala, see! Krsna and Balarama returning from the forest and the cows trailing behind. Oh! How beautiful they look!"

"I do not see anything Baba," replies Gopala with tears in his eyes.

"You will see. I have said, you will see," says Baba, affectionately giving a mild slap on his cheek.

The old Baba Ji is the *siddha* Jagadisa Dasa Baba Ji of Kaliyadaha and the young boy is Dhirendra Natha Cakravarti, the son of Śrī Bhupendra Natha Cakravarti, a landlord of Bengal. The boy later became known as Siddha Śrī Gauranga Dasa Baba Ji of Ramanareti, Vrndavana, but he was called Gopala" by Jagadisa Dasa Baba out of affection.

Jagadisa Dasa Baba Ji came of a respectable Brahmin family of Vardhamana in Bengal. He was a very successful doctor. He used to practise in Kalana. At the age of fifty he took initiation from *siddha* Śrī Bhagavana Dasa Baba Ji of Kalana. Soon after he renounced the world and came to Vrndavana. In Vrndavana he lived for some time in the old temple of Madana Mohana, but later shifted to a small cottage in Kaliyadaha.

Baba s life was simple and austere. He lived on *madhukari* and did not take salt. Bhagavan Dasa Baba ordinarily advised his disciples to do only Harinama-japa. But he considered Jagadisa Dasa to be above the ordinary in devotion.

Therefore he initiated him in Raganuga- bhajana. Practising *bhajana* according to the *raganuga* mode of *bhakti,* he used to be mostly absorbed in deep meditation. Sometimes he would be so absorbed that he would be unaware even of the food kept before him by his disciple for eating and it would remain untouched till he regained outer consciousness. Sometimes people would come to see him, perform *dandavat* and sit down before him but he would not know about their arrival until the absorption was over and someone told him about it. He would then feel very uncomfortable and think that he had committed an *aparadha* (offence) against the visitors. To guard himself against the *aparadha*, thereafter, when he sat outside his cottage; he put *salagrama sila* in front of him so that the *dandavat* made by the visitors might be to *salagrama,* not to him.

Whenever Jagadisa Dasa Baba had any difficulty in lila*-smarana* he sought the favour of the vaisnavas, or the *raja*, the holy dust of Vrndavana. One day, when he had no revelation of *Iila;* he went as usual to Srngaravata for *madhukari* in the evening. Premananda Gosvami, who was at that time the *adhikari* (presiding authority) of Srngaravata said, "Baba, what is the matter? Why do I not see the usual exaltation and brightness on your face today?" Baba replied, "What shall I say? My stars are dull today I stand in need of your benediction." Gosvami Ji understood what he meant He advised him to roll in the *raja* He began to roll on the ground in the courtyard of Srngaravata

As he was doing so, he began to feel that the flood-gate of the current of Krsna-IiIa, which had been closed against him, had to reopened.

Jagadisa Dasa Baba used to swim freely like a fish in the ocean of Krsna-lila. The changing scenario of the *IiIa* brought about corresponding changes in his emotions. His emotions were so strong that they visibly affected the body and brought about corresponding changes in its colour When for any reason the flow of lila stopped, he felt strangled and tossed in pain like a fish out of water The pain would be so severe that he would even think of committing suicide. Once in this state he said to Gopala, "Gopala, would you do one thing?"

"Surely Baba, let me know what I have to do."

"I will stand on the edge of the well You push me into it

For the first time in his life Gauranga Dasa was compelled to disobey Baba Not only that, he started keeping strict watch over him, so that he might not commit suicide Until Baba s original state was restored he always kept close to him.

As ordained by Jagadisa Dasa Baba Ji, Gauranga Dasa lived in Govardhana, where he practised Raganuga-bhajana. But very often he came to Vrndavana to look after Jagadisa Dasa Baba. Once while he was sleeping on the ground near Jagadisa-Dasa Baba s bed, Jagadisa Dasa Baba affectionately planted his foot on his chest. Since then the divine lila of Radha-Krsna began to unfold itself to him.

Once Gauranga Dasa Ji went to Radhakunda. There he was invited by a *sadhu,* whom he had not known before, to stay with him in Radhakunda for some time. He accepted the invitation. But he had stayed with him only for one night when he discovered that the *sadhu* belonged to a pseudo-religious sect, in which woman and wine were necessary parts of *sadhana.* The next morning he left his company. But he found that his heart was empty. The subtle effect of the company of the unholy man had blocked his vision and he was not able to see Krsna-lila. He felt choked like a fish, who suddenly finds that the water of the pond in which it lived, has dried up.

Immediately he started for the *parikrama* of Giriraja. It was the month of June, when the sun is hottest He was walking the whole day in the scorching heat of the sun and praying to Giriraja. In the evening he felt tired and lay down for rest on a step of the staircase of Uddhava-kunda. But he fell asleep. It was dangerous to sleep on the step, because, if he turned sides in sleep, he would fall into the *kunda* (pond) When he woke up he found that someone had lifted him bodily and laid him at a safer place. He looked all round to see who that person could be. But to his surprise he found that no one was anywhere near that place.

The next day he went to Jagadisa Dasa Baba in Vrndavana. No sooner had he laid himself prostrate before his feet than he shouted, "Like a fool you sleep wherever you like. You do not know where to sleep and where not to sleep." Gauranga Dasa understood that it was Baba, who had lifted him from the step of Uddhava-kunda. This brought him the realization that Gurudeva followed the disciple like a shadow wherever he went and protected him. The realization brought tears in his eyes.

Gauranga Dasa then told Baba about the loss of vision he had suffered on account of his meeting with the *sadhu* in Radhakunda, and prayed for his blessings. Baba blessed him and his vision was restored.

Jagadisa Dasa Baba was always very cautious lest he might commit an offence against someone. Saints like him often avoid visitors on account of their absorption in *bhajana.* But Baba was always careful not to disappoint anyone, who came to him. If anyone advised him to the contrary, he said, "My name is Jagadisa Dasa, which means the servant of Jagadisa (the Lord). I regard everyone who comes to me as Jagadisa Himself, Who has come in His form. I feel that it is my duty to serve Him. Mahaprabhu sends a man to me so that I may answer his questions, remove his doubts and give him necessary advice regarding *bhajana.* If I do not serve him in this manner, do I not commit an offence against both him and Mahaprabhu?"

Baba did not arrange *katha* or *patha* at his place, so that people, who came to him for advice at that time might not have to return disappointed. He was available to visitors for conversation at all times, except when he was lost in *lila-sma- rana.* In the midst of conversation he would sometimes himself ask a question and answer it. And then he would ask others to express their opinion, because he did not like to oppose them, if they expressed their views first; and their views happened to be different from his. After the conversation, when people wanted to leave, he would anxiously look at their faces to see whether they were going back happy and satisfied or not.

Once Baba s brother came from Bengal. Baba treated him affectionately and inquired about his welfare. When he said that his wife was dead, Baba said, "God s grace! He has made you free. You can now come to Vrndavana and do *bhajana."*

After the brother had gone, he began to think, "My brother did not look happy, when he was leaving. Possibly he was displeased with me, because instead of expressing sympathy with him in his bereavement, I had said that God had done him a favour." If Baba had known where he was staying, he would have gone there to apologize to him. But he had said on leaving that he would return to Vardhamana in a day or two. So he went to the station everyday for two or three days with the expectation that he might meet him there and apologize. But he could not meet him. He also did not know his Vardhamana address. Therefore he wrote a letter to a friend in Vardhamana asking him to inquire from his brother and let him know whether he had forgiven him. The friend replied that his brother was not at all displeased. He did not go to see him again, because he did not want to disturb him in his *bhajana.* Since then, whenever someone came to see him, he first inquired about his address.

Baba never found fault with anyone. Also he did not talk ill of anyone. He did not go anywhere to listen to any religious discourse, because he apprehended that the speaker might say something which he did not like or approve; and that might make him find fault with the speaker.

In Vrndavana the saints often organize festivals to celebrate certain occasions, as for example, the birthday of Rama or Krsna. But Baba never organized any festival, because that disturbed his *bhajana.* The devotees generally regard it as incumbent on them to observe the disappearance day of their *guru.* Baba observed the disappearance day of his *guru* by purchasing one *malapua* and giving a piece of it to everyone who came to him as *prasada.* Once a devotee named Sinu Babu offered him Rs. 40, so that he might celebrate the disappearance day of his *guru* decently. He could not turn down the offer made in the name of the *guru.* He went to the market and purchased mollases worth Rs. 20 for preparing *malapua.* After keeping the mollases in his cottage, he went to a nearby well for washing his feet, but forgot to close the door of the cottage. On returning he found that a number of monkeys had entered the cottage and were freely feasting on the mollases. Baba laughed to his heart s content to see the monkeys feasting. There was no question of his driving them away, for they were the monkeys of Vrndavana and they had kindly invited themselves to the function in connection with the celebration of the disappearance day of his *guru.* When the feast was over and the monkeys were leaving he respectfully bowed down to them.

In the evening when Sinu Babu came and inquired about the celebration, he said, "The celebration was very successful. A large number of monkey vaisnavas feasted on mollases. The feast cost only Rs. 20. Here are the remaining Rs. 20, which I thankfully return. Regarding *prasada* you may go inside the cottage. Probably you will find some particles of it scattered on the ground."

Jagadisa Dasa Baba was free from all desires. But once a desire arose in his mind. He desired that a beautiful lake be built in kaliyadahato commemorate Krsna s sport with the cobra called Kaliya. He mentioned this to Kamini Babu, the manager of Rajarsi Banamali Rayabahadura. Kamini Babu began to raise contributions for the project. Readily the rich people began to contribute. A widow of Lala Babu s family contributed seventy-five thousand rupees. The Raja of Hetamapura contributed twenty-five thousand. Rajarsi Banamali Raya agreed to take responsibility for the rest of the expenditure. The map and estimate for the construction of a beautiful lake were prepared in consultation with Jagadisa Dasa Baba. But soon Baba found that the lake had taken the place of Krsna-lila in his mind. This made him so angry with himself that he went and hid himself in some forest for a number of days. People went out in search of him, but he could not be found. Suddenly one day he came back. Kamini Babu asked for his permission to start the work of digging. He became very grave and said, The desire for the lake caused obstruction in my *bhajana.* If now the work starts, my *bhajana* will be over. So I insist that no one should make even a mention of it so long as I am alive."

Jagadisa Dasa Baba Ji was very humble. Mahaprabhu has said in the famous *trnadapi sloka* that one should be humble like a blade of grass and tolerant and forbearing like a tree. He should give respect to everyone without desiring to be respected by others. In Baba the meaning of the *sloka* had assumed a concrete form. When someone asked him how to achieve *prema,* he replied, "In order to achieve *prema* one should try to mould himself into the frame of the *trnadapi sloka.* The more he moulds himself according to it, the nearer he will be to *prema*, and when he has completely moulded himself, he would most certainly achieve it. He also said, " When your mind is completely free from all desires, except the desire for *prema-bhakti,* then and only then you will realize Krsna-bhakti."

*Siddha* saints can leave their body at will. When Jagadisa Dasa Baba became a hundred years old, he desired to leave his body. At that time Gauranga Dasa Baba Ji was living with him. On account of his affection for Gauranga Dasa it was not possible for Baba to leave the body in his presence. Therefore he asked him to go and live in Barasana and serve the Bhanukhara *sarovara (a* large pond) by sweeping and cleaning its surroundings everyday. When he was about to leave he said to him, "Remember three things: Never ask anyone for any favour. Never disclose the secret of your heart to anyone. Never attend a feast.

Gauranga Dasa began to live in Barasana and render regular service to Bhanukhara Kunda. After some days Jagadisa Dasa Baba appeared before him in a luminous body. He saw him standing at some distance from him. But as he moved towards him he asked him not to do so by waving his hand and disappeared. Gauranga Dasa Ji understood that Baba had left the body. Struck with grief he immediately started for Vrndavana. On the way he learnt that Baba had actually left the body on the sixth day of the second half of the month Asadha (1915 A.D.).

## Chapter 18 - Pandita Śrī Ramakrsna Dasa Baba Ji

Pandita Ramakrsna Dasa Baba Ji s earlier name was Ramapratapa. Ramapratapa was born in the year 1857 in a family with strong traditions of *bhakti.* His grand-father was a *vaisnava* of Ramanuja Sampradaya. He was *siddha* in *narayana kavaca*, and was well versed in the *sastras.* Ramasinha, the Maharaja of Jaipura, appointed him as a tutor for *yuvaraja* (crown prince). He also gave him two villages as *jagira* and built a house for him near his palace. His son Laksminarayana was also a great devotee. He left home at an early age and went to the forest, where for a number of years he did *japa* of Rama-mantra. He was blessed with the *darsana* of Rama and his brothers mounted on horses. Rama asked him to go back home and marry. From his wife Kamala Devi was born Ramapratapa.

Ramapratapa was only three years old when his father died. His extraordinary intelligence and love for learning enabled him to learn by heart the *sutras* of Panini, *sarasvata vyakarana* and *amara-kosa,* when he was only eleven years old.

After his sacred thread ceremony at the age of eleven, he performed Savitri-purascarana and had the *darsana* of Savitri Devi. Savitri Devi advised him to go to Vrndavana. He tried to go to Vrndavana, but his mother did not permit. However, at the age of thirteen he somehow succeeded in sneaking away. In Vrndavana he lived near Govinda Ji s temple under the care of the Gosvami of the temple.

Even after coming to Vrndavana he continued his studies. He learnt *nyaya* from Sudarsana Sastri of Ranga Ji s temple, Srimad Bhagavatam from Nrsinha Dasa Ji of Karauli-kunja, and Hari-bhakti-vilasa from Gopilala Gosvami. While studying *satasamdarbha* he used to read fifty chapters of Srimad Bhagavatam before Gopesvara Mahadeva everyday. He completed his education at the age of twenty.

After completing his education he took *diksa* and *vesa* from *siddha* Nityananda Dasa Baba Ji and was named Śrī Ramakrsna Dasa. But he was generally called Pandita Baba on account of his learning.

On the advice of Gurudeva he went to Govardhana to learn Krsna-lila-smarana from *siddha* Srikrsna Dasa Baba of Govardhana. After this he began to live in Barasana and practise *lila-smarana.*

In Barasana lived the famous Kirtaniya Gauracarana Dasa Baba Ji. Pandita Baba began to learn Bihagana *kirtana* from him. Gradually his absorption in *kirtana* increased and absorption in *lila-smarana* decreased. Gurudeva asked him to stop learning *kirtana* and to devote himself wholly to *bhajana.* He stopped learning *kirtana.* But even then he could not concentrate on *bhajana.* Gurudeva then asked him to do *purascarana* of Krsna-mantra for seventeen days. He did the *purascarana* in Uddhava-kyari under a Kadamba tree. During the first three days he took only milk, during the next twelve days only water and on the remaining two days not even water. On the seventeenth day he had the *darsana* of Radha-Krsna.

This was confirmed by Baba himself once while he lived in Dau-Ji s *bagici* (a small garden), the present site of Vrndavana Research Institute in Ramanareti. That day he was lying under the tin-shed in the *bagici.* Some devotees, including Priyasarana Baba, Krpasindhu Baba and Lalita Mohan Gosvami were sitting at some distance and talking about the *purascarana* performed by Baba. One of them said that Baba had the *darsana* of Krsna-Balarama after *purascarana.* Baba suddenly spoke out, "No, not Krsna-Balarama, Radha-Krsna."

He further added, "Radha-Krsna said, we are pleased with you. Let us know what you want?

I replied, I do not know what I want except your happiness. Radha and Krsna then smiled and disappeared."

After this Baba began to live in Raghava s cave in Puchari, the hind portion of Giriraja, in which Raghava Pandita, an associate of Śrī Caitanya Mahaprabhu lived. By the grace of Radha-Krsna he had now become *siddha* in *lila-smarana*. From 2 o clock in the morning to 2 o'clock in the afternoon he used to do *lila-smarana* inside the cave. After that he came out of the cave. At this time there used to be a crowd of *sadhus* in front of the cave They sought his advice in *bhajana.* Baba removed their doubts and difficulties. Then *patha* and *kirtana* were performed. This continued till evening In the evening Baba went out for *madhukari*

After some time Baba s absorption in *lila-smarana* became so deep that it became impossible for him to follow any routine. No one knew when he would sit down for *smarana* and when he would come out of the cave Sometimes he remained lost in *smarana* for two or three days continuously and did not go out even for *madhukari.* The *Sadhus* who used to collect in front of his cave were deprived of his company.

Once, at this time, Baba s mother came from Jaipura to see him. She had to remain sitting outside the cave waiting for him for two days. The third day, when he came out of the cave, he went straight for *madhukari* and did not even cast a glance at her. His mother followed him for some distance weeping and crying. Even then he did not turn to look at her She returned home with a broken heart.

When his mother had gone, he began to have difficulty in *lila-smarana* and felt restless. He went to Krsna Dasa Baba and inquired about the cause. Baba said, "The cause is obvious. You have committed an offence against your mother".

He then wrote a letter to his mother apologizing and requesting her to come to Vrndavana. He arranged for her stay in a separate house in Puchari, in which she lived till her death. He took care of her and did everything possible to make her happy. His *sakti* for *IiIa-smarana* was restored as soon as he started serving her.

Baba s mother was also a devotee. Krsna may forgive any offence committed against himself, but he never forgives an offence committed against his devotee.

After some time there arose a conflict in Gaudiya vaisnava Sampradaya regarding Gaura-mantra, some holding that a separate Gaura-mantra was necessary, others holding that it was not necessary, because Gaura was essentially Krsna. Pandita Baba did not want to involve himself in this conflict. Therefore he went to Barasana to live in Mora-kuti, a solitary place on the top of the Barasana hill. He lived there for eight years. After the conflict was over he again came back to the cave.

On a winter night he was sleeping inside the cave with the door closed, while a charcoal stove was burning in the cave to provide heat. In the morning, when he got up, he was surprised to find himself outside the cave. Obviously someone had brought him there to save him from the gas of burning charcoal, which could be fatal. This was however nothing unusual, since, as we have said before, the spiritual agencies in Vraja often come to the help of the *sadhakas* in times of difficulty or need.

Once again spiritual help came to Baba when some people, belonging to another *sampradaya* tried to harm him. Baba was generally respected by the *acaryas* of all the *sampradayas* of Vraja, because of his devotion, learning and broad-mindedness. He was well-versed in the *sastras* of all the *sampradayas* and was also conversant with the modes of their religious practice. Pandita Amolaka Rama Sastri and Baba Hamsadasa of Nimbarka Sampradaya, Śrī Sudarsanacarya, Śrī Dulari Prasada Sastri and Ganapati Sastri of Ramanuja Sampradaya, Samkarsana Dasa Ji of Ramananda Sampradaya and Śrī Purusottama Bhatta Ji, the famous scholar and *katha vacaka* of Pusti Sampradaya - all came to him for advice on matters relating to the *sastras* or religious practices, and Baba always advised them according to the philosophy and practice of their own *sampradaya.* Even Pranagopala Gosvami, a descendant of Nityananda Prabhu, and the biggest exponent at that time of Gaudiya vaisnava philosophy; and Srimad Bhakti Siddhanta Sarasvati Maharaja, the founder president of the Gaudiya Mission, held him in high esteem and came to him for advice. But some orthodox and vicious people of a particular *sampradaya* got jealous of him. They once tried to defame Śrī Rupa and Śrī Sanatana, the most respected Acaryas of the Gaudiya Sampradaya, by preaching that they originally belonged to their *sampradaya*, but were ousted from it on account of their misconduct. Baba forcefully criticized them. They felt very much humiliated and conspired to kill him. One day they hid themselves somewhere near his cave, so that when he went out for *madhukari* they might attack him on the way. But when Baba came out of the cave and started towards the village for *madhukari,* they saw that a lion was following him like a domesticated dog. When he reached the village the lion hid himself somewhere near the *samadhis* of the *gosvamins* on the right side of the village. When Baba returned to his cave the lion again followed him up to the cave and disappeared. The miscreants were both surprised and frightened. They realized that Baba was a *siddha mahapurusa* and their hostile attitude toward him was transformed into one of love and devotion.

One day, when Baba was doing *bhajana* inside the cave, a poisonous snake crawled over his body and coiled round his chest and neck. But he continued his *bhajana* undisturbed. After some time the snake crawled down and disappeared. At night, Baba heard a voice. Someone said, "You leave this cave." Immediately Baba left the cave and began to live in Shyama-kuti near Kusuma-sarovara.

Pandita Baba never gave *diksa* to anyone. But many people, who received instructions from him in *bhajana* regarded him as their *siksa guru.* Important among these were Śrī Gauranga Dasa Ji, Śrī Priyasarana Dasa Ji, Śrī Krpasindhu Dasa Ji, Śrī Visnu Dasa Ji, Śrī Kesava Dasa Ji, and Śrī Lalita Mohan Gosvami. There were also some, who, although never initiated by anyone, regarded him alone as their *guru*, as for example Thakura Kusala Sinha Ji of Gijagarha.

Gauranga Dasa Baba Ji, whose former name was Dhirendra Natha Cakravarti, and who was the son of a rich landlord of Calcutta; and a brilliant student of Scottish Church College, Calcutta, renounced the world at the age of twenty He took *diksa* from *siddha* Śrī Rama Dasa Baba Ji Maharaja of Calcutta and came to Vrndavana In Vrndavana he began to do *bhajana* under the guidance of Siddha Śrī Jagadisa Dasa Baba. Jagadisa Dasa Baba was very much impressed by his *bhakti-bhava* and extraordinary intelligence He asked him to study the *sastras* from Pandita Baba Pandita Baba took special interest in him and began to teach him. In due course he acquired mastery over Srimad Bhagavatam, Sata-Samdarbha and the other *bhakti-sastras.* Pandita Baba then asked him to take up one book after another and deliver discourses on it in the presence of *sadhus,* who had again started coming to him in the evening. His discourses began to attract large crowds and became a regular part of Baba s routine in the evening.

Priyasarana Baba also was young, when he came to Vrndavana. He was a student at Varanasi and came to Vrndavana on a short visit. But on meeting Pandita Baba he felt so much drawn by him that he never went back. Although he was already initiated in Nimbarka Sampradaya, he accepted him as his *siksa guru* and began to do *bhajana* under his guidance. Baba guided him according to the mode of worship and meditation prevalent in the Nimbarka Sampradaya. Just as Gauranga Dasa rose to be a famous *siddha mahatma* and a leader of the Gaudiya Vaisnava Sampradaya, Priyasarana Baba rose to be an eminent *mahatma* of the Nimbarka Sampradaya.

About this time also came Krpasindhu Dasa Baba. He endeared himself so much to Baba by his devotion to him that he accepted him as his lifelong companion and servant. He made good progress in *bhajana* under Baba s guidance. He became so advanced and so much attracted to Baba inwardly that he could know his mental condition even when he was away at some distance. Once Baba was ill. He became so weak that he could not speak. He felt thirsty and asked for water by saying a, a which the people by his side could not understand. Krpasindhu had at that time gone out for *madhukari.* He also began to feel very thirsty He drank water a number of times but the thirst could not be quenched. He understood intuitively that Baba was thirsty. Hastily he returned to Baba and gave him water. His thirst was automatically quenched along with Baba s.

Baba had severe toothache. A doctor was called to extract the teeth. The doctor asked

Baba, "Which tooth aches, Baba?"

I do not know" replied Baba.

The doctor laughed and said, "Then who knows, Baba?"

"Krpasindhu knows."

Krpasindhu pointed out the teeth.

In 1918 Pandita Baba had influenza. He was brought to Vrndavana for treatment. For eight years he lived in Vrndavana at different places. From 1926 he began to live in Dau Ji s Bagici, where he lived till the end.

Visnudasa came to Baba when he had shifted to Dau Ji s Bagici. He was at that time only fourteen years old. He was sent by his *guru* Śrī Yadunandana Dasa Baba Ji of Suryakunda to serve Pandita *Baba* in his old age and to do *bhajana* under his guidance. He came all the way from Suryakunda to Vrndavana on foot. Because the day was very hot he got fever on the way. When he reached Dau Ji s Bagici, the fever was so high that he lay down near the well of the *bagici.* Baba came out of his room in the evening, when it was time for *madhukari.* Visnudasa lay prostrate at his feet in obeisance. Baba said, "Welcome Visnudasa, let us go for *madhukari*."

"Baba, I have high fever," said Visnudasa.

"So what?" said Baba. He held him by the hand and took him along with him. The moment Baba held his hand, he felt that his fever and fatigue had vanished. The hand once held by Baba was never left. Visnudasa remained with Baba till the end, following him like a shadow wherever he went. Visnudasa Baba gained much from Baba s company. Baba used to be absorbed in *Lila* even when he went out for *madhukari* with Visnudasa. His inner experience was sometimes transferred to Visnudasa like an electric current on account of his touch, because he often held his arm or shoulder while going and coming.

One day in the month of June, Baba was returning to his *kuti* after *madhukari* with Visnudasa, his left hand resting upon his right shoulder. Suddenly on the way Visnudasa experienced that the environment had changed. The hot wind of June had turned into cool and soothing breeze of winter, and the atmosphere was filled with the sweet smell of *kadamba-flowers* of spring. Visnudasa seemed to be transported into a state of bliss. He said, "Baba! What is this?" Baba said, "By Radharani s grace you have been given a glimpse of the real nature of Vrndavana. Vrndavana is celestial. It only appears to be terrestrial. In Vrndavana all the seasons coexist and serve Radha-Krsna according to Their wish. When a *sadhaka s* heart is purified by *bhajana*, he sees the spiritual Vrndavana and the *IiIa* that eternally goes on here."

Thakura Kusala Sinha was a big *jagiradara* of Rajasthana and the chief justice of Jaipura highcourt. He was also very learned, religious, fearless and strong-willed. He resigned the post of chief-justice and came to Baba with the intention of renouncing everything and becoming a *sadhu.* He requested Baba to give him *vesa (vaisnava samnyasa).* Baba said, "You need not renounce. Is it not possible to do *bhajana* as a householder? My days are gone. Bad time is now coming. Sadhus will do everything that is done by a *grhastha.* They will build palatial buildings, keep bank accounts and live luxuriously. It is better to stay at home and do *bhajana* quietly. Thus you will not be known as a saint and nobody will come to disturb you."

Kusala Sinha Ji gave up the idea of becoming a *sadhu.* He used to come to Vrndavana once in a month or two and return to Jaipur after living in Baba s company for some time.

Thakura Hari Sinha was the *jagiradara* of Nimoniya in Jaipur state. Like Kusala Sinha Ji, he also regarded Baba as his *guru* and used to come to him for *satsanga.* Once he said to Baba, "Baba, my wife is seriously ill. Astrologers have predicted that her stars are bad and she will die soon. The doctors have also lost all hope for her. Kindly advise what she should do in her last days for her salvation."

Baba said, "Let her read Srimad Bhagavatam. Her stars will become favourable. The stars are unfavourable only to the people, who are no devoted to the Lord. They are friendly and serviceable to those, who are devoted to the Lord."

"But she is too weak to be able to read Bhagavata," said Śrī Hari Sinha Ji.

"Then you can read it to her."

Thakura Hari Sinha started reading Bhagavata to her. The stars actually turned in her favour.

She became allright and is alive till this day.

Pranagopala Gosvami came to Vrndavana from Calcutta every year and read Srimad Bhagavatam and other *bhakti-scriptures* before Baba for a month. Once, even before the scheduled time of his arrival, Baba began to make anxious inquiries about him from everyone who came. People were surprised and said, "Baba, why are you so anxious? He will come at the scheduled time in the afternoon. It is only about 12 o clock now." But Pranagopala came one day late, because there was a train collision at Bamarauli and hundreds of people travelling in the same train, in which he was travelling died. The compartment, in which he and his party were travelling, was safe, while the compartments just before and after were completely crushed. When Pranagopala arrived in Vrndavana, he said that Baba s anxiety had saved him and his party, *otherwise how could his* compartment remain safe, when those before and after it were crushed?

Pranagopala Gosvami was famous for his learning and masterly exposition of the *sastras.* He was generally regarded as the final authority on all religious matters. Whenever anyone had any doubt or difficulty, he approached Pranagopala Gosvami for solution. But when Pranagopala Gosvami himself had any doubt or difficulty, he sought the advice of Baba. Baba invariably solved his difficulty. Many times the difficulty was solved merely by going to Baba and without actually referring it to him. His mere presence was enough to infuse intuitively into his mind the right solution.

Even more mysterious than this was the manner in which Baba once solved the difficulty of Gauranga Dasa Baba Ji relating to the commentary of the *sutra, "anandamayo-abhyasat."* At that time Baba was living in a cave at Ramanareti, which is known as Madanamohana-ki-tibari. Gauranga Dasa went and expressed his difficulty to Baba from outside the cave. He heard Baba giving the solution from inside. He then asked Gauranga Dasa, "Have you understood?"

"Yes Baba," said Gauranga Dasa. But when Gauranga Dasa was returning to his *kuti* he was surprised to see Baba coming from the opposite direction with Amolaka Rama Sastri. Gauranga Dasa said, "Baba, I have just been to your cave.

I had some difficulty with a *sutra* and you solved it. How is it that I now see you here?"

Baba parried the question and said, "Many *siddha* saints live in Vrndavana, who are invisible. They help the *sadhakas* in their difficulty unnoticed."

Pandita Baba was a living example of Śrī Caitanya Mahaprabhu s "*trnadapi sunicena" sloka*, according to which one should be humble like a blade of grass and respect everybody without desiring to be respected by anyone. Whenever he went to a religious gathering in which *katha* or *kirtana* was going on, he sat behind everyone else. No one had the courage to request him to take a forward seat.

He never allowed anyone to touch his feet. A resident of Bihar, who suffered from severe stomach-ache, was told by someone in a dream, "If you rub the dust of Pandita Baba s feet, your disease would be cured." He also gave him the address of Pandita Baba. The man came to Vrndavana. He met Pandita Baba in Banakhandi, when he was returning to his *kuti* after *madhukari.* He wanted to touch his feet. But Pandita Baba rebuked him and did not let him touch his feet. The man felt very much mortified and disappointed. Someone advised him to see Krpasindhu Baba. Krpasindhu Baba advised him to take a pinch of dust from Baba s foot-print, when he went out for *sauca* (nature s call) next morning. He did the same. He was cured of the disease immediately after rubbing the dust of Baba s feet on his forehead.

Another man of Bihar who was suffering from the same disease, also came to Baba. He said, "Baba, I have come from Bihar. I am suffering from a bad stomach disease. If you kindly let me have the dust of your feet, I am sure I will be cured." Baba said, "The disease will be cured not by taking the dust of my feet, but by giving me the dust of your feet." As he said this, he took the dust of his feet and rubbed it on his forehead. This man s disease was also immediately cured.

Once a man, who saw Rama Dasa Baba Ji Maharaja of Pathabari *asrama,* Calcutta touching the feet of Pandita Baba, said to him, If you can allow a great saint like Rama Dasa Baba Ji to touch your feet, why can t you allow us?"

Baba replied, "Rama Dasa Baba Ji is a *mahatma.* Whether he touches my feet or asks me to touch his, in either case I have to acquiesce."

Baba was generally courteous to the visitors. But when someone went to him with some worldly proposition, or did something, which was against the *vaisnava* rules of conduct; he became furious. Once a Raja came to him in his usual dress with all his retinue. He brought with him a purse full of gold *mohurs.* He kept the purse before Baba and sat down. Baba asked, "What is this?"

"Some gold *mohurs* Baba. You can use them as you like," replied the Raja.

Baba said, "They are of no use to me. You give them to someone else."

"But I have brought them for you, Baba. You have to take them," the Raja said in a somewhat dictatorial tone.

Baba became furious. He showered a volley of abuses upon him and asked him to quit. Still the Raja persisted. Baba then shouted, "If you do not leave at once with your purse, I will break my head." He was about to beat his head against the ground, when the Raja stood up, took the purse and went away.

The Raja was friendly with a Vrajabasi Pandita. He narrated the whole story to him and said, "What kind of a *siddha mahatma* is he? I wanted to do some service to him. But he suddenly vituperated and gave me a thousand abuses. What business had he to insult me like this?"

The Vrajabasi said, The fault is yours. What you thought was service to Baba was actually disservice to him. You persisted in disservice. Therefore Baba became angry. You have committed an offence against him and by doing so you have invited the wrath of the Lord, who never excuses anyone for an offence committed against His devotee. You can be freed from the offence if you go with me to Baba and do as I say. If you do that you will also see how kind hearted and courteous he is.

The Raja agreed to do as the Vrajabasi advised. He went along with him to Baba the next day. As advised by him he did not take his attendants with him and did not put on the royal dress He went like an ordinary man with *tilaka* on his forehead, *tulasi-kanthi* round his neck, and a rosary bag in his hand which made him look like a *vaisnava* devotee.

Both he and the Vrajabasi Pandita performed *dandavat* before Baba and sat down. After Baba had inquired about the *pandit s* welfare, the *pandita* said, I am told that the Raja caused much annoyance to you yesterday. I am sure he will come to grief for the offence he committed."

Baba said, "No, no. He did not commit any offence. He was very good. Although a Raja, he heard my abuses without saying anything. I abused him for his own good. If I had accepted his purse and used it in the service of the *vaisnavas,* it would have poisoned their mind. Besides, they would have been harassed by thieves and dacoits. The Raja would have had to suffer on account of his responsibility in bringing about that state of affairs,"

The Raja was moved to hear this. He fell upon Baba s feet and said, "Baba, I am that foolish Raja. Kindly excuse me for the offence committed by me."

Once Gauranga Dasa Baba Ji went to Radhakunda. He was to stay there for the night and return the next morning. He was persuaded by a *sadhu* of Baula Sampradaya to stay at night with him. His real intention was to convert him to his faith, in which the company of a woman was an essential part of *sadhana.* He treated him well, gave him nice things to eat and arranged for his bed. When he was sleeping, he felt the soft touch of a woman massaging his feet. He suddenly got up, rebuked the lady and the *sadhu* and left for another place. The *sadhu* feared that sooner or later Gauranga Dasa will report the matter to Pandita Baba Ji and it would become impossible for him to stay in Vraja. He thought that Pandita Baba would probably not give credence to his report if he poisoned his mind against him beforehand. He went to him the next morning and said, "Baba, you think that Gauranga Dasa is a good *sadhu.* You do not know that he secretly keeps the company of women."

Baba could not tolerate this. He was wild with rage. He burst out, "You devil! You have the cheek to malign a great saint like Gauranga Dasa. You do not know what the consequence will be. You will die the death of a barking dog.

Go, I have said you will die the death of a dog."

Soon after he was bitten by a mad dog and died barking like a dog.

Baba s *vairagya* was unparalleled. The only possessions he had in his *kuti* were l*angoti*, *bahirvasa*, *karava* and some books. If anything besides these was brought and kept in his *kuti* by anyone, it became a victim of his wrath and was thrown out. Once during summer season Priyasarana Baba brought a new pitcher full of water and kept it inside the *kuti.* Baba saw it on returning from *madhukari.* Immediately he brought it outside and smashed it. He asked Priyasarana, "Why did you bring it?"

Priyasarana replied, "I brought it, because it is too hot and you repeatedly ask for water."

"I know, you brought it because you had to draw water every time from the well. Fool that you are, you do not know that it will cause disturbance in my *bhajana* by inviting others to drink water."

Once Baba was ill. He was advised by the *vaidya* to take *makaradhvaja.* Syamasundara Dasa, a *vaisnava* attendant of Baba, used to ionize *makaradhvaja* with the help of a *kharala*and *lorhi.* If Baba came to know that a *vaisnava* was taking so much trouble for him, he would have stopped taking *makaradhvaja.* So Syamasundara did ionization when Baba was out for *madhukari* and kept the *kharala* and *lorhi* always concealed from him. Somehow the *lorhi* broke and Syamasundara asked Kusala Sinha Ji to bring another. One day Baba saw Kusala Sinha Ji giving something wrapped in a piece of paper to Syamasundara. After Kusala Sinha had gone he asked Syamasundara what he had brought. Syamasundara said, "Nothing Baba, only a small *lorhi* for grinding *makaradhvaja."*

Baba stood up. His face was red with anger. He picked his *karava* and the *madhukari* bag and said, "Only a small *lorhi!* You live here with the *lorhi*, I am off." As he was about to leave Syamasundara fell at his feet and said, "Baba! I have committed an offence. Kindly excuse me, otherwise I shall die."

"Then go and return the *lorhi* immediately."

Syamasundara immediately went and returned the *lorhi* to Kusala Sinha Ji. Then Baba s anger subsided.

Baba took *madhukari* only from the Vrajabasis. Even from the Vrajabasis he never took anything brought from outside Vraja. Once someone brought *parabala* in a large quantity for Narahari Dasa Baba Ji, the *mahatma* of Radhakunda. Narahari Dasa sent some *parabala* to Pandita Baba. Pandita Baba said to the man who had brought *parabala*, "Tell Narahari Baba that he should not send me anything brought from outside Vraja."

A visitor, who was present, said, "Baba, what harm if you take something brought from outside?"

Baba replied, "Do you do *bhajana?* If you did *bhajana* you would have realized what harm is done by taking anything brought from outside."

An ordinary person may find it difficult to comprehend this. But Baba was not an ordinary person. He was a saint, whose heart was pure and totally engrossed in Vraja and Vraja-IiIa. There was no place in it for anything that was not part and parcel of Vraja-IiIa or which was in any way associated with anything outside Vraja. Any such thing appeared to him unacceptable like a black spot on a white sheet.

Baba also did not take the *prasada* of temples, in which service of the deities was done with the financial aid of a worldly-minded person. Once Kusala Sinha Ji brought the *prasada* of Govinda Ji from Jaipura along with a garland of flowers used by Govinda Ji. Baba wore the garland with pleasure. But he took only a particle of the *prasada* to avoid *aparadha* and distributed the rest among the *vaisnavas.* He said to Kusala Sinha Ji, Never bring Govinda Ji s *prasada* again. Govinda Ji alone can digest it." Govinda Ji s *seva* was financed by the Maharaja of Jaipura.
