Again, we may not understand how the *prasada* financed by a person given to worldly enjoyments is not good for the spiritual health of the *sadhaka.* But this is a fact, which has been verified by the *sadhakas* times out of number. The subtleties of the spiritual world are beyond the comprehension of our material minds. If Baba was knowingly or unknowingly given anything to eat which did not come from the right source, he could easily detect it on account of the subtle disturbance it created in his mind. Subsequent inquiries about the source always confirmed his intuition.

Once Rajarsi Banamali Rai Bahadura sent the *prasada* of his deity Śrī Vinodi Lala to Baba. Baba took only a particle of that also and distributed the rest among others. At night Vinodi Lala said to him in a dream, Why did you not take my *prasada?* My seva is done out of the earnings of my own property and not Banamali Rai s." This was correct. The property out of the income of which Vinodi Lala s seva was done stood in His own name.

The next morning Baba went all the way from Govardhana to Vinodi Lala s temple in Vrndavana on foot and begged for His *prasada.* He ate the *prasada* to his fullest capacity. After that he never gave Vinodi Lala an opportunity to make a similar complaint.

Like any other *siddha mahatma*, Baba had the capacity to perform miracles, but he tried, as far as possible, not to use it for fear of becoming known as a *siddha purusa.* But if circumstances compelled him to do so, he tried to explain the miracle away in some way or the other. Once Pranagopala Gosvami sent him Rs 200 for feeding the *vaisnavas* in connection with the death anniversary of his mother. Accordingly Baba arranged for a feast to which a limited number of *vaisnavas* were invited. The same day came Rama Dasa Baba Ji and his party of Bengali *vaisnavas* from Calcutta. Rama Dasa Baba sent word to Pandita Baba that he would go to his asrama with his party in the evening and perform Gaura Gadadhara *kirtana.* Baba s standing instruction to Krpasindhu Dasa Baba was that any *vaisnava*, who happened to come to the *asrama* when an *utsava* or feast was going on; he must be fed along with the other *vaisnavas.* Therefore, extra food for 10 or 12 persons was always prepared. But in Rama Dasa Baba Ji's party there were about 300 *vaisnavas.* They could not all be fed with the limited amount of food that was prepared. Krpasindhu Baba expressed his concern in this connection to Pandita Baba. Pandita Baba treated the problem lightly. He said, "Do not worry. Bengali *vaisnavas* eat very little." This did not relieve Krpasindhu Baba of his anxiety. But what could he do? He resigned himself to fate. However, in the evening, when the feast was over, he was surprised to find that everybody had eaten, still there was a small quantity of food left. Baba said to Krpasindhu, "Didn t I say that the Bengali *vaisnavas* eat very little?" Krpasindhu Baba did not say anything, but he laughed within himself at Baba s cleverness.

Gradually Baba became very old and weak. It was not possible for him to go out for *madhukari.* He began to live on fruit juice. But though physically weak, he was mentally alert and his *bhajana* went on as usual. He got up at 2 o clock in the morning and continued to sit in *japa* and *IiIa-smarana* till 2 o clock in the afternoon. *Nama-japa* had become a part of his life. It continued all the time without any break. Even while asleep he was sometimes heard chanting the name. His finger, which remained outside the bag of rosary while doing *japa*, had become so straight and stiff that it could not be bent and used even at the time of eating.

Slowly Baba s intake of fruit-juice also became less and less and his weakness went on increasing. He remained mostly confined to bed in his *kuti.* When he wanted to go out of the *kuti* his attendants carried him on his bed. Once, while he was being brought out in this manner, he chanted humorously,  *Ramanama satya hai*  (the name of Lord alone is true). Customarily this line is chanted aloud by the carriers of a dead body, when it is carried to the funeral pyre. Although the line was chanted by Baba in good humour, Krpasindhu Baba thought that he had hinted at his end, which was very near.

On hearing that Baba s condition had worsened, Kusala Sinha Ji and Hari Sinha Ji came from Jaipura. They called a Vaidya from Calcutta and treatment was started. But what could the Vaidya do, if Baba himself did not want to live. After a few days Kusala Sinha Ji and Hari Sinha Ji went back to Jaipura. But while taking leave of Baba, Hari Sinha Ji prayed to him mentally that he and Kusala Sinha Ji be informed before the apprehended end came. His silent prayer was noted by Baba.

Baba asked Krpasindhu Dasa to call Gada-dhara Dasa Baba Ji, Kirtaniya and his party from Govardhana and arrange for one month s *astakalina IiIa kirtana* in the asrama*.* This was another indication to Krpasindhu Baba that Pandita Baba was preparing for the end. He made all arrangements for the *kirtana.* The *kirtana* went on for a month and Baba remained all the time absorbed in *IiIa.*

One evening, when all those, who were close to Baba were sitting round him, he said, When I leave the body, you leave the *bagici* and go elsewhere." Perhaps he said this to ensure that none of his attendants took possession of the *bagici* after him.

Someone asked, Baba, your *samadhi*"

No samadhi. Your bhajana will be my samadhi."

And what about *utsava?"*

No *utsava.* That day all of you bring *madhukari,* sit together and eat."

Then he turned his eyes towards the young *sadhakas* of his asrama and said, "Save these boys from women."

The next day was Radhastami. Baba fasted as usual. On Ekadasi again he fasted. Krpasindhu Baba insisted on both the days that he might take some *pancamrta* or at least some water, because he was getting weaker and weaker with every fast, but in vain. On greater insistence he said with folded hands, "Please do not break my fast."

After a couple of days he asked Krpasindhu to call Kusala Sinha Ji and Hari Sinha Ji by wire. Krpasindhu did not pay any heed to this, because he thought Baba s condition was not yet too serious. The next day Baba asked again, "Have you sent the telegram to Jaipura?"

After this Baba went into *samadhi.* The telegram was sent.

On *dvitiya*, the second day of Krsna-paksa of the month of Asvina Baba suddenly spoke out. He said to Krpasindhu, "See, See Priya and Priyatama (Radha and Krsna) have come."

Priya-Priyatama are alone, or your *manjari svarupa* *(siddha* body) is also there?" asked Krpasindhu.

Baba replied, "My *manjari svarupa* is also there."

On *caturthi*, the fourth day of the month, Baba asked Krpasindhu to prepare for him a bed of the *raja* (holy dust) of Vrndavana. Everybody was shocked to hear this.

The bed was prepared and Baba was laid on it. Just then arrived Kusala Sinha Ji and Hari Sinha Ji from Jaipura. Kusala Sinha Ji had brought Govinda Ji s *prasadi mala* (garland) with him. He put the mala round Baba s neck. The moment he did this Baba left the body.

Govinda Ji came in the form of the *mala* and took him away.

## Chapter 19 - Śrī Manohara Dasa Baba Ji

Manohara Dasa Baba Ji s earlier name was Mahendra. He was born in 1847 in a village called Madhava-pura in district Nadiya. His mother Pyari-sundari died when he was six years old and father Śrī Bholanatha Adhikari, when he was thirteen. After the death of his father he took *diksa-mantra* from Śrī Nandakisora Gosvami, a descendant of Śrī Advaitacarya.

Mahendra was fond of learning from his early childhood. He completed his primary education in a *tola* in Madhavapura. After initiation, he went to Navadvipa for studying Sanskrta Grammar, Alamkara, Nyaya and Darsana, first under Vipinacandra Bhattacarya and then under Śrī Krsnacandra Smrtiratna.

He took *vesa* from Śrī Svarupa Dasa Baba Ji of Bana Akhana of Navadvipa. After *vesa* he was named Manohara Dasa. While in Navadvipa Manohara Dasa Baba had the good fortune of enjoying the company of *siddha* Śrī Caitanya Dasa Baba, Pandita Narottama Dasa Baba Ji Maharaja of Bana Akhana of Navadvipa and *siddha* Śrī Bhagavan Dasa Baba of Kalna.

In 1881 he went to Vrndavana. For five years he studied Bhaktisastras from Śrī Gopilala Gosvami Prabhu of Śrī Radharamana\* temple and availed himself of the company of *siddha* Mahatmas like Balarama Dasa Baba Ji of Jhanumandala, Jagadisa Dasa Baba of Kaliyadaha and Nityananda Dasa Baba of Madanamohana Thaura. After that he practised *bhajana* in Kusuma-sarovara, Kamyavana and Nandagrama. In 1893 someone asked him in a dream to go and live in Govindakunda at the foot of Giriraja. Since then he began to live in a *kuti* under a banyan tree in a garden in Govindakunda. At this time *siddha* Śrī Ramakrsna Dasa Pandita Baba lived in Puchari, about one and a half miles from Govindakunda. Often, when he went for *madhukari* to village Anaura, he also visited Manohara Dasa Baba in Govindakunda and enjoyed talking with him on various topics relating to the *IiIa* of Radha-Krsna.

In 1915 Monohara Dasa Baba built a cave in Govindakunda, in which he lived and practised *bhajana* throughout the day and came out only in the evening for *madhukari.* He did *lila-smarana* and *nama-japa* all the time. *Japa* and *smarana* continued even at night. He slept very little, perhaps only for an hour or two. He repeated *harinama* no less than 7 lacs times a day. He ate only r*oti* (bread) with soup prepared from the leaves of *neema* tree. Later he gave up *roti* and took only milk in very little quantity. He avoided the company of people and spoke very little. He was too humble. He bowed down to all and sundry, and never allowed anyone to touch his feet. He always talked ill of himself.

His absorption in *bhajana* was so deep that he was almost unaware of the body. Sadhus in Govardhana used mosquito net to protect themselves from mosquitoes, but when they insisted on his using a mosquito-net, he said, Mosquito, does not do any harm to us. It only helps us in *bhajana* by keeping away sleep."

Once it was so cold in Vraja that the temperature reached below freezing point. Baba had only a *kantha* (rags or shreds patched together) to cover himself. At night his body shivered and created obstruction in *bhajana.* Getting angry with the body, he went and plunged into the icy-cold water of the Govindakunda. Navadvipa Dasa Baba, who lived near by, asked him why he plunged into the *kunda* at night. He replied, The body has begun to ask for more clothes. It must be disciplined."

Navadvipa Dasa Baba said, In severe cold it is necessary to use a quilt."

"Vairagis, who have renounced the world, do not use quilt," retorted Baba.

Baba s wholehearted and steadfast application to *bhajana* and his complete indifference to body and the world was proverbial. No wonder, therefore, that the mercy of Radha-Krsna descended upon him and he became *siddha.* He used to have direct perception of Krsna-IiIa. He wrote a book pertaining to Krsna-lila called Vaidagdhi-vilasa. He also wrote a book on Harinama*,* titled Namaratna-mala.

Once a *vaisnava* asked him how to get over the difficulties and obstacles in *bhajana.* He replied, "One must be firm in *bhajana.* To a man, who practises *bhajana* steadfastly and with all his heart and soul, staking for it his life and everything else, the obstacles begin to appear as illusory as the horns of a hare. The Lord Himself makes the path of *bhajana* smooth for him. But this does not happen in a day; both perseverance and patience are necessary."

Manohara Dasa Baba used to say that *bhajana* is not possible without complete surrender at the feet of the Lord and total dependence upon Him. It is only in the state of utter dependence on Him that one enjoys the calmness of mind, which is necessary for *bhajana.* A mind, which is disturbed by various kinds of worldly anxiety, is not fit for *bhajana.*

He defined *bhajana* as a state in which the mind is constantly absorbed in the meditation of the Lord and revolts to the thought of everything else as to poison.

## Chapter 20 - Śrī Madhava Dasa Baba Ji

SriMadhava Dasa Baba was born in 1851 in village Bargharya, situated near the confluence of rivers Mahananda and Padma in Bengal. He was only a child when his father died. Therefore his education remained incomplete. But he had learnt only to read and write, when he began to spend most of his time in the study of *bhakti* literature, *kirtana* and *sadhu-sanga* (company of holy persons). At the age of 27 he renounced the world and went to Vrndavana. He took initiation from Śrī Parmananda Prabhupada of Srngaravata and began to do *bhajana.*

After sometime he obtained the *guru s* permission to practise *bhajana* in some quiet and lonely place in Vraja. He went to Samketavata and began to live there with Śrī Jagadananda Dasa Ji, a disciple of *siddha* Nityananda Dasa Baba of Vrndavana. Madhava Dasa Baba was very much benefited by the company of Jagadananda Dasa, who was an ideal *sadhaka.* Both of them went out separately for *madhukari* in the evening, but sat down to eat together on their return.

One day Madhava Dasa Baba got some butter-milk in *madhukari.* As he sat down to eat he said, It would be better if I add some salt to the butter-milk. Let me go and beg for some salt."

Jagadananda Dasa Baba cautioned, "Madhava Dasa, don t yield to every temptation. Always beware of the mind! It is by nature restive. There is no end to its craving and demands. Today it is asking for salt, tomorrow it will ask for molasses and then for sweets and what not. Your *bhajana* will be spoiled." Madhava Dasa abided by his advice. He learnt to be content with whatever he got in *madhukari.*

Once there was a festival in some village. Madhava Dasa had gone there to participate in the feast. When he returned from there, Jagadananda asked, How far is that village from here?

"Four miles, replied Madhava Dasa.

"How many *malapua*sdid you eat?"

"Eight."

"How do you feel now?"

I am feeling tired and drowsy."

Then why live in a place, from where you have to travel four miles for eating *malapual?* Why not go and live in Vrndavana, where feasts and festivals are so frequent and one does not have to go far for eating *kacauri* and *malapua?"*

Jagadananda Dasa Baba s reprimand had the desired effect. Madhava Dasa stopped going to feasts.

After sometime Jagadananda Dasa went to Barasana and began to live there on the bank of Bhanukunda. Madhava Dasa accompanied him. While living in Barasana Madhava Dasa also got the opportunity of availing himself of the *satsanga* (holy company) of Pandita Ramakrsna Dasa Baba and Śrī Haricarana Dasa Baba Ji.

From Barasana Madhava Dasa went to Kamyavana. There he lived and practised *bhajana* for a number of years on the bank of Vimalakunda near the place, where *siddha* Jayakrsna Dasa Baba used to do *bhajana.* Single-minded application to *bhajana,* continued over a number of years, purified his mind. Subtle remnants of all worldly attachments and desires were eliminated. The only desire that remained was the desire for the *darsana* of Radha and Krsna. The desire became so strong that it was impossible for him to live without its fulfilment. One day he actually thought of committing suicide. He said to himself, "I have been doing *bhajana* for such a long time, but the two Lords of my heart, whose *bhajana* I have been doing, have not appeared to me once even in dream. Even Radha, who is by nature merciful, has not been kind to me." "But," he said, "The fault cannot be attributed to her. The fault must be mine. My heart is full of craftiness and my *bhajana* has not been sincere. I do not really deserve to have her *darsana.* Then why should I carry the useless load of life? Why should I go for *madhukari* and suffer unnecessary pain for keeping soul and body together. Let the body die." That day he did not go for *madhukari.* The fire of repentance and self-reproach was burning in his heart. He lay down and started weeping.

Just then a young *brahmin* girl called at his door, "Baba, O Baba!" He opened the door and saw the girl standing with a *thala* (a metallic dish) in her hand, which was full of *puri, kaucauri, khira* and other delicious things to eat. She said, Baba, you did not go for *madhukari* today. My mother has sent this to you. You must eat.

Baba recognized the girl. Her arrival with food just at the time, when he had decided not to go for *madhukari and* eat any more, made him think that it was a kind gesture from Radharani, who had prompted the girl s mother to send the food to him. He felt reassured that sooner or later he would be blessed by her *darsana.* He accepted the food.

Vrajabasis regard anything offered by a *sadhu* out of his *madhukari* as sacred and eat it with relish. Therefore Madhava Dasa Baba asked the girl to eat some of the good things she had brought. But she said, No Baba, today a neighbour offered me some good things to eat and I have eaten to my fill. I can t eat any more."

The girl went away. Baba sat down to eat. He was surprised by the unearthly odour and taste of everything he ate and by the *sattvika bhavas* it generated. He had taken *madhukari* from the girl s house a number of times before, but never before he had that kind of experience.

Also, he now began to think, he had never before seen on the girl s face the kind of divine lustre he saw, when she came with the food, nor had he ever seen her looking at him so tenderly and talking to him so sweetly. Was it then the same girl or Radharani Herself, who had kindly come in her guise? The thought sent a shiver in his body and brought tears in his eyes. Immediately he set out for the home of the girl. The girl s mother was milking a cow in front of the house. She said, "Baba, you are late today. Just wait a little, I shall give you *madhukari*."

"Ma! have you not already sent me a *thala* full of puri*, kacauri* and *khira* through your *lali?* "What?" said the lady, "Someone else must have sent those things. My daughter has not gone out since morning.

"No Ma, you can inquire from Lali. It was she, who came and gave those things to me."

Immediately the lady called her daughter and said, "Look Lali, what is Baba saying? He says, "You went to him a little while ago with a *thala* full of *puri, kacauri* and *khira*," and the lady laughed. The girl also laughed and said, "Baba, you must have seen a dream. Were you awake or asleep when you saw me handing over the *thala* to you?

Baba was satisfied that it was Radharani, who had played the trick. But in order to conceal everything he said, "Yes Lali, you are right. I must have seen a day-dream. I do not know what has happened to me," and he returned to his cottage.

Baba was happy that Radharani had at last given him *darsana.* But he was sorry that She came under the guise of the *brahmin* girl. He only cursed himself for this, for he thought he did not deserve to see Her in Her real form.

It is said that *bhakti* is the mother of humbleness. Madhava Dasa was so humble that he regarded himself as the lowest of the lowly and as untouchable by others. Therefore he never accepted the invitation for a *bhandara* or feast. But when there was a *bhandara* in Srngaravata, his *guru s* place, he had to go. His *guru* knew that he would not sit to eat in the *pangata* (line) of the *vaisnavas.* Therefore he let him sit down in the line of the low class people and the beggars inspite of protests made by the *vaisnavas.*

Madhava Dasa s humility and his devotion to the service of the *vaisnavas* had endeared him to all the *vaisnavas* of Vraja. They offered to make him the *mahanta* (head) of the *asrama* of *siddha* Jaikrsna Dasa Baba of Kamyavana. He could not disregard the *vaisnavas.* Therefore he agreed to be the *mahanta* for a short time, after which he again began to wander freely in Vraja. He lived longer in Vrndavana, sometimes in the Samadhisthana of Gopala Bhatta Gosvami, sometimes in Lotana-kunja and sometimes in Jhanumandala.

While living in Jhanumandala he once thought of the general belief that Sanatana Gosvami, who was specially charged by Śrī Caitanya Mahaprabhu with the responsibility of looking after the needs of the *vaisnavas*, who renounced the world and went to live in Vraja for *bhajana,* even at that time discharged his responsibility towards them and did not let them go without food. He decided, in order to test the belief, not to go for *madhukari* that day and to eat only, if anyone offered anything to eat on his own. After that when he was passing along the *samadhi* of Gopala Bhatta Gosvami that day, he saw Mathura Dasa Baba, the *sevaka* (servitor) of the *samadhi* standing at the gate of the *samadhi*, as if he was waiting for him. On seeing him he said, "Baba, are you going for *madhukari?* Don t go today. Come inside. Thakura s *bhoga* is ready. I am suffering from fever. You kindly offer it to the Thakura and to Gopala Bhatta Gosvami and eat." Madhava Dasa did likewise. His heart was filled with gratitude towards Sanatana Gosvami, whose mercifulness he thus realized fully.

In 1892 Śrī Radhikanatha Gosvami arrived in Vrndavana. Madhava Dasa was drawn towards him on account of his soul inspiring Hari-katha (discourses on Śrī Hari). Radhikanatha Gosvami was drawn towards Madhava Dasa on account of his *bhava-bhakti* and his capacity to understand and enjoy the delicacies of Rasa. They came closer and closer till it became impossible for them to live without each other. Everyday both sat together and talked for hours of Krsna and Krsna-lila, losing consciousness of time and place, hunger and sleep.

In 1894 Rajarsi Banamali Rayabahadura, the Jamidara of Tanasa, came to Vrndavana. He was drawn towards both Radhikanatha Gosvami and Madhava Dasa and joined their company. He had a palatial building in Radhakunda, where he lived for some time, but he built another in Vrndavana, which is now known as Tanasa Mandira. He began to live in this building so that he might always have the company of Madhava Dasa Baba Ji.

Śrī Krsnananda, a *samnyasi* of Sankara Sampradaya was so much influenced by Madhava Dasa Baba that he began to regard him as his *guru.* On his advice he took *diksa* from Pranagopala Gosvami and became a prominent *vaisnava* of Caitanya Sampradaya, who had a very large following.

For about 18 or 19 years towards the end of his life, Madhava Dasa Baba lived in Gopala Chanidar s Asrama in Gopinatha Bagha. During this period *patha, kirtana* and *vaisnava-seva* were regular features of the *asrama.* Śrī Raghunandana Prasada Sinha, the Raja of Munghera used to finance most of these activities. Once Madhava Dasa said to the Raja, "When I die the *vaisnavas* will approach you for donation in connection with an *utsava* (celebration with feast) on that occasion. Do not give them anything. Give me as much as you can at present so that I can spend more on *patha* and *kirtana*." The Raja started giving donations more freely.

In 1933 Madhava Dasa Baba left the body to enter the transcendental Vrndavana, in the midst of *kirtana* performed by the famous Kirtaniya Śrī Ganesa and his party, and in the presence of Prana Gopala Gosvami and other prominent *vaisnavas.*

## Chapter 21 - Śrī Jagannatha Dasa Baba Ji

There lived in Barasana on the bank of Bhanukunda a Baba Ji, who left Vardhamana, his native place at an early age and came to Barasana to live here and meditate in the hope that he would be blessed with the *darsana* of Radharani. His name was Jagannatha Dasa Baba Ji.

Baba lived only on a few pieces of bread he got in *madhukari*, but one could see in his cottage a number of earthen pots, which contained rice, wheat, pulses and other food grains and a number of small bags, which contained *amalaki* (emblic myrobalan), *harra* (myrobalan), *bahera* and other medicines. These were meant not for him, but for the *sadhus* and Vrajavasis, who came to him, when in need. He also cooked for them at times.

Once Barasana was plagued by a severe famine. Prayers and cries for mercy rented the sky of Barasana. Baba hesitated in going to the Vrajavasis for *madhukari.* He could not consume the foodstuffs he had stored for the *sadhus.* He had now to give it all away to them, because they were the people worst affected by the famine. He could not go elsewhere, because the moment he thought of leaving Barasana his heart began to sink and tears began to flow from his eyes. He did not know what to do. He passed day after day without a morsel of food, but ultimately he had to decide to leave Barasana.

As he was leaving Barasana with bag and baggage, a young Vrajabasi girl, whom he knew, came and asked, "Baba, where are you going? "

Somewhere, " Baba said, with tears in his eyes.

"But why? "

"Why? There is dearth of *madhukari* here in your village. How can I live without *madhukari?*

What do you say, Baba? Dearth for you? My mother keeps *madhukari* for you everyday. Why don t you come? Even today she has kept *madhukari* for you in the kitchen. Go and take it and do not go away from here.

Baba was very hungry. He thought, since the girl s mother had already kept *madhukari* for him, it would be better to eat before going out. He went back to his cottage, kept the luggage there and then went to the girls s house.

As soon as the girl s father saw Baba, he said, Baba, you have come very. late. There is no *madhukari* now. Why didn t you come earlier? "

"But I have come on the invitation of your *lali,* who told me that her mother had kept *madhukari* for me in the kitchen. "

"My *lali!"* said the Vrajabasi amazedly. "She is not here. She went to her father-in-law s long ago. You must have mistaken some other girl for my *lali*. "

"No, no. It was she, I am sure. "

The Vrajabasi smiled, but he turned to his wife and said, "See if there is something in the kitchen you can give to Baba to eat. He must be hungry. "

His wife went to the kitchen. As soon as she opened the door of the kitchen, she shouted "Oh my God, how wonderful! Who kept this *thala* full of *roties*, *data*, rice and vegetables here? I didn t. "

The Vrajabasi was stupefied. But suddenly the thought flashed in his mind: "It must have been Radharani herself, who went to invite Baba in the guise of my l*0li* and who kept the *thala* in my kitchen for Baba. Who else could it be? " Baba also thought the same. He was so overwhelmed with *bhava* that tears streamed out of his eyes, and his heart, burdened with an intense feeling of gratitude towards Radharani, drowned deep into the ocean of her mercy.

The Vrajabasi s wife gave to Baba everything Radharani had placed for him in the *thala.* Baba used to accept only pieces of bread in *madhukari,* but this time he accepted rice, *dala* and the other things as well, because the *madhukari* was from Radharani.

As Baba took leave of the Vrajabasi, both the Vrajabasi and his wife said, "Baba, do come everyday for *madhukari.*  Baba was reminded of Śrī Krsna s assertion in Gita that He Himself took care of His devotees and the story of the *brahmana* devotee, who doubted it s verasity. The story goes like this:

There lived in Jagannatha Puri a virtuous *brahmana pandita* with his wife. He lived on alms, but he was very devout. He loved Gita and used to pass all his time in reading and meditating on it s verses. Once he was meditating on the following sloka*:*

*ananyascintayanto mam ye janah paryupasate*  
*tesam nityabhiyuktanam yogaksemam vahamyaham (Gita 9. 22)*

**"Those who are exclusively devoted to Me and always think of Me and nothing else, I Myself own the responsibility of fulfilling all their wants (including the want of emancipation or the attainment of my feet) and protecting them from all ills. "**

He thought the word *vahamyaham,* which mean "I Myself carry the burden of fulfilling their wants" was inappropriate. It should have been *karomyaham*, which meant, "I arrange for the fulfilment of their wants or get them fulfilled through others. "Why should Krsna, who was the controller and governor of the universe and whose commands are obeyed by Brahma, Siva and Visnu and all other gods and goddesses, Himself carry any burden on His head? So he scored the word "*vahamyaham* and wrote *"karomyaham. "*

One day it rained so heavily that the *brahmana* could not go out for alms. He and his wife both had to go without food. He went out the next day, when the rains stopped. Not long after he had gone out, a very handsome boy, who was carrying provisions on his head and whose forehead was bleeding on account of a scratch, came in and said to his wife, "Ma, Pandita Ji has sent this *prasada. '* Pandita s wife was charmed to see the boy s lovely face and hear his sweet voice, but was pained at the sight of blood, dropping from his forehead. She said, "My child, who has hurt you? "

Pandita Ji", replied the boy.

"Pandita Ji! Which Pandita Ji? "

"Your husband. "

"My husband! He is so simple and kind hearted. Why should he hurt a lovely boy like you? "

"I tell you the truth Ma. It was your husband, who pierced my forehead with the point of a dart. "

"But why? "

"He alone knows why. "

The boy kept the load of provisions before the lady and disappeared. She was aghast to see him disappear before her very eyes.

When the *brahmana* returned, his wife narrated the story to him in words, betraying at once astonishment, grief and resentment. The poor *brahmana* was dumb-founded. There was no end to his grief and repentance. For it did not take him long to understand who that lovely boy was. The thought dawned upon him that Gita was a verbal image of Krsna and by scoring through one of it s words with his pen he had actually pierced the body of Krsna. Also by Himself bringing the provisions to his house, He had proved the literality of the truth that He Himself carried the burden the of responsibility of fulfilment of the wants of his devotees. The *brahmana* was so full of remorse on account of his guilt that he fell senseless on the ground.

As Jagannatha Dasa Baba thought of this story, he said to himself, "If Krsna can be so merciful to His devotees, how can Radharani lag behind? She is even more merciful. "

The author of Gaudiya vaisnava Jivana wrote about fifty years ago that even at that time by. the grace of Radharani, Banakhandi, the descendant of the Vrajabasi family, who used to give *madhukari* to Baba, was the happiest and the wealthiest man of Barasana.

## Chapter 22 - Śrī Pranakrsna Dasa Baba Ji

Pranakrsna Dasa Baba was born in 1802 in village Bagnapana of Vardhamam district of West-Bengal. He took initiation from Yadunandana Prabhu of Bagnapana, a Gosvami in the *parampara* (disciplic succession) of Ma Jahnva, but practised *bhajana* for a long time under the guidance of *siddha* Bhagavan Dasa Baba of Kalna. Thereafter he went to Vrndavana and began to live in Kaliyadaha near the *kuti* of *siddha* Sr! Jagadisa Dasa Baba.

He had carefully read the slokas IX, 12, 1-2 of Srimad Bhagavatam, in which Śrī Krsna says to Uddhava:

"O Uddhava! *Yoga, jnana, dharma*, study of the Vedas, austerities, renunciation, sacrifices, works of public utility such as the excavation of well, tanks etc, fasts, pilgrimages, self-restraint and observance of religious rules, all these cannot captivate me. But I am easily captivated through *satsanga* (association of holy persons), which uproots all worldly attachments. "

He was convinced of the importance and the utter necessity of *satsanga.* He knew that the Lord could be attained only through *prema* or love for the Lord and love for the Lord can be attained only by the grace of a Mahapurusa, who has attained the grace of the Lord:

*mukhyatastu mahatkrpayaiva bhagavat krpalesabdva*  
*(Narada -Bhaktisutra-38, )*

He, therefore, adopted the service of the *mahapurusas* as the primary means for the attainment of divine love. Fortunately for him there lived close to his *kuti* in Kaliyadaha two great saints, who had attained the grace of the Lord - Śrī Jagadisa Dasa Baba and Śrī Dayala Dasa Baba. He began to serve them with all his heart and soul. Both of these *mahatmas* usually remained absorbed in the divine *Iila,* mostly without consciousness of the body and its needs. Pranakrsna Dasa Baba carefully looked after them. He cooked for them and sometimes even fed them with his own hand. He massaged their body and rendered all kinds of menial service to them. Besides, in their changing moods he served them by singing songs according to their *bhava.*

Through the service of these saints, who were *siddha* (accomplished) in *manasi-seva* (service through contemplation) of Radha-Krsna, he also became *siddha* in *manasi-seva.* In *manasi-seva* he had adopted sweeping of the *nikunja* (the transcendental grove or retreat) of Radha-Krsna as his main service. While engaged in this service he outwardly appeared as sweeping Kaliyadaha, but inwardly, that is, in his *siddha deha,* that is, in his spiritual body as a *gopi* of Vraja, he swept the *nikunja.* In his *siddha deha* he was not only an observer but also a participant in the *Iila* of Radha-Krsna.

Śrī Rupamadhuri Ji, a *siddha* saint of Suka Sampradaya, who was a contemporary of Pranakrsna Dasa Baba, wrote a poem in Braja-bhasa in his praise, in which he said, "Baba is 104 years old. He has made Radha-Krsna captive in his heart. His words are extraordinarily sweet and exhilarating. " *(Rupamadhuri Ji Ki Vani, 3-19)*

The reason why his words were so sweet and exhilarating was that they were the outpourings of a heart in which dwelt Radha and Krsna, the twin embodiments of transcendental sweetness and bliss.

Radha and Krsna were drawn to him, because they were fond of listening to talks about themselves and Pranakrsna Dasa always thought and talked about them and did nothing else. Srimad Bhagavatam says:

*srnvatah sradhaya nityam grnatasca svacestitam*  
*kalena natidirghena bhagavan visate hrdi*  
*(Srimad Bhagavata, 2. 8. 4)*

**"Those who always narrate or listen Krsna-kathawith faith, in their hearts, Śrī Bhagavan enters soon. "**

Pranakrsna Baba was not learned. He could hardly read or write. Yet he had learnt Krsna Dasa Kaviraj s *Govinda Lilamrta*, describing the *astakalina IiIa* of Krsna by heart. He meditated on Krsna-lila as described in that book. He had become so old, meditating and talking always of Krsna-lila, yet his eagerness to think and talk of it, instead of decreasing, had gone on increasing day by day. He did not simply think or talk about the lila, but saw it and seeing it, he sometimes laughed and sometimes wept. Even at night, when he was supposed to be sleeping inside his *kuti,* he was drowned in *IiIa* and could be heard sometimes laughing, sometimes weeping and sometimes talking.

When the cup of his heart got overfilled with the nectar of Krsna-lila, he became eager to share it with someone else by talking to him about it. If he did not find anyone to listen to him, he would, in his impetuosity, go out and sit on the *parikrama-marga* (the path of circumambulation) of Vrndavana by the side of beggars. While the beggars persisted in their request to the pilgrims, on circumambulation, to give them something, Baba persisted in his request to take something from them. He offered them *prasada.* They could not refuse to take *prasada.* When they came near and extended their hand for *prasada*, he looked at them courteously with eyes brimming with tears and said prayerfully, "would you listen to Krsna-Katha? Krsna-Katha is very sweet. " The manner in which he implored was so courteous and persuasive that, even if they did not have any interest in Krsna-Katha, they could not say no. He described Krsna-lila, to them in such fascinating way that the seed of devotion to Krsna got firmly implanted in their hearts.

It is said that in a devotee, who has realised Krsna, all noble qualities like humility, kindness, forbearance and non-attachment exist in their ideal form. In the case of Jagadisa Dasa Baba and Pranakrsna Dasa Baba, this can be beautifully illustrated by means of an episode, which relates to them both.

People, who came to Jagadisa Dasa Baba, usually made some offerings to him. But he did not even touch them. He had asked Pranakrsna Dasa Baba to collect and keep them aside for the service of Thakura and the *vaisnavas.* Once a visitor offered him ten rupees along with a *lota* (small water pot) and *balati* (bucket). Pranakrsna Baba collected them. A greedy Jatadhari Baba Ji (Baba Ji with matted hair) who lived nearby, said to Jagadisa Dasa Baba, "Give them to me. " Jagadisa Dasa Baba readily promised to give. He thought he would ask Pranakrsna Dasa to give them to him, but he forgot. The Jatadhari Baba Ji got angry. After two or three days he threw a brickbat towards Jagadisa Dasa Baba. It did not hit him, but it reminded him of his promise. Immediately he called Pranakrsna Dasa and said, "I have committed an offence against the Jatadhari Baba. I promised to give him the *lota-balati* and the money that visitor had offered, but I forgot. He has kindly reminded me of it by throwing a brickbat at me. We should give those things to him. "

"Yes, we must, " said Pranakrsna Dasa Baba Ji. We should also request him to take everything that is offered to us, considering it as his own. If he agrees, we should feel very grateful to him, because he would thus keep *maya* away from us.

Jagadisa Dasa Baba was very happy to hear this. The next day he invited the Baba Ji to his *kuti* for *prasada.* He came. After greetings and obeisance he was made to sit for *prasada.* He was served *sandesa, rasagullas, rabani* and other delicacies. While Jagadisa Dasa Baba served him nicely, Pranakrsna Dasa Baba fanned him with a palm-leaf fan in his hand.

During the course of service Jagadisa Dasa Baba said, "Maharaja, I beg to be excused for forgetting to send you the promised *lota-balati* and the money. I should have sent them the same day. But you know how forgetful and negligent I am in my behaviour. "

As he said this Pranakrsna brought the *lota-balati* and the money and placed them before the Baba Ji. Jagadisa Dasa Baba added with folded hands, Maharaj, I have one more request. You know that although we are both old, we are like your children and you are like our father. We are living here and doing *bhajana* under your kind protection. We are so weak and imbecile that we may yield to temptation and come under the clutches of *maya* any moment. You are so powerful that Maya cannot even touch you. You can hold *maya* captive in your *kamandalu*.So, kindly keep your *kamandalu* here. The visitors will put their offerings in it. You kindly lift it every evening. Thus you will help us keep *maya* at a distance. "

The Baba Ji kept on listening. He bent his head with shame. The fire of repentance began to burn in his heart. He could reply only by shedding *tears. He went* back to his *kuti leaving the lota balati and the* money behind. He *could not sleep that night. From the next day, he* became not only the *true protector, but* also *the* true servant of Jagadisa Dasa Baba and Pranakrsna Dasa Baba.

Pranakrsna Dasa Baba was 104 years old, when Rupamadhuri Ji wrote the aforesaid poem about him. Even at that time he was full of energy and there was power and attraction in his *speech. But he lived and continued to inspire and* guide *people* in *raganuga bhajana tor 32 more* years. He left the body in 1938 at the age of *136. His samadhi exists near* the *samadhi* of Jagadisa Dasa Baba in Kaliyadaha.

## Chapter 23 - Śrī Manasinha Rajavata

Śrī Manasinha Rajavata was the eldest son of Śrī Savaisinha the Jagiradara of Lorani, a *jagira* under Jaipur state comprising 12000 *bighas* of land. He was born in 1913. As he grew, his qualities of head and heart inspired the chroniclers of the estate to write verses in his praise. But Savaisinha was unhappy because inspite of all his good qualities, he was not developing as a *ksatriya.* He was averse to meat and wine and was totally opposed to any kind of violence. In *navaratra* on the occasion of the worship of goddess Durga in his house, when animal sacrifice was made, he used to leave the place, because he could not bear the sight of the blood of an innocent animal being shed for the appeasement of the goddess.

Savaisinha Ji wanted him to take interest in firing, riding and hunting etc., the traditional pursuits of a *ksatriya.* But none of these activities suited his temperament. He was interested in religious talks, the company of *sadhus*, the study of scriptures and the worship of deities.

Savaisinha Ji thought that marriage might turn his mind from these things and make him a true *ksatriya.* So he married him at the early age of seventeen. Although his wife was beautiful and he also had a son from her, she failed to wean him away from his religious mooring. On the other hand, his married life fanned the fire of *vairagya,* which had been slowly burning in his heart since long by exposing thoroughly the hollowness of the world and all its enjoyments.

Srimad Bhagavatam says, "He who is devoted to the Lord, the repository of supreme good, revels in the ocean of nectar. He cannot feel drawn towards the enjoyments either of this world or the next, which are to him like the turbid water of a muddy pool. " (Bhagavata, VI. 12. 22)

Manasinha was born with strong *samsakaras* of devotion. It was, therefore, natural that all worldly objects and enjoyments should appear to him as turbid. Being the eldest son of his father, he was the rightful heir to his rich estate. He had everything that the world could give him - power, wealth, health, pride of birth, respect, affection of parents, beautiful wife and son. But these appeared to him like live baits thrown by *maya* to entice and capture him and keep him bound to the world to suffer all its miseries and the fearful cycle of birth and death. His heart pined for the lotus feet of the Lord, which alone could give him eternal peace and happiness.

He decided to throw off the mantle of *maya* and proceed headlong on the path that lead to the desired goal. But he did not know how to proceed. He used to listen to the instructions of Śrī Narayana Svami, a saint who came to his mother, and Śrī Gopala Dasa Ji, a saint of Dadu *sampradaya*, to whom he often went. But they could not convince him. He had heard of Thakura Kusalasinha Ji, the Jagiradara of Gijagarha, who had become famous in Rajasthana as the most advanced devotee of his time amongst the Raj-puts. He lived in Jaipur. So one day Manasinha quietly slipped away from home and went to Jaipur. He met Kusalasinha Ji. After introducing himself he described his mental state and requested for guidance. Kusalasinha Ji said, "The reason why you are so disturbed and unhappy is that you have chosen the *jnana-marga*, (the path of knowledge), while your natural inclination is towards *bhakti.* Bhakti is the best and the easiest path for God realisation.

*anyasmat saulabhyam bhaktau*  
*(Narada-Bhaktisutra, 58)*

**"It is also the surest way for the attainment of peace, because it is of the very nature of peace and supreme joy. "**

*santi rupatpa ramananda rupacca*  
*(Narada-Bhaktisutra, 60)*

"What should I do for the attainment of bhakti? " inquired Manasinha.

"The centre of *bhakti* is Vrndavana. You should go there. "

"Whom should I meet and surrender to in Vrndavana? "

"You should meet Pandita Ramakrsna Dasa Baba, who is a *siddha-*saint. He is the very incarnation of *bhakti. '*

Manasinha did not lose time. Forthwith he started for Vrndavana. On reaching Vrndavana he stayed with a *sadhu* in Sahajahanpura- Ki-BagTcT, near Dau Ji-Ki Bagici (the present site of Vrndavana Research Institute), where Pandita Ramakrsna Dasa Baba used to live.

On account of humility he could not muster courage to go and meet Pandita Baba directly. Every morning he went and stood at a place from where he could see Pandita Baba going out of his cottage to ease nature. As he went along, Manasinha made obeisance to him. He nodded in reply, but never said anything. He did not even lift his head to see who he was. Three months passed like this. One day Pandita Baba cast a glance at him out of curiosity and asked, "Who are you? "

Manasinha introduced himself. Baba asked, "What for do you stand here everyday? "

"For your *darsana*", replied Manasinha.

Baba did not say anything further. One more month passed like this. Then Baba asked one day, "Where do you live? "

"In Shahajahanpura-Ki-Bagici, " replied Mana-sinha.

Mana-sinha.

Baba said, "At four o clock in the evening we have *katha* (religious discourse) in Dau-Ji s Bagici. You may come and attend. "

Manasinha started going.

Baba was impressed by his earnestness and enthusiasm in devotion. One day he said to him, "Why not come and live here under some tree? "

Manasinha, who had so far lived in despondency and gloom, saw a silver lining in the clouds. There was no end to his happiness. He had never imagined that Baba could be so merciful to him. He began to live in his *asrama* under a tree laden with creepers.

There were too many monkeys in the *bagici.* When Manasinha baked his bread under the tree, they used to be on the lookout for an opportunity to pounce and run away with it. Sometimes Pandita Baba himself stood near him with a stick in hand and said, "You bake your bread. I shall watch the monkeys. " This was too much for Manasinha. How could he bear the burden of Baba's mercy to that extent? But he had to. It was like having to swallow a pill that was bitter yet sweet.

Manasinha s parents were much distressed at his sudden disappearance from home. They organized an intensive search. In the end they came to Vrndavana and found him. They tried to persuade him to return home. But they had to go back disillusioned and disappointed.

After staying in Pandita Baba's *bagici* for eight months, Manasinha requested him for *diksa.* He advised him to take *diksa* from Śrī Madhava Dasa Baba Ji of Puchari. He took *diksa* from Madhava Dasa Baba Ji, but continued to regard Pandita Baba as his real *guru* and practise *bhajana* under his guidance. Pandita Baba also treated him as his disciple and showered all his affection upon him. He did not allow anyone even to touch his feet, but he allowed Manasinha Ji to massage his feet.

After *diksa* probably Manasinha Ji took *vesa* from Madhava Dasa Baba. But no one knows for certain, whether he actually took *vesa* and if so from whom, though he lived like a *vaisnava samnyasi.* He begged for his food and stitched his clothes from rags, mostly thrown away by people. For water he used an old tin container instead of *karava,* (an earthen pot, usually kept by *sadhus* in Vraja), because *karava* was likely to break and every time it broke a fresh effort had to be made for its procurement. People did not know his *samnyasa* name, but they called him Bhagata Ji.

His parents and others, who loved and respected him, often came to see him. When they saw him in this condition, there was no end to their grief. The contrast between his princely style of life at home and his present life as a recluse was too much for their tender heart to bear. A picture of Manasinha in princely dress with sword in his hand can even now be seen in Bhagavata Nivasa, an *asrama* in Ramanreti, Vrndavana, built by Śrī Krpasindhu Dasa Baba, the foremost disciple of Pandita Baba. Someone once asked Krpasindhu Baba why he had kept in his asrama the picture of Manasinha as the heir apparent of a big *jagiradara,* instead of his picture as a *sadhu.* He replied, "People would know Manasinha Ji better, if they know what he was before he became a *sadhu.* How many people, who roll in wealth and have every other thing, that a man can desire or expect from this world, renounce the world like him as a trifle or a trap laid out by *maya?"*

After some time Pandita Baba left the world. His separation became unbearable to Manasinha. He wept day and night and roamed about crying "Ha Baba! Ha Radhey!, Ha Baba! Ha Radhey! " like one, who had gone mad. His parents thought that he had actually gone mad. Forcibly they took him to Lorani for treatment. Manasinha gave up food and drink in protest. But his parents regarded this also as a phase of his madness. They kept the choicest fruits, sweets and other delicacies in his room to entice him. But he did not even look at them. For eighteen days he did not take even a morsel of food and became too weak. He said to his parents, "I will not eat anything until you send me back to Vrndavana. "

They were obliged to send him back to Vrndavana. But in Vrndavana they made elaborate arrangements for his stay in Dhiraja Lala Ki Bagici. They kept a cow and two servants for his service, which he did not like. He also did not like to live in Dhiraja Lala Ki Bagici, because other *sadhus* also lived there. He wanted to live in solitude. So he shifted to Bihari two servants followed him. Then he stopped taking cow s milk and started begging from a *ksetra* (a place where food is distributed free to the beggars). He begged two breads every day from the *ksetra.*

Finally in order to get rid of his people completely, he disappeared from the *bagici* and lived in hiding in some unknown place for six months. His people tried to search him, but in vain. He returned to Bihari Ji Ki Bagici, when the servants and the cow had been removed from there. When again his people came to see him, he told them frankly, You must not come to me now. Now I have no relationship with you. Your coming disturbs me in my *bhajana. "* They went back.

In the month of Magha they came again. This time Manasinha Ji asked his brother Madana, "Can you go to Mathura? "

"Yes, why not? " replied Madana.

"Then you go and bring for me *bahirvasa* (a sheet, worn as a lower garment), a *cadara* (a sheet, worn as an upper garment), and a mat. I want to go to Pandita Baba. But my clothes, which have been prepared from rags collected from different places, are impure. When I go to him my clothes should be pure. Besides, you know Phalguna, the month of Holi is approaching. I shall need scent and gulala\[91\]for Radharani. Kindly bring them too. "

Perhaps Manasinha Ji had received a call from Pandita Baba and was preparing to go to him in eternal Vrndavana. His people thought that he wanted to go to Pandita Baba s *samadhi* in Bhagavata Nivasa. They were both surprised and happy to find that he had for the first time asked for some service from them. This was an indication to them that his madness was gone and he would turn more and more towards them and accept more service from them.

Madana brought everything from Mathura. Manasinha asked him to keep them in an *almirah* in his room. Then he asked his people repeatedly to go back home. They went back, but left their servant RangiIa to look after him.

Two days after they had gone (in the year 1945), Manasinha Ji went to Radha-Vallabha s temple for *darsana* in the morning and saw *rasa-IiIa* in Uniya Baba s *asrama.* Then after bathing in Davanala-kunda he returned to *bagici.*

Just at that time came RangiIa. He asked RangiIa, "Do you know Bhagavata Nivasa? "

I do not know. But I can inquire and go. "

"Then you go. Tell Krpasindhu Dasa Baba that Bhagata Ji has asked for Pandita Baba s *prasada* and his *prasadi kanthi*

After RangiIa had gone, Manasinha Ji put on the new *bahirvasa* and lay down on the mat, covering himself with the new *cadara.*

When RangiIa returned, he was shocked to see Manasinha Ji lying on the mat with his eyes fixed above him in a particular direction, as if he was anxiously waiting for the arrival of some one dear to him. Tears incessantly flowed from his eyes, as if he was unable to bear even for a moment the delay in his arrival. The phial of scent and *gulala* kept by his side indicated that he was ready for his ceremonial welcome.

RangiIa said, "Radhey, Radhey! I have brought the *prasada. "*

Manasinha Ji extended his hand without turning his eyes from the direction in which they were fixed. RangiIa gave him the *prasada* and the *kanthi.* Manasinha Ji put a particle of the *prasada* in his mouth and the *kanthi* on his neck, with his eyes still fixed in the same direction.

RangiIa went running to Śrī Chajurama Ji, who lived in the *bagici* and said, Go and see what has happened to Bhagata Ji. If necessary please call a doctor. "

Chajurama Ji rushed to see Manasinha Ji. From what he saw, it did not appear to him that he was unwell. He took RangiIa outside and said, "Manasinha Ji seems to be meditating. You should not disturb him. Watch him from a distance. If necessary, call me again. "

Chajurama Ji went back. RangiIa returned to the cottage of Manasinha. While returning he heard him saying in a low voice Jai ho! " As if he was greeting someone. The next moment, when he reached the cottage, Manasinha Ji was not there. Where had he gone? He had gone in his *siddha manjari* *deha* (spiritual body as *manjari)* to transcendental Vrndavana. Pandita Baba had come in his *siddha manjari svarupa* to take him to the *nikunja* of Radharani to serve Radha and Krsna with scent and *gulala*, while they were engaged in the love pastime of Holi.

## Chapter 24 - Rajarsi Śrī Banamali Rajabahadura

Born in 1 863, Rajarsi Banamali Rajabahadura was the landlord of Tanasa in district Pabana of East Bengal. He was conferred the title of Raja by the British Government. He had inherited the religious *samsakaras* of his parents. While he was still in the womb, his father Śrī Gangaprasada Raya and his mother had gone on pilgrimage to Purl on foot. Not very long after his birth, the parents gave him in adoption to Raja Banavari Lala, the Landlord of Tanasa and went to Vrndavana for *bhajana.*

Banavari Lala died when Banamali Lala was only 17 years old. The burden of running the affairs of the big *zaminadari* of Tanasa fell upon his shoulders. Like a noble landlord, who had the well-being of his people very much at heart, he started constructing wells and rest houses and opened schools and hospitals in his estate. But his inner life from the very beginning was that of a *sadhaka.* He had come under the influence of Brahma *sampradaya* and was practising *sadhana* according to the teachings of the *sampradaya.*

The story of his life is mainly connected with the story of his family deity Śrī Radhavinoda Ji. Originally Radhavinoda Ji was found mysteriously by Śrī Vancharama Ji, the *adhikari* (managing authority) of Navagrama, a village in Tanasa estate. Vancharama was a very devoted person. Every day before his *seva-puja* he went to bathe in the river Karatoya in Navagrama. One day while he was bathing he heard someone saying in a sweet voice, "Take me out of water to your home. " Vancharama looked all around, but he could not discover the source of the voice. He went home after bathing, but the voice kept ringing in his ears. The next day, when he went to bathe, he again heard the same voice, but he could not locate where it came from. The third day, when he was going out of the river after bathing, he heard the voice again and simultaneously felt that something had clung to his feet. He brought it out. And lo! It was the beautiful idol of Radhavinoda. For some time he stood dazed and petrified. Then he hugged it and bathed it with the tears of his eyes. There was horripilation, shivering and sweating all over his body.

Radhavinoda alone knows how long and how intensely he had cherished the desire to be hugged by this devotee and to bathe in his tears of joy and love. He did not even hesitate to touch his feet for its fulfilment! Nothing strange for him, for he is the Lord of Love. He loves his devotees and love is blind.

Vancharama Ji brought him home. After due ceremony, he began to serve him as best as he could. But Vinoda Ji, as Vancharama called him, was very luxurious. There was no end to his demands. He would sometimes ask for sweets of different kinds, sometimes for new and fancy dresses, and sometimes for scent and other luxurious things. When Vancharama could not fulfil his demand, he either urged someone else or he himself brought the things he wanted somehow from somewhere, even though he had to steal or plunder.

Once Vinoda Ji wanted to eat vegetable prepared from mustard flowers. After sunset he went to the field of a farmer and began to pick mustard flowers. The farmer heard the sound of someone sacking the field. He shouted, "Who is there? " Vinoda Ji quickly tied the flowers in a corner of his *pitambara* and ran away. At night he said to Vancharama in a dream, "Since long I have not eaten vegetable prepared from mustard flowers. You offer it to me today. " Vancharama did not know where he could get mustard flowers, because There was no field within a couple of miles from his place, in which mustard was grow. He was filled with some anxiety. When he went to Vinoda Ji s temple for morning service, he was surprised to see some mustard flowers adorning both of his ears and some tied to a corner of his *pitambara.* He understood that Vinoda Ji had himself brought the flowers from somewhere so that he might not have to take the trouble of going out in search of them. He prepared vegetable from the flowers and offered it to him.

One does not know how Vinoda Ji got into the habit of smoking *hukka* (hubble-bubble). Possibly some devotee, who had served him before used to smoke. He was not conversant with the rules of ritualistic worship and did not know what should or should not be offered to the deity. He lovingly offered the *hukka* to Vinoda Ji before smoking and Vinoda Ji not only accepted the offering, but enjoyed it, because it was made in love.

Once Vinoda Ji asked a wealthy devotee in a dream to offer him a *hukka.* The devotee offered him a *hukka* with a long pipe and a silver mouth-piece. Vinoda Ji smoked every morning and evening after Raja-bhoga, (offering of principal meals). Some fortunate devotees could at times also hear the hubble-bubble of the *hukka.*

Vancharama was very hospitable. Whosoever came to his village for him his doors were always open. If he chose to stay for some time, he was well served. One night, when he and his wife were sleeping, three guests knocked at his door. Vinoda Ji did not want that his sleep should be disturbed. He went to the door in the form of his son and received the guests. He told them Pita Ji (my father) is asleep. I can awaken him, if you so desire, otherwise you may cook for yourselves. I shall arrange for the provisions and everything else. "

"No, you don t disturb his sleep. We shall cook for ourselves, " replied the guests. Vinoda Ji went to the shop from where Vancharama used to purchase provisions and said to the shop-keeper, Vancharama Ji has sent me to get some provisions from you on loan. Please give. "

"How can I give the provisions to a person like you, who is not known to me? I never saw or heard about you before, " replied the shop-keeper.

Then Vinoda Ji took out the gold-bangle he was wearing and said, "You keep this with you as surety and return it to Vancharama Ji after he has cleared the account. "

The shop-keeper agreed. Vinoda Ji went back home with the provisions and made all other arrangements for cooking. The guests cooked and ate and then went to sleep.

Next morning, when Vancharama saw the guests, he asked, "When did you come? Who opened the door? "

"You were asleep, when we came. Your son opened the door and made all arrangements for our dinner. We did not like to disturb you. "

Vancharama stood stunned for a while. Could this be the *IiIa* of Vinoda Ji? " He began to think. At noon, when the shop-keeper came with the bangle, the mystery was solved.

For long Vinoda Ji was without a consort. His loneliness began to weigh heavy upon him. Sportive as he was, he began to think of marriage. He decided to marry Raja Banavari Lala s 10 or 11 years old daughter, whose name was Radha. He directed one of his devotees to Raja Banavari Lala and speak about him in such a way that he is obliged to come for His *darsana.*

The devotee went and said to the Raja, "In village Navagrama of your own estate you own employee Vancharama has found a *murti* of Śrī Krsna. The deity is very much alive and sportive. Every day He surprises everybody by performing a new lila. Hundreds of people go to Him for *darsana.* Will you not go? "

"How did Vancharama find the deity? What *IiIas* does he perform? " asked Raja Banavari Lala out of curiosity. The devotee told him everything. He felt very much attracted. One day he went for the *darsana* of Vinoda Ji along with his wife and daughter Radha.

As they were looking at Vinoda Ji, Radha felt very much excited. She clasped her mother and said, "Look Ma, how Thakura is smiling at me! "

"Foolish! " said Ma, as she affectionately patted her on her cheek.

"No, Ma! He is really smiling and winking at me! "

But Ma ignored it as her childish fancy.

The sweet smiles of Vinoda Ji so captured the heart of Radha that she thought of Him day and night. Several times, on her insistence, Raja Sahib had to take her to Vinoda Ji with him for His *darsana.*

Slowly Raja Sahib also became more and more attracted towards Vinoda Ji. Once, when he had gone to see Him, Radha said to her father, "Pita Ji, let us take Vinoda Ji to our place. I have a strong desire that I should serve Him and decorate Him beautifully. "

Raja said, I also feel like taking Vinoda Ji away. But will Vancharama let us do so? " He looked at Vancharama, as he said this.

Vancharama, instead of acquiescing or denying directly, said, "Sir, I am your servant as well as Vinoda Ji s. How can I say that you should or should not take Him away. The matter lies between you and Vinoda Ji. "

At night Vinoda Ji said to vancharama in a dream, "You let me go with the Raja. I am pleased with you, but now I want to accept the service of the Raja. You need not be sorry, for you will soon realize me. "

What could Vancharama do? He sent the message to the Raja. The Raja was overwhelmed to know that Vinoda Ji was so kind to him. Immediately he went and conveyed the message to his wife and Radha. They began to dance with joy.

Elaborate arrangements were made to bring Vinoda Ji. On an auspicious day he was brought to the palace in a palanquin escorted by elephants and horses and musicians, performing music on different instruments.

Radha now served Vinoda Ji with all her heart and soul. There was, no doubt, a *pujari* to serve Him, but it was Radha**,** who decided each day what dress He would put on and what He would eat. She also prepared different kinds of ornament from flowers and decorated Him with her own hands. Vinoda Ji was very much pleased with her service. But He often teased her. He would sometimes, after eating, rub his dirty hands with her clothes, sometimes pinch her and sometimes even spit at her.

Radha s love towards Vinoda Ji went on increasing. So did Vinoda s love pranks. One day when Radha was offering Him a garland, He caught hold of her *ancala* (edge of *sari)* and said, "You must marry me. " She spoke about this to her mother, but mother did not believe.

After some time Radha fell ill. Vinoda Ji said to her mother in a dream, Radha will not survive. But you need not worry. She will die to live with me as my bride. There is a dry *devadara* tree in your garden. Out of the wood of that tree you make a *murti* of Radha and marry the *murti* to me.! "

Radha s mother was then convinced that Vinoda Ji really wanted to marry Radha. She told her husband about the dream. Both of them began to shed tears of sorrow as well as joy to think of this new lila of Vinoda Ji. They were sorry to know that Radha would not survive. But they were happy to know that Vinoda Ji would accept Radha as His bride and both the daughter and son-in-law would live with them in their house in the form of *murtis.*

The *devadara* tree was cut down and the work of making an idol out of it started. As soon as the *murti* was ready, Radha died. Arrangements for her funeral and her marriage in the form of the *murti* began to be made simultaneously. When the marriage was performed, Vinoda Ji began to be called Radha Vinoda. Even today Vinoda and Vinodini (Radha) can be seen living happily together in the Tanasa temple by the side of Ramakrsna Seva-asrama in Vrndavana. Even today their marriage anniversary is duly celebrated.

Life in Tanasa estate centred round Radha Vinoda till Raja Banavari Lala was alive. *Katha, kirtana* and festivals were the order of the day. But these activities slackened when the burden of running the affairs of the estate fell on the shoulders of Banamali Lala. The service of Radha-Vinoda was particularly neglected, because Banamali Lala was initiated in Brahma-dharama, which did not believe in *murti* worship. The *pujari* carried on the routine service of the deities. But on account of the negligent attitude of Banamali Raya, no one felt enthusiastic about it.

A sudden change came in the attitude of Banamali Raya, when one day he met Jagadbandhu Prabhu, the great saint of Pabana, who, on account of the golden lustre of his body and the intensity of his love for Radha and Krsna was regarded by some as the very incarnation of Śrī Caitanya Mahaprabhu. That day Raja Banamali Rajabahadura was going through the highway of Pabana in right royal fashion, mounted on a decorated elephant and escorted by gunmen and other attendants. From the other side was coming Jagadbandhu Prabhu, dancing in the midst of Kirtana-party, consisting of hundreds of devotees, who were also singing and dancing to the tune of a large number of *mrdangas* and *karatalas*

Banamali Rai got down from the elephant, walked barefooted up to the Kirtana-party and joined it. Like one who was hypnotised, he raised both of his hands and started dancing and singing with the party. He continued doing this till the party reached its destination and stopped *kirtana.* After *kirtana* he bowed down to Jagadbandhu Prabhu, requested him to grace Banavari Nagara once by his presence, and returned.

On return he felt that a new wave of *bhakti* was surging in his heart. He felt that travelling on the crest of that wave he had crossed the desert of *brahma-upasana* (meditating on brahman) and was standing at the threshold of an oasis, rich with the foliage and fountains of *bhakti.*

He found entrance into the oasis, when Jagadbandhu Prabhu came to his house, and staying with him for a number of days, gave him instructions in Raganuga Bhakti. Then he understood that the Lord was not only an object of reverence, worship or prayer as Brahma-samaja had taught him, but also of love or *priti. Priti* was not one-sided. It was not that the devotee alone loved the Lord, the Lord also loved the devotee. In fact, He loved the devotee more than the devotee loved Him, and nothing was more valuable to Him than the loving service of His devotee. Because the devotee in his fallen state had no access to Him, he Himself came down in the form of Śrī Murti to receive his service.

Banamali Ray s mind now turned towards Radha Vinoda. He made the best possible arrangements for His service. But he discontinued the service of the *hukka,* because it was not permitted by the *sastras.*

During those days, Śrī Krsnasundara Raya Prabhu, who was *a siddha mahatma*, lived with Banamali Raya. He also used to smoke *hukka.* Before smoking, he offered the *hukka* to Radha Vinoda. Raya Prabhu was always lost in lila*-smarana.* At the time of smoking also his *lila-smarana* was his primary occupation, smoking was secondary and it went on automatically as a matter of habit, though it slowed down, when the *smarana* was deep, and he put the pipe into his mouth after long intervals. During the intervals Radha Vinoda put the pipe in his mouth and smoked more freely. Thus He was not very much affected, when Banamali Raya stopped His *hukka.*

On the twelfth day of Krsna-paksa in the year 1892 Raya Prabhu chose to quit this world. For four days since then Radha Vinoda had to practise forced abstinence and go without *hukka.* But abstinence is not in His nature. On the *amavasya* day, after the morning service, when the *pujari* was doing *japa*, he sank into drowsiness. In that state Radha Vinoda said to him Give me my *hukka.* Since these people discontinued my *hukka* service, I used to go and smoke with Raya Prabhu. But now that Raya Prabhu is no more, I have not smoked for four days.

In the evening, when Banamali Raya went to the temple, the *pujari* told him every thing. Banamali Raya made necessary arrangements for the service of *hukka*, but the doubt persisted in his mind as to whether Vinoda Ji really smoked.

Once Jagadbandhu Prabhu was staying in Banamali Ray s palace in a room adjacent to the temple of Radha Vinoda. After Raja-bhoga he called Banamali Raya and said, Let us today enjoy the hukka-smoking lila of LiIamaya (the sportive Lord). He took Banamali Raya with him and sat in the veranda before the temple. After sometime he said, "See, Vinoda Ji is smoking. Listen to the hubble-bubble of the *hukka. "* Simultaneously, Jagadbandhu gave Banamali Raya the spiritual ears, with which he could hear the sound of the *hukka.* As he was listening to the sound, he went into *bhava-samadhi* and tears began to trickle down his eyes.

Now Banamali Raya s faith in the spiritual nature of Śrī Murti became so firm that if any one said or did anything, which implied that the Śrī Murti was a mere statue, it hurt him. He now became restless to render personal service to Radha Vinoda. But he was not qualified for service without proper initiation. So in 1897 he went to Vrndavana and took initiation from Śrī Radhika Natha Gosvami Prabhu, a descendant of Advaitacarya Prabhu.

He got so much absorbed in the service of Śrī Radha Vinoda that it became impossible for him to do anything else. Fortunately he had a very capable and trustworthy manager in Śrī Kamini Kumara Ghosa. He entrusted to him the entire management of the estate so that he could devote all his time to the loving service of Radha Vinoda.

After some time he went to Vrndavana and constructed two buildings, one in Vrndavana and the other in Radhakunda. These are now known as Tanasa Mandira and Rajabani. He lived with Radha Vinoda sometimes in Vrndavana and sometimes in Radhakunda.

Ever since Banamali Raya came to Vraja, he was the central figure of the *vaisnava* community in Vraja. Sadhus and *vaisnavas* always graced his house with their presence, because *katha*, *kirtana* and festivals were always going on there. He utilized all the resources of his estate in the service of the *vaisnavas.* He built a hospital for free treatment of *sadhus, vaisnavas* and the Vrajabasis. He started a school for teaching *bhakti-sastras* to the students and arranged for their free board and lodging. He also started a press, in which he printed Srimad Bhagavatam with eight commentaries and other *bhakti* literature, which he distributed free amongst the *sadhus* and the *vaisnavas.*

In his time there were a galaxy of *siddha mahatmas* in Vraja. He was benefited by the company of each one of them. But there were some to whom he was specially attached and who freely showered their blessings upon him. They were Śrī Radhikanatha Gosvami Prabhu, Jagadbandhu Prabhu, Śrī Radharamana Carana Dasa Baba Ji Maharaja, Śrī Vijayakrsna Gosvami, Śrī Gaurakisora Siromani, Śrī Ramahari Dasa Baba Ji, Śrī Krsnasundara Raya Prabhu, Śrī Harisundara Bhaumika Bhuiyan, Śrī Jagadisa Dasa Baba and Śrī Ramakrsna Dasa Pandita Baba.

With the blessings of these *mahatmas* he easily attained the lotus feet and the loving service of Radha Vinoda in His eternal *dhama* Vrndavana.

## Chapter 25 - Śrī Krsnacaitanya Dasa Baba

Śrī Krsnacaitanya Dasa Baba, whose family name was Asvani Kumara, was born in 1861 in Candana Nagara in West Bengal. His father Śrī Vrajakumara Nanda was a contractor in Calcutta.

Although Asvani Kumara was the son of a wealthy man and had all the opportunities for worldly enjoyment, the world had little attraction for him. From his very childhood a divine spark stirred his heart. He yearned for something higher and more sublime than anything the world could offer and was always lost in contemplation.

This caused anxiety to his father. He did two things to make him less contemplative. Immediately after he had passed the Entrance Examination, he put him in business and married him.

In due course Asvani became the father of five children. He seemed to have been completely surrounded by Maya; but the divine spark in him did not die. It continued to remind him that there was a higher purpose and destiny for him. He took *diksa* from his family *guru* and began to lead a life, which looked worldly from outside, but was spiritually well disciplined. He was most regular in his morning bath in the Ganga, *patha*, *kirtana* and *japa.* The result was that the spark of *bhakti* slowly developed into a volcano and it became impossible for him to remain a *grhastha* (householder). He decided to renounce the world and devote himself wholly to *bhajana.*

He sought permission of his father to go to Puri on the occasion of Ratha-yatra for *darsana*, but instead of returning home from Puri, he went on pilgrimage to the South on foot. He walked 20 miles everyday doing *japa.* Until the required number of rounds of *japa* was completed he did not rest. After completing *japa* he bathed and performed his daily routine of *puja* and then went out for *bhiksa.* He went for *bhiksa* to only seven places and ate whatever he got from them. Sometimes he did not get anything and had to go without food. He had to face much trial and tribulation, but he never transgressed the rules of spiritual discipline he had set for himself during the pilgrimage.

He went from Puri to Godavari, Sivakanci, Visnukanci, Bala Ji, Śrī Rangama, Pancatirtha and Ramesvara. From Ramesvara he went to Nasika, from Nasika to Dvaraka, Puskara, Jaipura and Karauli and from Karauli to Vrndavana. The whole journey took him three years.

He wanted to settle in Vrndavana for *bhajana.* But one night he saw a dream, in which some divine being told him, "The time for your Vrndavana-vasa has not yet come. You have still to perform some *karma. "* He did not heed the advice, because he was determined to stay here till the end and do *bhajana.* But again he saw the same dream and heard the same advice, which seemed to come as a command. He felt compelled to obey the command. He went back to Calcutta. His parents, wife and children were happy to see him back after a long period of anxiety and suspense.

Asvani Kumara now started his own business. He worked hard and made good fortune. But during this period also his *bhajana* went on undisturbed and with the usual zest and enthusiasm. He used to go to bed at nine and get up at one o clock, when he sat down for *bhajana.* Again after his morning bath in the Ganga at 5 o clock, he went through his daily routine of *puja, patha, japa* and *kirtana.* Then he cooked for himself, because he believed that food cooked by one s own self was more conducive to *bhajana.* His wife and children helped him and took care to see that nothing happened to disturb his *bhajana.* Thus his days passed on happily.

But all along he felt uneasy for want of one thing. In Vrndavana he had seen two pictures of Nitai-Gaura and Radha-Govinda, which had captured his heart. He had been trying to get those pictures but had failed. He often passed sleepless nights on account of intense feeling of separation he experienced in the absence of the pictures. One night he wept and said to Nitai complainingly, "Nitai, you are known as the saviour and the benefactor of the fallen. Am I so fallen that you do not consider me worthy of your *darsana,* along with that of Gaura and Radha-Govinda even, in the form of pictures? Then why not wipe off all trace of my worthless existence on earth? " The same night he saw in a dream that he had obtained those pictures from a shop in Citpura road in Calcutta. In the morning he went to Citpura road and was surprised to see a shop exactly like the one he had seen in the dream. When he went inside the shop he was happy to find a picture of Nitai-Gaura exactly like the one he had seen in Vrndavana. He looked round to see if the picture of Radha-Govinda also was there. But he could not see it anywhere. Suddenly his eyes fell on an almirah, which he had seen in his dream. He asked the shop-keeper to see if the picture of Radha-Govinda was in that almirah. But the shop-keeper said, "There are many other things in the almirah, but no pictures.

Asvani Kumar s heart was broken and his eyes were filled with tears. Suddenly the shop-keeper was reminded by some unseen power, as it were, that some time before he had received a packet of pictures, which he had kept in that almirah and had forgotten. He brought the packet out. As soon as he opened it, Asvani Kumara saw a picture of Radha-Govinda exactly like the one he had seen in Vrndavana. "Here is the picture! ", he shouted, as he jumped with joy. He bought both the pictures. They were not for him simply pictures, but Nitai-Gaura and Radha-Govinda themselves, who had come to him in response to his prayers.

Four years had now elapsed since Asvani Kumara came back from Vrndavana. His heart again began to pine for Vrndavana. Since his business was now well established and his sons were grown up and fully capable of looking after themselves and the business, he thought that the *karma*, which had remained to be fulfilled, was now done and he could finally renounce the world and go to Vrndavana. His parents, wife and children gave him their consent to do so, because he told them that he could not survive longer if he stayed out of Vrndavana. He thought that it was due to the mercy of Radha-Govinda that his going back to Vrndavana was facilitated, because they had come to him from Vrndavana to take him there along with them.

The mercy of Radha-Govinda did not stop here. They had come to him in the form of pictures, because he had so desired. But they wanted to be with him in the form of deities, so that they could enjoy his *prema-seva* (loving service) more than in the form of pictures. So, when he reached Vrndavana, they appeared to him in a dream and asked him to worship them in the form of deities. They also told him about the place, where they were waiting for him in that form. He brought the deities from there, built a temple for them and duly installed them in it after *prana-pratista* (the rite of bringing life to an idol).

After *prana-pratistha* Govinda Ji got his new name, Gopijanavallabha. Asvani-Kumara began to serve Gopijanavallabha with love and devotion.

After some time he took *samnyasa diksa* from Śrī Haricarana Dasa Baba Ji, a disciple of Śrī Gaurakisora Siromani and was named Śrī Krsnacaitanya Dasa. Haricarana Dasa Baba advised him to go and do *bhajana* in some solitary place in Vraja. At this time his son Gaura also came to live in Vrndavana. Śrī Krsnacaitanya Dasa Baba entrusted the service of Gopijanavallabha to him and went to Kusumasarovara. He practised *bhajana* at Kusumasarovara for some time and then went to Radhakunda.

While staying in Radhakunda his mind was always with Gopijanavallabha. If there was any default or delinquency in the service of Gopijanavallabha, he always came to know about it. Once he called Gaura from Vrndavana and told him, "You do not pay proper attention to the *seva* of Gopijanavallabha.
