In 1948 Krsnaprema set out on pilgrimage to the South. He visited Maharsi Ramana s Asrama in Tiruvannamalai, where he had a typical and revealing experience.

When he entered the hall, where the Maharsi reclined daily on his coach, he sat down in silence, along with the others to meditate at his feel. But as soon as he sat down, he heard a voice, questioning him again and again: "Who are you? Who are you? Who are you? He tried hard to ignore it, but it went on and on like an importunate visitor, knocking at the door. So in the end he just had to formulate an answer: "I am Krsna's servant. " At once the question changed into "Who is Krsna? He answered: "Nanda s son. " But the questioning went on pauselessly. He thought of other answers like He is an Avatara, the One in all, the Resident of every heart" and so on... But the questioning would not cease, till at last he gave it up, left the hall and returned, deeply disturbed, to meditate. But he had no peace. The voice gave him no respite, till, in the end, he had to evoke Radharani.

Radharani revealed the answer to him. She said: "Nothing exists besides Krsna. So how can He be described? You can only say that Krsna is Krsna! "

Next morning, when Krsnaprema sat down again at Maharsi s feet, the Maharsi gave him a lightening glance and smiled. He understood that he was behind all that questioning. Then as he closed his eyes to meditate, a delectable peace descended upon him. He put a question to the Maharsi in silence: "And who are you, may I humbly ask? " As he put this question, he had to open his eyes involuntarily to see that the coach was empty. He closed his eyes once again and opened the next moment to see that he was seated on the coach as before. Obviously his disappearance implied that he was beyond *nama-rupa* (name and form) and this was the only way in which he could answer the question from his point of view and realization as a *jnani.*

The silent conversation, that took place between the two great souls, highlights the distinction between the *jnani* and the *bhakta.* The *jnani* swims in an ocean of negative peace and tranquility, which is empty, colourless and motionless, the *bhakta* swims freely in an ocean of love and bliss that throbs with life and sports of the great Lord, the concentrated form of all beauty, love and goodness.

From Tiruvannamalai Krsnaprema went to Srirangama. He had a wonderful experience in the Srirangama temple. As soon as he prostrated himself before the Lord s image, he lost outer consciousness and saw a vast ocean made of liquid light. Then there was a ripple in the ocean and countless white lotuses erupted on its blue waves, one after another and on each flower stood a lovely Krsna with Radha, Radha smiling and Krsna playing His magic flute! Krsnaprema shivered and tears flowed from his eyes as he saw this.

Krsnaprema once said, "If we want to reach the goal, the Eternal, we have to steer the boat straight out and across to the other shore. " His boat had now reached very near the other shore. On November 14, 1965 it reached the destination, the Eternal Vrndavana, where Radha and Krsna must have been waiting impatiently to give him a warm welcome.

Krsnaprema had many Western admirers and disciples, but the number of Indians, who lighted their torches at his flame, was much greater. There are many Indian *gurus,* at whose feet we can see sitting many foreign disciples, but he was, perhaps, the only foreigner of his time, at whose feet we could see sitting many Indian disciples. By imbibing the teaching of the great *rsis* of India and living according to it he had reawakened and intensified in them the awareness of their own spiritual inheritance. His three books *"Search for Truth, " "Yoga of Bhagavat-gita"* and  *Yoga of Kathopanisad, "* still draw discerning seekers, who feel the need for guidance in their search for the Eternal and the Blissful.

Krsnaprema emphasized that there are two things necessary for reaching the Goal unflinching faith and self-surrender at the feet of Śrī Guru and the Lord. He defined faith as "the light which the higher personality sends to the lower. " It is one s firm conviction about the Eternal, which keeps the flame of aspiration alive amidst the darkness of doubt and despair. He wrote "I keep a whole collection of doubts, grow them in fact like mustard and cress, and when they are ripe, I eat them up. "

He defined self-surrender *(saranagati)* as "the offering of the mortal *(ahuti)* in the pure flame of the immortal. " Self-offering must be total and unconditional. It involves, "the staking of everything that does not matter for the One Thing that matters and the complete replacement of the ego by Krsna s will. "

## Chapter 29 - Śrī Gauragovinda Dasa Baba Ji

Gauragovinda Dasa Baba Ji, whose original name was Gurudasa, was born in 1879 in Dvaribena, a village near Pichalada in district Medinipura. His father s name was Hariprasada Bera. While still a child, he began to exhibit signs of greatness. Unlike the other children, he was quiet, seclusive and meditative. He was interested more in *kirtana* and study than in play. He surprised everybody by mastering the Sanskrita grammar called Mugdhabodha at the early age of eleven. He won the hearts of the uneducated village-folk by reading out to them, Srimad Bhagavatam and Śrī Caitanyacaritamrta every day.

He loved very much the company of Śrī Madhusudana Dasa, a highly devoted Baba Ji, who practised *sadhana* in a cottage made of straw and reeds near his home, and whom he regarded as his only friend, philosopher and guide. Madhusudana Dasa impressed upon him the necessity of *bhajana* and the importance of Harinama. He said, "In this age of Kali, *Harinama* and *Harinama* alone is the means by which Krsna may be realized"

*harernama, harernama, harernamaiva kevalam kalau nastyeva, nastyeva, nastyeva gatirnyatha*

"There is no difference between Krsna and His name. Unlike the names of other things, the name of Krsna, like Krsna Himself, is qualified by Truth, Consciousness and Bliss *(sat-cit-ananda).* It is as powerful and merciful as Krsna. It removes the impurities of the heart and makes it fit for the realization of Krsna and His Lila. It is both means *(sadhana)* and end *(sadhya).* If you take shelter under Harinama, it will protect you from all ills and pave the way for the supreme realization. "

Gurudasa took the advice of Madhusudana Dasa Baba to heart. He began to chant Harinama. He would retire to some lonely place and chant Harinama for hours or collect the boys of the village and perform *kirtana* with them. Sometimes he lost consciousness in *kirtana.* This worried his father very much, who apprehended that he might renounce the world altogether and become a recluse. In the hope that a pilgrimage may bring about a change in him, he sent him out on pilgrimage with Madhusudana Dasa Baba.

Both of them visited all the holy places in Gauramandala including Ambika Kalana, where Gauri Dasa Pandita, a close associate of Śrī Caitanya Mahaprabhu, worshipped the deities Nitai-Gaura, presented to him by Nitai-Gaura themselves. There Gurudasa took *mantra-diksa* (initiation) from Sripada Akhilacandra Gosvami, a descendant of Gauridasa. Gurudasa returned home after a year.

Pilgrimage did bring about a change in Gurudasa, but not the kind of change his parents had desired. During the pilgrimage, he had met Śrī Gaurakisora Dasa Baba Ji, who had advised him to renounce everything like Rupa and Sanatana and go to Vrndavana. Therefore the fire of renunciation was constantly burning within him. To add fuel to the fire, he was deprived of the company of Madhusudana Dasa Baba Ji, who had now gone to Vrndavana to pass the rest of his life there.

His father thought that the only way of cooling the fire was to marry him. So he lost no time in arranging his marriage. On the night of marriage Gurudasa seemed to stand at cross-road. He had to decide immediately whether he should tread the apparently rosy path of married life or the thorny path of renunciation. His mother had died only a short time before and she had asked him to marry. He had to decide whether he should act according to the wish of his mother or follow the advice of Gaurakisora Dasa Baba Ji. He decided to renounce. At night he quietly went out of home and ran towards Navadvipa to take *vesa (vaisnava samnyasa)* from Gaurakisora Dasa Baba. In Navadvipa he came to know that Gaurakisora Baba did not give *vesa* to anyone. Therefore he took *vesa* from the *mahanta* of the Bana Akhana of Navadvipa and was named Gauragovinda Dasa.

He then went to Śrī Gaurakisora Dasa Baba for precept. Baba instructed him as follows:

1\. Maintain the dignity of your *vesa* like Rupa and Sanatana.

2\. Regard money as your enemy.

3\. Keep away from women. Remember how Mahaprabhu had punished Chota Haridasa for going even to an old lady for *bhiksa.*

4\. Practise *sadhana* under the guidance of a Mahabhagavata or a person who has realized Krsna.

5\. Do not develop intimacy with worldly people.

6\. Live on *madhukari* obtained from the Vrajabasis.

In 1900 Gauragovinda Dasa went to Vrndavana. For some time he kept wandering in Vrndavana, seeing the beautiful spots connected with the divine sports *(lila)* of Śrī Krsna and chanting Harinama. He ate what he got in *madhukari* from the Vrajabasis and slept under the trees.

After some time he began to live in the Samajabani of Syamasundara temple in Vrndavana. Here he did *bhajana* under the guidance of Siddha Śrī Jagadisa Dasa Baba of Kaliyadaha. In the evening he read out Caitanya Bhagavata to him in his cottage. All the rest of the time he passed in chanting Harinama in his cottage.

He found that some vaisnavas in Vrndavana meditated upon Krsna-IiIa according to the *gutika* of *siddha* Krsnadasa Baba of Govardhana. He also began to do the same. But while thus meditating upon Krsna-IiIa, he could not complete his *japa.* He did not know whether he should give up meditation or *japa.* He sought the advice of Jagadisa Dasa Baba. Baba advised him to first complete his *japa* and then meditate upon *Iila,* if he had any time left after that. He had to give up meditation, because the *japa-*figure he had assigned for himself was so large that he had hardly any time left for meditation after *japa.*

After some time the reputation of Gauragovinda Dasa as a saint spread all over Vraja and people began to come to him for *darsana.* This caused much disturbance in his *bhajana.* Therefore he went to Ari, a village about four miles away from Govardhana and began to live there. Every day he went to Govardhana from Ari and returned after performing the *parikrama* of Govardhana. Thus he walked 20 miles every day chanting Harinama.

His reputation, however, kept on chasing him. The increasing crowd of his admirers did not let him live in peace even in Ari. He had, therefore, to flee from Ari and live in a dilapidated house near Killolakunda at some distance to the north-east of Ari. His *bhajana* and *parikrama* of Giriraja continued as before. As a natural result of prolonged *bhajana* some spiritual powers automatically developed in him. He could easily read the past and future of a person. Once he took pity on a Vrajabasi in grief and consoled him by telling him many things about his past and future. From that day there was a decline in his humility and *bhajana.* In anxiety he went to Pandita Ramakrsna Baba for advice. It was by the mercy and advice of Pandita Baba that he succeeded in disengaging himself from the spiritual powers that had blocked his way in *bhajana.*

By way of giving him a warning for future, Pandita Baba said, "*Harinama* is like a *kalpataru* (a tree which yields to everyone s desires). It gives to the *sadhaka* whatever he wants. If while doing *nama-japa* a *sadhaka* thinks of the powers of Krsna, it generates those powers in him; if he thinks of His love *(prema),* it instils his heart with love for Krsna. It is through the eyes of love that he enjoys the exuberant sweetness *(madhurya)* of His divine *IiIa.* Without love one may realize Krsna, but not His *madhurya.* Realization of Krsna without love generates powers like omniscience which block one s spiritual development.

Since then Gauragovinda Dasa began to join the assembly of *sadhus,* which used to be held every evening for religious talks somewhere in Govardhana under the natural leadership of Pandita Baba. One day the assembly was held in the courtyard of Nrsinha Ji s temple. As soon as the assembly was over, it began to rain. Gauragovinda Dasa said, It would have been better if the rain started a little later, when each one of us had returned to his *kuti*. Pandita Baba snubbed him by saying, "What? Self-seeking! You cannot realize Krsna so long as you have the slightest desire for your own happiness. You must give up the desire for your own happiness or well-being in this world as well as in the celestial world and desire only the happiness of Krsna, if you want to realize Him. "

On the advice of Pandita Baba, Gauragovinda Dasa began to do *bhajana* in a *kuti* near Apsara-kunda in Puchari. This was a lonely place. No one lived here except him and another *mahatma* in the form of a *bela* (wood-apple) tree in front of his *kuti,* who conversed with him on topics regarding Krsna and His lila*.* When the *bela-tree* first bore fruit, it appeared before him in human form and said, "You have watered and made me grow under your care since my infancy. I have a request to you. When my fruits ripen, you pluck and distribute them amongst the temples and the *mahatmas* of Vraja for the service of Krsna. " Gauragovinda Dasa did likewise.

One day someone came and hung his clothes over the tree. The tree asked Gauragovinda Dasa not to allow anyone to hang his clothes over it, because that disturbed its *bhajana.*

Gauragovinda Baba continued to make steady progress in *bhajana.* He could now repeat Harinama 8 lacs of times a day. For him day and night made no difference. He neither woke nor slept. In a half-conscious state he was always doing *japa.* While doing *japa* he was often overwhelmed with *bhava* (emotion) and danced and wept.

In Adi-purana Krsna says to Arjuna:

*gitva ca mama namani nartyena mama sannidhau*  
*idam bravimi te satyam kritoaham tena carjuna*  
*gitva ca mama namani rudanti mama sannidhau*  
*tesamaham parikrito nanyakrito janardanah*

**"Listen O Arjuna! Those who dance or weep before me while chanting My name, I am sold out to them, as I am not sold to anyone else. "**

Gauragovinda Baba had nothing to do except chanting Harinama and dancing and weeping before Śrī Hari. Therefore, it was natural that Śrī Hari should have been sold out to him. The extent to which He was sold out to him would appear from an interesting episode.

Once Baba returned late to his *kuti* after *madhukari* due to rain. He was very hungry. Therefore he forgot to offer the *madhukari* to Krsna before eating. He was swallowing the first morsel, when suddenly he realized that he was eating unoffered food. He held his throat to prevent swallowing it, but it went down. Then there was no end to his self-condemnation. His hunger disappeared and the *madhukari* lay before him untouched. At that time a sweet voice coming from inside his cottage rang into his ears: "Every day you offer me the remains of the food of Vrajabasis. Will heavens fall if today you offer me the remains of your own food? Come, offer now! Do not hesitate. "

Gauragovinda was thrilled to the core! A shiver went through his body, hair stood at their ends and tears flowed from his eyes. Hesitatingly he offered to Krsna the remains of his own food and then ate it himself. The taste of the *madhukari* he ate that day was exceptionally delightful and ravishing. It was bound to be, because that day Krsna Himself had eaten the offering with greater relish. Has anyone ever heard of a God as loving and loveable as Śrī Krsna of Vraja?!

In 1959 Gauragovinda Dasa left his physical body to join the divine *IiIa* of Śrī Krsna. His *samadhi* exists near the Apsarakunda.

## Chapter 30 - Śrī Sacinandana Dasa Baba

Śrī Sacinandana Dasa Baba was 110 years old in December 1972, when I went to see him with Śrī Rama Jivana Dasa Baba Ji of Barasana, who was well known to him. Baba lived in an old dilapidated *chatari* (room containing someone s *samadhi*) near *smasana-bhumi* (place for burning the dead) on the bank of Bhanusarovara in Barasana. As we entered the *chatari,* we saw Baba sitting square-legged with his back resting against the wall in a state of deep meditation. His lips were moving slowly and a resonant sound was coming out of his throat. Rama Jivana Baba told me that he was chanting "Rai, Rai" (Radharani s nick-name). The chanting had become habitual with him and it went on automatically even in the state of deep meditation.

Baba had lost his eyesight, but his audition was intact. Still he could not hear Rama Jivana Baba talking, because he had no outer consciousness.

On the craggy floor of the *chatari* with pits here and there were kept on one side an earthen pitcher with water, an earthen *karava* and an earthen plate. There was a piece of gunny-cloth on which Baba was sitting, and a *gudani* or a quilt made of rags, half of which was hanging from his left shoulder and half lying on the floor. Baba was bare-bodied with only a loin-cloth round his waist. There was nothing else in the *chatari.* The severe cold of the month of December did not seem to be a matter of slightest concern to the Baba.

Baba did not easily give *diksa* to anyone. He had only four disciples, of whom Anuragi Baba was the foremost. He did not allow his disciples to live with him. However, Anuragi Baba came and stayed with him occasionally but at this time Baba was all alone.

We sat down and began to wait for some sign of Baba s returning to the outer world. But for a long time there was no such sign. Baba was sitting all along like a statue. This was his normal condition. He used to be generally in the inner world, the transcendental world of the *IiIa* of Radha-Krsna and came out of it only casually, specially when compelled by the needs of the body. Even while attending to the needs of the body he used to be sometimes lost in meditation, and the bodily activity like eating or easing nature either continued automatically as a matter of habit or slackened or even stopped and remained suspended for hours.

After we had waited till late in the evening, Rama Jivana Baba shouted "Radhey, Radhey! " After he had shouted several times, Baba said in a law voice, "who? , as if he was speaking from a long distance.

Rama Jivana Baba said, "I, Rama Jivana. " Then introducing me to him, he said, "He has come from Vrndavana for your *darsana.* Kindly show mercy on him. "

Baba was quiet for sometime. Then he began to sing a song, which described Krsna-Balarama returning from the forest at dusk with the cows and the cowherds. Krsna was playing on the flute and the cowherds were blowing horns and dancing, while the milkmaids of Vraja, overwhelmed with joy and emotion at the sight of Krsna, sang propitious songs from the balconies. He was thus describing what he had just seen.

Baba was a good singer and his voice even at this age was sweet. As he sang, it did not appear that it was an old Baba Ji, who was singing, but a milkmaid of Vraja, who was so overwhelmed at the sight of Krsna that she could not but pour her heart out in the form of the song.

As the song was over, Baba again sank into deep meditation. We quietly came out of the room, leaving Baba alone to relish the home-coming of Krsna.

Very little is known about the early life of Sacinandana Dasa Baba. He was born in Bengal, but exactly in which place and when no one knows. He once told Anuragi Baba that since his very childhood he had great liking for *katha* and *kirtana.* Even if *katha* or *kirtana* was held at a place ten miles away from his home and at night, he did not hesitate to go there without informing his people. He stayed there throughout the night and returned next morning. When he was reprimanded and punished for this, he prayed for deliverance from the bonds of the family. His prayer was heard and he was given necessary strength to cut asunder the bonds.

One night he slicked away from home. After remaining on pilgrimage to the different holy places in the country for about two years, he went to Vrndavana. He had already taken *diksa* from someone. On reaching Vrndavana he took *vesa* from *siddha* Ramahari Dasa Baba, a disciple of *siddha* Jagannatha Dasa Baba. For some time he wandered here and there in Vraja. Finally he came to the *chatari,* where he had been living for over eighty years, when I first met him with Rama Jivana Baba.

Anuragi Baba says that once, when he was staying with Baba, there was unprecedented rainfall, on account of which the area around the *chatari* was flooded and it became very difficult to move in and out of the *chatari.* He requested Baba to shift to some other place, specially because snakes and other poisonous creatures were likely to creep into the *chatari* to save themselves from the flood. But Baba refused to shift. At night, when it was still raining heavily, Baba was stung by a scorpion on his finger. There was excruciating pain. Anuragi Baba tied his finger with a string so that the poison might not spread and then became restless to go out in search of some other treatment. But it was not possible to go out on account of torrential rain. Baba said to him, "Do not bother. I will soon be absorbed in *IiIa.* I shall then be unaware of the pain. " This is what actually happened. Next morning, when Anuragi Baba inquired about the pain, Baba had forgotten all about it.

Baba used to serve Radharani, the deity in the temple of Barasana, in various ways. He picked flowers from various places and weaved garlands for her. Sometimes when there was utter want of flowers in Barasana, he went on foot to Vrndavana at night and returned next morning with flowers. He weaved the flowers into garlands and offered them to Radharani. During the Holi festival he used to be busy all day in cleaning the passage from Bhanusarovara to the temple of Radharani.

Once he saw beautiful flowers blooming in a garden near Kosi He desired to weave them into garlands for Radharani. He asked for the owner s permission to pick them. But the owner refused permission. Then at night he slept in the verandah of an empty house near the garden. At midnight some thieves gathered near the house with a view to commit theft somewhere. They saw Baba sleeping in the verandah. Their chief gave him a jolt and asked, "Who are you? "

"A thief", replied Baba

"Thief? Where are you going to commit theft? "

"In this garden. "

What is there in the garden? "

"Flowers.

"Will the flowers bake your bread? "

"They will. "

"How? You will hardly get anything by selling them"

"I will not sell them. I shall weave them into garlands for Radharani. Radharani will be pleased. Her pleasure will bring about my deliverance from the bondage of *maya* and all my needs will be fulfilled for all times. " The thieves were impressed by Baba. They said, "Very well Baba. Let us all go. We shall join you in the theft.

Baba stole heaps of flowers with their help. They also helped him in weaving garlands and different kinds of ornament for Radharani. Stealing for Radharani made the thieves forget stealing for themselves. They became devotees of Radharani and often visited Baba in his *chatari* for his company and guidance.

Baba used to do *bhajana* in his *chatari* throughout the day. In the evening he went out for *madhukari.* Sometimes he got more *roties* in *madhukari* than he could eat. He did not throw them away, nor gave them to someone. He dried them so that he might eat the dried *roties*, when, on account of his absorption in *IiIa,* he did not feel like going out for *madhukari.* Later, when he lost his teeth and it was no more possible to eat the dried *roties*, he used to grind them into powder and make pills by mixing the powder with water. He used to swallow the pills. If the pills did not taste well or did not satisfy his hunger, he started chanting Rai, Rai. " He relished the name so much that he forgot all about the taste, hunger and thirst. He often used to sing the following Bengali song, which describes this potency of the name to conquer thirst and hunger:

*dhuri khela ara khelibo na,*  
*hariname mana menje che*  
*mayera kache ara jabo na,*  
*ksudha pele ara khabo na*  
*Rai - Rai amara ksudha-trsna*  
*saba hare che*

**"No more shall I play with dust,for I have now begun to relish Harinama. Neither shall I go to my mother when hungry, nor eat, for the name "Rai" has conquered my hunger and thirst. "**

Baba always used to chant "Rai" and be absorbed in the meditation of the Astakalina Lila of Radha-Krsna. After some time when his absorption in the Name and the *IiIa* became very deep, he stopped going for *madhukari.* He remained seated on his *asana* in deep meditation like a statue. For the tourists, who came to Barasana, he was a marvel. For the pilgrims he was an object of worship. The *pandas* took them to him, just as they took them to the different images of gods and goddesses for *darsana* in their own interest. The pilgrims bowed down to Baba, just as they bowed down to the other statues, made some offerings and came away. The offerings were later picked up by the *pandas.* Baba neither knew anything about them nor had any need for them. If, when a pilgrim visited him, he happened to be in a conscious or semi-conscious state, he entertained him with a song relating to the *astakalina IiIa* of Radha-Krsna of that particular time.

Baba left his physical body to serve Radharani in his spiritual body in the form of a *manjari* (a *sakhi* exclusively devoted to Radharani) on the tenth day of Krsna-paksa in the month of Vaisakha in 1974.

Baba was great and wonderful. One might ask: Is it possible to conquer *maya* fully while remaining in the physical body, which is a product of *maya?* Is it possible to live, if one depends entirely upon the Lord and does not even go to beg for his food? Does the Lord Himself really bear the burden of the well-being of such a person? The length of Baba s life inspite of his utter indifference to the world and the body, and his contentment and beatitude, would provide the answer.

## Chapter 31 - Śrī Avadha Dasa Baba Ji

In *Mithilapuri* in Bihar there lived Śrī Harikinkara Yogindravara, a saint of Madhva-Gaudiya Sampradaya, who had unlimited faith in Srimad Bhagavatam. One day, when he was in perfect health, he called his disciples and said that he would leave his body on a particular day in the month of Kartika while reading Srimad Bhagavatam on the bank of Ganga in Semariyaghata. On that day he went to Semariyaghata with his disciples, sat down on the bank of Ganga and started reading Srimad Bhagavatam. As soon as he began to read Rasapancadhyayi, the portion relating to Rasa-IiIa,he was so overwhelmed with *bhava,* that he exclaimed "Ha Krsna! " and left the body to join Rasa-IiIa in his *siddha-deha* in transcendental Vrndavana.

Śrī Jamuna Dasa and Śrī Avadha Dasa were the two foremost disciples of Śrī Harikinkara. Before he left for Semariyaghata, he had called them both. He had told Jamuna Dasa, "After I have gone, you should go to different parts of the country and preach vaisnavism. " To Avadha Dasa he had said, "You should go to Vrndavana and do *bhajana. "*

Avadha Dasa Baba was born about the year 1826 in a Maithila Brahmin family in a village called Mandara-Madhusudana, in district Bhagalapura of Bihar, which was sanctified by the dust of the feet of Śrī Caitanya Mahaprabhu, when he was on his way to Gaya. He renounced the world at an early age and took initiation from Harikinkara Yogindravara. He remained in his Asrama in Mithilapuri so long as he was alive.

Śrī Harikinkara was not only a *siddha mahatma*, but also a great pandita. He was regarded as the crest-jewel of the *pandits* of Mithila, which had been famous for ages as a great centre of learning. Avadha Dasa studied the *sastras* from him. He made a special study of Srimad Bhagavatam and learnt the whole of it by heart.

After the passing away of Gurudeva, he went to Vrndavana and began to live in a house in Dhobi Gali, which, in course of time, became his Asrama. All his life he was confined to this Asrama. He went out of it only twice during a year, once on the occasion of the anniversary of Śrī Madhvacarya and once on the anniversary of his *gurudeva*, when he led the *nagara-kirtana.* He used to be absorbed in *bhajana* all the time, sleeping only for two or three hours at night.

His *bhajana* consisted exclusively of the *patha* (reading) and *pujana* (worship) of Srimad Bhagavatam. He went on reciting the slokas of Srimad Bhagavatam, and turning its pages without looking at them, tor he not only knew all the *slokas* by heart, he also knew the *slokas* with which each page began and ended. He worshipped Srimad Bhagavatam in exactly the same manner in which Śrī murti of Krsna is worshipped, because he regarded Srimad Bhagavatam as a manifestation of Śrī Krsna Himself. He wrapped it in a silken wrapper, placed it on a decorated *sinhasana*,offered food to it and ate it s *prasada.* He also fanned it in summer, covered it with a quilt in winter and swung it in a swing in the rainy season.

Whenever any person went to him, he found him either reading Srimad Bhagavatam or talking about it. He hardly talked or allowed anyone to talk of anything else.

Once a young man came to Vrndavana from Allahabad. He had heard that if one ate the remains of food of a *siddha* saint, he was blessed with the *darsana* of Radha-Madhava. Someone told him that Avadha Dasa Baba had won the favour of Radha-Madhava. He went to his *asrama.* At that time he was taking *prasada.* The young man went straight to him, although he was asked by his disciple not to go near him while he was eating. He sat before him and requested with folded hands for a little *prasada* out of his plate. But Baba was lost either in meditation or in the *darsana* of some lila*,* even while he was eating. He did not hear. The young man thought that Baba had ignored his request. Therefore he stretched his hand and took some *prasada* from his plate. As he was going to eat the *prasada*, Baba caught hold of his hand. He opened his fist and, quickly taking the *prasada* in the other hand, put it in his mouth.

As soon as he put the *prasada* in his mouth, he fell unconscious on the ground. He remained unconscious till late in the evening, when Baba had finished his daily *patha* of Srimad Bhagavatam; he asked the *sadhus,* who had come to listen to his *patha* to perform *kirtana* around him. As soon as *kirtana* began, he opened his eyes. For sometime he looked all round in bewilderment. Then suddenly catching hold of Baba s feet, he began to weep and say, "Pardon me Maharaja I have committed a grave offence at your feet. But now I have come to know about your real self. I shall remain at your feet and serve them all my life. I shall not go anywhere else. You will have to accept me. Have mercy Gurudeva, have mercy on me! "

Baba not only pardoned him, he gave him *diksa* and took his entire responsibility upon himself. One of the *sadhus* asked the young man what he had come to know about Baba.

He replied, "As soon as I put the remains of Baba s food in my mouth, I was transported to a transcendental region. There I saw that the mid-day *bhojana-lila* of Vrajendranandana was going on. Baba and Śrī Krsna were sitting in the middle surrounded by the *sakhas* (friends of Śrī Krsna) and, in an atmosphere of jollity and mirth, both were putting with their hands the remains of their food into each other s mouth. "

A Bengali took *diksa* and *vesa* from Baba and began to live in his *asrama.* He was very unsteady. He could not stay at one place for long. After some time he asked for Baba s permission to go and roam about in the country. Baba refused permission. Even then one day he packed up to go.

Baba asked, "Where are you going? "

"Nowhere" he said. I will just roam about a little and come back.

Baba cautioned him, "You will not gain anything by disobeying the *guru* and roaming about like a monkey. "

"I have obeyed the *guru* so long. Should I obey him all my life? " he replied and went away.

For several years Baba had no information about him. He began to worry about him. One day, while he was doing *japa* and loitering in the court-yard of the *asrama,* a young monkey came and caught hold of his feet. Baba understood that he was the same Bengali disciple. He had died and was born as a monkey, because he had disobeyed the *guru.* But on account of *bhakti* he remembered the offence he had committed in his previous life. He was repentant and had come to him to ask for pardon. Baba took pity on him and said, "You have done well by coming. Now you do not go anywhere. Remain near about the *asrama* and come for *prasada* both the times. " He tied *tulasi-kanthi* round his neck and asked his people to take care of him.

The monkey began to live on the roof of the asrama and near about it. Both the times, when *prasada* was distributed, he came and sat down in the court-yard. In the evening, when Baba said the *katha* of Srimad Bhagavatam, he came and sat at the window and listened to the *katha* with great attention.

Baba called him Ramadasa. One day he said to his disciples, "For two days I have not seen Ramadasa. You also have not looked for him. " Just then a disciple went to the roof of the *asrama* and saw that he was lying dead.

When Baba was informed, he said, "Ramadasa was a *vaisnava.* His funeral should be performed according to the *vaisnava* tradition. " Accordingly he was laid on a *vimana* and carried to the Yamuna by his disciples along with *kirtana* and delivered to the holy river. A *bhandara* was held on the occasion and the *vaisnavas* took *prasada.*

Once Baba had the *darsana* of Śrī Krsna in a very unusual and striking manner. He saw Him doing the *parikrama* of the Srimad Bhagavatam he used to recite, after he had finished the *katha* of Srimad Bhagavatam in the evening. It was striking, because it is always the worshipper, who performs the *parikrama* of the object he worships. Should Śrī Krsna have regarded Srimad Bhagavatam as the object of His worship?

But the answer is obvious. Bhagavatam is not only the manifestation of Krsna in words, it is also the manifestation in words of Prema or Transcendental Love. Prema is the End, not only for the finite souls, but also for Krsna.

Undoubtedly Krsna is Love itself, yet He yearns for more and more love. For such is the nature of love. The more love one has, the more one longs for it. It is the nature of Krsna as Love that makes Him say: *aham bhakta paradhinah* - I am always subservient to my devotees, who love me. He derives greater happiness from the love of His devotees than He derives from His own nature as bliss. Srimad Bhagavatam depicts His love-pastimes with His devotees. Therefore He regards His own manifestation as Bhagavatam as superior to Himself and as worshipable by Him. Therefore also, He is always eager to listen to the *katha* of Srimad Bhagavatam. Śrī Jiva Gosvami depicts Him in Gopala-Campu as listening to the stories of his own lila from Madhukantha and Snigdhakantha with great zest.

That day Śrī Krsna must have been present in Baba s *asrama* as one of the listeners of the Bhagavata-katha told by him and must have felt constrained to do *parikrama* of the Bhagavatam like the other listeners after the *katha.* He must also have specially enjoyed the *katha*, told by a devotee like Avadha Dasa Baba and must have felt constrained to give him something as present. Since he had nothing to give as present to Baba that might satisfy him, except his *darsana*, he gave him *darsana.*

Baba s *patha* and *puja* of Srimad Bhagavatam continued as usual, even when he was 110 years old. In 1937 he fell ill. He became so weak that it was not possible for him to walk even a small distance. He had to be lifted and carried. On the second day of Krsna-paksa of the month of Phalguna, his condition became more serious. His body was still and motionless. His disciples thought that his end had come. So they started *kirtana.* But as soon as Baba heard the first line of the *kirtana Śrī Krsna Caitanya Prabhu Nityananda"* he stood up to dance with the *kirtana.* His disciples got alarmed. They stopped *kirtana* and lay him on the bed. After a short while his condition worsened; it became obvious that he was about to breathe his last. Then according to the instruction he had given to his disciples some time before, they brought the dust of jnana-gudani,spread it on the floor and lay him down over it. At that time his lips were moving slowly and he could be heard reciting the *slokas* of Srimad Bhagavatam in a very feeble voice. He continued to recite the *slokas* in that manner till 3 a. m. next morning, when he left the body to participate in the eternal lila of Śrī Krsna in transcendental Vrndavana.

## Chapter 32 - Śrī Gopala Dasa Baba Ji

Śrī Gopala Dasa Baba Ji was born in 1851 in Imphala in Manipura. His father Śrī Meghavarna Sarma was a great devotee. Gopala Dasa took after his father. When he was still very young, he seemed indifferent to the world and spent most of his time in *bhajana.* Meghavarna Sarma was very happy to see this. Like a true father he gave him *diksa* and advised him to go to Vraja and do *bhajana* after taking *vesa* from a saint in the disciplic order of Śrī Narottama Thakura Mahasaya.

Gopala Dasa was unusually fortunate. Generally people have to pass through many ups and downs, doubts and difficulties and periods of uncertainty and irresolution before they learn that the only purpose of life is to do *bhajana* and realize the Lord; and they have to struggle hard with themselves and their family, if they want to renounce the world. But Gopala Dasa s own *samskaras* and his discerning and beneficent father s guidance made everything so easy and smooth for him.

He went to Vraja and took *vesa* from Śrī Vinodadasa Ji, a disciple of Śrī Damodara Dasa Ji in the disciplic succession of Śrī Narottama Thakura. Vinoda Ji lived near Cakalesvara Mahadeva in Govardhana. He advised him to go and live in Radhakunda and study the *sastras* from Śrī Isvara Dasa Ji. He lived in Radhakunda for three years and studied Srimad Bhagavatam and the works of the Gaudiya Gosvamis from isvara Dasa Ji. He also wanted to learn Vraja-bhasa. But isvara Dasa Ji told him, "It would be good for you not to learn Vraja-bhasa.If you learn, you will come close to the Vrajabasis. Your contacts with them will create disturbance in your *bhajana* and the chances of your committing offences against them will also increase. " Therefore he gave up the idea of learning Vraja-bhasa.

After completing his study of the s*astras,* Gopala Dasa Baba kept wandering in Vraja for some time. He did not live at any particular place for more than a day. But in the end he settled down in village Konhi and began to live near Vamsikunda in that village. He got *madhukari* from the Vrajabasis and ate only once in the evening. If any day he got more *madhukari* than he could eat, he did not go for *madhukari* on subsequent days, until the madhukari he already had was finished. He ate with pleasure whatever he got in *madhukari,* even if it was stale or rotten. But if he got sweets or other good things, he did not eat them. He gave them away to others. He used only earthen utensils made from the dust of Vraja.

He lived in Konhi for 40 years. But he loved very much the company of Śrī Harigopala Dasa Baba, a disciple of Siddha Madhusudana Dasa Baba Ji. Harigopala Dasa Baba lived in Suryakunda. Therefore after 40 years he shifted to Suryakunda and began to live in the verandah of an old temple there at the bank of Suryakunda.

Baba used to read at least one chapter of Srimad Bhagavatam every day. When he became 90 years old, he lost his eye-sight. This pained him very much, because he could not read Bhagavatam any more. One afternoon, when he was brooding over his plight, he became drowsy. In his drowsiness he saw an exquisitely beautiful girl, the lustre of whose body filled the surroundings with divine light that was soothing beyond description. She looked at him tenderly and asked in compassionate voice, "Baba, you seem to be in distress. Tell me what afflicts you. "

Baba said, " LaIi, I have lost my eye-sight and I cannot read Bhagavatam. Life hangs heavy on me. What is the use of a life in which Bhagavata-patha and *darsana* of the deities and the *mahatmas* is not possible? "

"Then do not worry Baba, " said the girl assuredly. "You have that garment on which the name Radhey-Syama is printed. Tie it round your eyes when you go to sleep tonight. In the morning you will find that your eye-sight is restored. "

As soon as Baba heard this, his drowsiness was broken. But the sweet voice of the girl was still ringing in his ears and her lustrous figure was lingering in his mind. As she had bidden, he slept at night with the garment, on which Radhey-Syama was printed, tied round his eyes. Next morning, when he got up, he was happy to find that he could see everything as before. The daily routine of his Bhagavata-patha was resumed But the girl had stolen his heart. He was convinced that She was Radharani, upon Whom he had meditated day and night. Who else could be so kind and affectionate?

But now it was impossible for him to live without Her. He was constantly shedding tears in Her separation. He could not bear the separation very long. On June 3, 1971 at mid-day, which is the time, according to the *astayama-lila*, when Radharani goes with Her *sakhis* to Suryakunda for sun-worship, he left the body to meet Her in transcendental Suryakunda in his *siddha sakhi-deha.*

Baba used to say that three things were important for a *sadhaka.* (1) *Santa-seva* (service of the saints), (2) Harinama and (3) Faith in Vraja-raja. Faith in Vraja was more important than even the service of the saints and Harinama, because at the time of death, *santa-seva* was not possible and Harinama often departed, but Vraja-raja did not leave a *sadhaka,* who lived in Vraja with faith till the end.

## Chapter 33 - Śrī Harigopala Gosvami

SriHarigopala Gosvami was a descendant of Śrī Narayana Bhatta Gosvami, to whom the deity Radharani in the temple at Barasana first appeared, and who, at the behest of Śrī Rupa and Śrī Sanatana, completed the remaining task of the rediscovery of the holy places connected with the *IiIa* of Śrī Krsna, which they and their forerunner, Śrī Lokanatha Gosvami had started.He was a disciple of Śrī Dhulesvara Gosvami of Uncagrama. He used to live in Nimarana in Alvara State, where he had his *gaddi* and the temple of his Thakura Śrī Lanile Sarakara.The Raja of Alavara was his disciple.

He was a *vaidya* (physician) by profession. But he was also a great *pandita.* He was well versed in Hindi, Sanskrta, Telegu and English. He had thorough knowledge of the six systems of Indian philosophy and the six Samdarbhas of Śrī Jiva. He had made a special study of Srimad Bhagavatam.

He was by nature very simple and saintly. Inspite of his learning, lineage and status as the Raja-Guru of the Raja of Alvara, his living was most simple and unostentatious. Still his fame had reached the people of Haryana. Many Thakuras, Gujaras, and Yadavas of Haryana were his disciples.

He used to pass most of his time quietly in *bhajana.* In his old age he shifted from Alvara to Uncagrama near Barasana, so that he might spend all his time in *bhajana* without any interruption. He also took along with him his Thakura Śrī Lanile Ji.

In Uncagrama he lived in his ancestors' old Dau Ji s temple, in which Narayana Bhatta used to live. In front of the temple, at the top of the hill, is the old temple of Lalita Sakhi. On the right hand side of Lalita Ji s temple is a beautiful spot covered by green bowers and thick foliage. Here Gosvami Ji used to sit and do *bhajana* throughout the day. He returned home at night. But most of the night also he passed in *bhajana.* His eyes were always wet in remembrance of Radharani.

Thus he passed the last twenty years of his life quietly in *bhajana.* On the night of March 30, 1990, when he was more than hundred years old, but in good health, he suddenly exclaimed, I will go, I will go.! The doors of Śrī Ji s (Radharani s) temple have opened. Purnamasi has come.! "

The other members of the family got up from their sleep with a start. They asked in bewilderment, "Where will you go? It is 12 o clock at night. Śrī Ji s temple is closed."

God alone knows whether he heard them. He went on repeating "Radhey, Radhey!" At 2 o clock the crown of his head rent and his room was aglow with a divine light. His body fell back on the pillow and his soul, passing through the *brahma-randhra*,flew to Radharani s *kunja* in the form of Her *sakhi* to live with Her and serve Her eternally.

## Chapter 34 - Śrī Ramakrsna Dasa Baba Ji

*avyavrtabhajanat*  
*(Narada-bhakti-sutra, 36)*

In this aphorism Śrī Narada Muni says that success in the practice of devotion is achieved through ceaseless *bhajana.*

The essence of *bhajana* is ceaseless remembrance of the Lord. Śrī Krsna says:

*ananya-cetah satatam yo mam smarati nityasah*  
*tasyaham sulabhah partha nitya-yuktasya yoginah*  
*(Gita 8.14)*

**O Arjuna, I am easily reached by the yogi, who constantly thinks of me, who has no other thought and is ceaselessly attached to me."**

The devotee cannot tolerate even a moment s break in continuous remembrance of the Lord. Srimad Bhagavatam says:

"If the devotee is offered all the wealth and splendour of the three worlds in exchange for discontinuance of remembrance of the lotus feet of the Lord even for half a second, he remains unmoved. How can he detach himself from the Divine Feet, which even the *devas* fail to realize through constant meditation?" (Bh, II.2.53)

Śrī Ramakrsna Dasa Baba Ji s life is an illustrious example of ceaseless *bhajana.* Born in 1911 with strong *samsakaras* of *bhakti*, in a village near Jasohara in East Bengal, he did not lose time in determining his goal and dashing towards it like an arrow He renounced the world at the early age of seventeen or eighteen, immediately after passing the intermediate examination, went to Śrī Ramadasa Baba Ji Maharaja, the renowned saint of Pathabani-Asrama, Calcutta, took initiation from him, and stayed with him for some time to serve him. Then with his permission and blessings he went to Varanasi to study Sanskrta and the Sastras He studied *Srimad Bhagavatam, Satasamdarbha, Bhakti-rasamrta-sindhu, Ujjvalanilamani* and other important *bhakti-sastras.* After completing his study of the *sastras*, he went to Vrndavana, took *vesa* from his god-brother Śrī Rajani Baba Ji Maharaja of Govindakunda, Vrndavana and proceeded to Maghera, a small village about four miles from Chatikara, for life-long ceaseless *bhajana.*

An essential part of his *bhajana* was *kirtana.* He performed *kirtana* four times during the day, *prabhati kirtana* early in the morning at 3.30 a.m., *madhyanha kirtana* at mid-day, *arati-kirtana* in the evening and *abhisara-kirtana* at night at about 10 p.m. He performed *kirtana* at such a high pitch that his voice could be heard in the remotest corner of the village. His engrossment in *kirtana* was so deep that sometimes during *kirtana* he exclaimed aloud "Ha Nitai!" or "Ha Radhey!" and fell senseless on the ground. All the rest of his time he spent in Giridhari-seva and *lila-smarana.* Not a moment of his life was lost without *smarana.* He was often heard repeating the following lines of Hanumana:

*kaha hanumana vipati prabhu soi*  
*jaba tava sumirana bhajana na hoi*

***"*****O Lord! When there is no remembrance or** ***bhajana*** **of your lotus feet, that time is the time of the greatest calamity or misfortune!"**

Even while Baba attended to his bodily needs like easing nature or bathing, his *smarana* went on uninterrupted. That time he used to be primarily engaged in *smarana,* the bodily activities went on automatically on account of habit.

After a few years of strictly disciplined life and constant *bhajana,* he conquered sleep and hunger He used to be engaged in *bhajana*, constantly from 3.30 a.m to 2.30 a.m the next day, thus sleeping only for one hour out of the 24 hours in a day. He went out for *madhukari* to only three houses at midday. If he did not get anything from them, he returned to his *kuti* and fasted instead of wasting time by going to other houses. He ate only two *roties* in the day and one-fourth of a *roti* at night.

It may seem incredible from the bodily point of view that one may live long, if he sleeps and eats so little. But, as we have already seen in so many others cases, the saints, who practice bhajana constantly, rise above the body. They drink deep of the nectar that drips into their hearts from above at the time of *smarana.* It is this that keeps them alive and happy.

The condition in which Baba lived was itself a proof that he had risen above the body. His *kuti* was so small that he could hardly lie down in it with his legs fully stretched. Around the *kuti* the land was so low that during rains it was flooded and the *kuti* was cut off from the rest of the village. Baba had to wade through the flooded land every time he went out for *madhukari.* The mosquitoes also plagued and pestered his body beyond description. Yet he seemed to be either totally unaware or unmindful of them. The villagers requested him to move to some other place in the village, but he refused. He regarded the situation as even more favourable to him, because it deterred people from going to him and causing disturbance in his *bhajana.*

After some time Baba became blind. But this did not make him unhappy, because after he lost his physical eyes, his spiritual eyes opened. He could see Radha and Krsna and talk with them. People often heard him talking.

So far Baba had not accepted any disciple. But after his blindness he accepted two disciples, Pranagauranga Dasa and Madanamohana Dasa, one of whom always lived near him in a separate *kuti* and served him.

Baba asked both of his disciples not to accept money from anyone. He neither touched money himself nor allowed his disciples to touch it. If he needed anything for the service of his Giridhari or for the protection of his own body, that also he did not accept from any person, who offered it. There was no question of his accepting it, if it was brought from outside Vraja or was purchased with the money of a person, who lived out of Vraja. If inadvertently some such thing was brought, he always came to know about it and asked his disciples to return it.

Once Baba fell ill. He was taken for treatment to his *guru-asrama* in Vrndavana. I went to see him, since I had known him for some time and he was very kind to me. I requested him to take some milk, because he was too weak. After a good deal of persuasion he agreed. I arranged for the supply of milk. A few days later my friend Śrī Harirama Singhaniya, an industrialist of Calcutta, came to Vrndavana. He requested me to take him to some *siddha* saint for *darsana.* I took him to Baba. He was very much impressed by him and wanted to do some service to him. I said, "Baba does not accept any service from anyone. He also does not need anything except *madhukari.* With great difficulty he has agreed to take milk for a few days." At once he said, "Then please let the milk be supplied at my expense." I agreed rather reluctantly. Singhaniya Ji had not yet paid for the milk. But it could not remain hidden from Baba s keen spiritual sense that henceforth the milk he would drink would be purchased with the money of a person, who lived outside Vraja. The very next day he called his disciple Madana Mohana and said, "Madana, I will not live here. Take me back to Maghera immediately."

Madana said, "Baba, everything here is so favourable and your health has begun to improve. You should stay here until you are quite well."

"No, No. You don t know. There is *parapeksa* (dependence on others) here. I cannot stay here even for a moment. Get a *tonga* at once."

Madan had to bring a tonga and take him back to Maghera the same day.

Once again Baba fell ill in 1984. His godbrother Śrī Visvarupa Dasa Baba, who lived in Rala, a village about four miles from Maghera, took him to Rala for treatment. He kept him in a room in Basanti-ki-bagici, close to his own kuti. Baba was physically ill, but spiritually he seemed to swim always in the ocean of bliss. The waves of the ocean tossed him up and down and he sometimes laughed, sometimes wept and sometimes cried aloud like one who had gone mad, because the bliss was too intense for his frail body and mind to contain. One night at about 1 o clock he shouted, "Jai Nitai! Jai Nitai!" and became unconscious. Visvarupa Baba came running. When he saw him lying unconscious, he apprehended that he had perhaps breathed his last. He caught hold of his wrist to feel his pulse. But as soon as he touched Baba a heart-rending sound emanated from his throat and a shock like that of an electric current threw Visvarupa Baba three or four feet away from his bed. Perhaps Baba had seen Nityananda Prabhu and his body was charged with a divine current that caused the shock.

The next day was Ekadasi. At 1 o clock at night, when Baba was engaged in *bhajana,* he saw that his *guru* Śrī Rama Dasa Baba Ji, *parama-guru* Śrī Radharamana Carana Dasa Deva and a number of other *mahatmas* entered the *bagici,* dancing and performing *kirtana* to the accompaniment of *mrdanga* and *karatata.* They said to him, "We are going to Vrndavana. You also come." With this they suddenly disappeared and Baba fell unconscious.

On Trayodasi, May 13, 1984 at noon Baba sent for Śrī Sivacarana Varsaneya, a disciple of one of his god-brothers. When he came, Baba was doing *japa.* He said to him, "Baba Ji Maharaja (Śrī Rama Dasa Baba) and Bane Baba (Śrī Radharamana Carana Dasa Deva) have asked me to go to Vrndavana. So please take me there at once."

Varsaneya Ji said, "Baba, it is very hot just now. Let us go in the evening."

"No, No, I shall go just now. Don t waste time.

Varsaneya Ji brought a *tonga.* Baba sat in the *tonga* along with Varsaneya Ji and Madana Mohana. As Varsaneya Ji touched Baba to help him get into the *tonga*, he felt that a current ran through his body. Baba s body was still charged with the divine current that had struck him and made him unconscious on the Ekadasi day. At 2.30 p.m. the *tonga* reached the *guru-asrama* of Baba. At *guru-asrama* non-stop Hari-kirtana had been going on for thirty years. Baba said, "Take me to the place of *kirtana."* He went and sat there and remained sitting in meditation for about half an hour. He was, perhaps, waiting for Nrsinha Caturdasi, which was to start after half an hour. As soon as Nrsinha Caturdasi started, he shouted "Jai Nitai! Jai Radhey! and left the body to meet his Gurudeva and Parama-Gurudeva in transcendental Vrndavana, and to play his part in the divine *IiIa* of Radha-Krsna under their guidance.

Everyone had thought that Baba had gone to Vrndavana, but would return to Maghera after a few days, as he had done before. But this time he was called to the transcendental Vrndavana, from where no one ever returned. If he appeared to have gone to the phenomenal Vrndavana, it was because what appeared as the phenomenal Vrndavana was itself the transcendental Vrndavana. It appeared as phenomenal to people, whose vision was perverted. To one like Baba, whose spiritual eyes had opened, it was the same transcendental Vrndavana, where Krsna-IiIa was eternally going on, where not only the *gopas* and *gopies,* but the birds and animals, the trees and creepers and even the grass and the dust were made of *sat* (existence), *cit* (intelligence), and *ananda* (bliss) and were eternally engaged in the service of Krsna; where everything was tuned to the flute of Krsna, where even time stood still or flowed, and space expanded or contracted like the petals of a lotus, according to the sweet will of Krsna. It was the same Vrndavana, which surpassed in beauty all the other *lokas,* including Vaikuntha and Goloka i and the same Vrndavana to which Śrī Prabodhananda Sarasvatipada pointed out when he said:

Oh! this Vrndavana of mine,  
stationed above every other dhama!  
How it shines near me like a big moon  
in all its resplendent beauty.  
Vrndavana Mahimamrta 4.83

## Chapter 35 - Śrī Radharamana Dasa Baba Ji

Living to die in Vrndavana is regarded as the surest shortcut to eternal life of loving service to Radha-Krsna in transcendental Vrndavana. Sanatkumara Samhita says:

"If a man only surrenders to Vrndavana, that is, lives here with faith and devotion till the end, then he has nothing else to do in order to attain the highest end. Vrndavana by virtue of its own nature (as a manifestation of the internal potency of Bhagavan, which consists of *sat*, *cit* and *ananda)* gives him the *parama-pada*, that is, a place in the highest transcendental region (which is the transcendental Vrndavana itself) without considering his merit or demerit." (Sanatkumara Samhita, 32.5)

But since the *adhisvari*(empress) of Vrndavana is Radharani, no one can die here if he does not have Her blessings. It has bee noticed that some people, who lived in Vrndavana all their life, were pushed out of Vrndavana at the end, because they did not have Her blessings; while some others, who lived out of Vrndavana all their life, came here at the end and died here, because they had Her blessings.

Śrī Radharamana Dasa Baba Ji was one of those exceptionally fortunate persons, who was called to Vrndavana by Radharani Herself, the very day on which he was destined to leave his body so that he might leave it in Vrndavana.

Śrī Radharamana was born on April 14, 1932 in Mulati, a village in 24 Paragana of West-Bengal, in a respectable Brahmin family. His father Śrī Vinaya Krsna was a landlord and a famous advocate of Calcutta Highcourt. His mother Lalita Devi was a very religious lady. She was a disciple of Śrī Rama Dasa Baba Ji Maharaja, the renowned *siddha* saint of Pathabani, Calcutta. She got Radharamana Dasa also initiated by Rama Dasa Baba Ji Maharaja, when he was still young.

After initiation a sudden change was noticed in him. He used to retire to a lonely place and meditate for hours. His eyes always swam in tears. In this state he somehow completed his education and passed the M. A. examination from the Calcutta university. But when his parents began to negotiate for his marriage, he found himself standing on cross-road. He renounced the world and went to Vrndavana.

In Vrndavana he took *samyasa-diksa* from Śrī Madhava Dasa Baba Ji. Then he went to village Maghera, where his elder godbrother Śrī Ramakrsna Dasa Baba lived. Under his guidance he studied *Srimad Bhagavatam, Śrī Caitanya-caritamrta, Śrī Caitanya Bhagavata*, and other scriptures.

After this he began to live in Vihara-vana near village Rala and practised *sadhana.* His absorption in *bhajana* was so deep that he would not even go for *madhukari* every day. He went for *madhukari* only once or twice a week and collected sufficient *roties* to last him for a number of days. When the *roties* dried he dipped them in water and ate. The result was that he developed gastric ulcer. Acute pain in the stomach caused great difficulty in *bhajana.* He had to go to Calcutta for treatment. He was admitted in a hospital. His younger brother Sacidulala and niece Kumari Nandini attended upon him. But his condition worsened day by day. One day in the evening the pain in the stomach suddenly became too severe. The doctors were called. He overheard one of them saying to the other, He may vomit blood tonight and die."

He was not afraid of death, but his anxiety was that he would die out of Vrndavana in a Calcutta hospital. In his anguish he began to pray to Radharani: O Svamini (the mistress of my heart)! Why have you turned me out of Vrndavana to die here like a dog? Perchance I have committed some offence. May be that instead of doing *bhajana*, I have only deceived myself and others by aping as one absorbed in *bhajana.* If I had not done so, you must at least have given me the boon of death in Vrndavana. You thought I did not even deserve to die in Vrndavana. But my Svamini! Even though undeserving, am I not yours? Even if I have aped, have I aped as someone other than your servant?

You are by nature so merciful that even if a man takes your name by mistake, you give him *darsana.* I do not know why you are so indifferent to me, I do not ask for *darsana,* because I know I do not deserve it. I ask only for death in Vrndavana. Have mercy on me, Svamini, have mercy on me!" As he said this, he cried aloud in deep distress and became unconscious. His cry did not go in vain. It touched the heart of Radharani. She came running along with Krsna and gave him *darsana* in his unconscious state. With a smile on Her face She showed him Her right hand, as if to bless him with death in Vrndavana, while Krsna gently moved His lotus hand on his stomach and asked in a tender voice, "Where do you feel pain, Baba?" How could Baba say where he was feeling pain and to whom? For the pain as well as the questioner had disappeared along with the question and Radha had disappeared too.

The next morning the doctors were surprised to see that the patient, who was writhing with excruciating and incurable pain till only eight or ten hours before, and should have died by morning, had sat up and was asking for food.

Baba returned to Vraja and again began to live in Vihara-vana. He had regained his health. His stomach pain had disappeared. But he had developed another kind of pain, for which no doctor had any treatment. He had begun to feel severe pain in separation from Radha-Krsna. He had felt this pain before, but it was neither so severe nor so constant and unbearable. This was natural, because ever since Radha-Krsna had responded to his prayer and blessed him, not only with their *darsana*, but with their winsome smile and affection, he had developed a new conviction, a conviction that they were his own more than anyone else. How could he bear the thought that those, who were his own more than anyone else, be remote from him more than anyone else? The thought pained him so much that he often became unconscious.

After some time he went to Jatipura and began to live in a cave which is known as the cave of Raghava Pandita, a close associate of Śrī Caitanya Mahaprabhu. But his *viraha* (pain in separation) went on increasing. Sometimes he lay unconscious in *viraha* for hours together. His indifference to body also increased. He lived only on buttermilk and the leaves of the Nima tree. The result was that he became too weak. His disciples, particularly Śrī Baladeva Rama Gupta of Jaipur and Śrī Siva Carana Varsaneya of Rala, began to worry about his health. On their insistence he went to Jaipur so that Baladeva Rama Ji might take proper care of him. Still there was no abatement in his *viraha.*

In this condition his soul would have departed from his body long before, but for the occasional appearance of Radha or Krsna before him in some form or other. Once while he lived in Raghava Pandit s cave, he saw Radharani along with Her *sakhis* picking flowers from a garden and smiling at him, as if to reassure him that he continued to have Her blessings.

Once while he and Baladeva Rama were returning from a village to Jaipur after sunset, they lost their way in darkness and entered a jungle. They were trying to get out of the jungle, when some tribal boys appeared on the scene. One of them, who looked most attractive, said to Baba, "You will go to Jaipur. Come, I will show you the way." As he said this, he held Baba s hand. He had hardly gone a step or two with Baba, when suddenly the scene changed. There was neither the jungle nor any of the tribal boys and Baba and Baladeva Rama Ji found themselves standing near the Jaipur station.

But though these experiences came to Baba as oases in the desert, and gave him temporary relief, they could not quench his thirst for *darsana.* It went on increasing till the limit was reached. On November 26, 1987, when he was still in Jaipur, he had a vision and a message from Radharani. He called Baladeva Rama and said to him, "Radharani has called me to Vrndavana Take me there." The next day Baladeva Rama started with Baba in a car for Vrndavana On the way Baba went to meet some of his disciples and devotees and said to them while parting, "I take leave of you. May Radharani bless you!"

Baba said this in a manner which indicated that he was going to leave them for ever. This perturbed Baladeva Rama. He began to think, "Is Baba really going to leave us all for ever? Does Radharani s call mean call to eternal Vrndavana? But the thought was too much for him. He explained it away by thinking that Baba was going to live in Vrndavana for the rest of his life and would not return to meet his disciples and devotees of Jaipur again.

Baba reached Vrndavana in the evening. He proposed to stay for some time with his devotee Śrī Atmarama Ji, who lived in Jnyanagudani. So Baladeva Rama took him there. After some time he went with Baladeva Rama to Loi Bazara. He purchased two pictures of Gaura-Nitai and Radha-Krsna. Then he said to Baladeva Rama. "You go back to Atmaram s place. I shall return after meeting Murari Lal." Murari Lal was another devotee of Baba. Baladeva Rama reached Atmaram s house. But Baba did not reach there till late at night. Baladeva Rama and Atmarama Ji went to the house of Murari Lal to inquire about him. They were told that Baba had not gone there at all. For two days Baladeva Rama, Atmarama and Murari Lal searched every nook and corner of Vrndavana, but could not find Baba. After two days they read in Amara Ujala newspaper, that the dead body of a *mahatma* was found in the cave underneath Radharani s Ranga Mahala in Nidhivana. A shudder went through their body. They rushed to the spot and found to their surprise and dismay and that it was the body of Radharamana Dasa Baba Ji.

Since Baba s death had occurred in mysterious circumstances, the police carried his body to Mathura for postmortem. The postmortem was done. Nothing incriminatory was found. It was obvious that Baba had gone to transcendental Vrndavana at the call of Radharani, Who had accepted him in his *siddha manjari deha* as Her *sakhi* in Her Ranga Mahala or *nitya-nikunja* in transcendental Nidhi-vana.

## Chapter 36 - Shah Kundan Lal (Lalit Kishor) and Shah Phundan Lal (Lalit Madhuri)

Shah Kundan Lal and Shah Phundan Lal, the builders of the famous and beautiful temple of Shah Ji in Vrindavan were the sons of Shah Govind Lal and the grandsons of Shah Bihari Lal, a fabulously rich landlord of Lucknow Kundan Lal was born in 1882 and Phundan Lal in 1885. The two brothers inherited the enormous wealth of their grandfather. But they were richer in their Samskaras of Bhakti than in wealth. Since their childhood they had loved Śrī Kṛṣṇa and Vrindavana and longed for an opportunity to go to Vrindavana to see their family Thakur Radharamana and the places connected with Kṛṣṇa-lila, about which they had so far only read and heard.

The opportunity came, when Shah Bihari Lal built the present temple of Radharamana in Vrindavana and a golden altar for Radharamana. He asked his grandsons to go to Vrindavana to deliver the altar. They went and has the darshan of the exquisitely beautiful figure of Radharamana. The figure cast a magic spell upon them. What happened to them reminds us of the warning given by Rupa Gosvami to anyone desirous of seeing his deity Govind Ji. He has said in a sloka, Dear friend! If you want to enjoy life with your friends and relations, do not see the Śrī Vigraha of Śrī Hari, called Govind Deva in Kesi Tirtha, Who stands there smiling in thrice bent pose with a peacock feather on His crown and a flute on His lips, and Who casts sidelong glances with His big eyes on the onlookers to steal their hearts. If you look at Him once, you will forget their kith and kin and everything else. The two brothers forgot their kith and kin and everything else on seeing Radharamana. They saw Him every morning and evening and wanted to see Him everyday and serve Him as best as they could till the end of their life. Bhaktirasamrta-sindhu, 1,2,239

Lal, a fabulously rich landlord of Lucknow. Kundan Lal was born in 1882 and Phundan Lal in 1885. The two brothers inherited the enormous wealth of their grandfather. But they were richer in their Samskaras of Bhakti than in wealth. It was impossible for him to pass urine or stool on the soil of Vraja. He used to ease himself and urinate in earthen pots, made from the earth of Agra and had the pots thrown out of Vraja. He had willed that on his death his body must not be carried on the shoulders of people, but dragged through the lanes and bylanes of Vrindavana in such a way that it touched the dust of Vrindavana.

Though engaged in various ways in the service of Radharamana and the Vaishnavas Kundan Lals heart always yearned for the direct service of Radharamana and Radharani. On Kartika Shukla Pratipada in the year 1873 he had a call from Radharani. The same day he took Sannyasa. The next morning he said to his brother, I have a call from Radharani. I must go Prepare for me a cabutara (small platform) with Vraja- raja (the dust of Vraja) to sit on, pitch round it trunks of banana trees with leaves and decorate them with flowers and pictures of Radharaman so that it looks like a kunja (arbour).

His brother was taken aback. But he started preparing the kunja, as instructed. After a couple of hours, when the kunja was ready, he sat down on the cabutara of Vraja-raja inside the kunja and asked his people to perform kirtana. He also joined the kirtana. At that time his face beamed with happiness. Tears flowed from his eyes. Tremor, horripilation and stupor appeared on his body After some time he fixed his gaze upon the pictures of Radharamana, his voons, do not see the Śrī Vigraha of Śrī Hari, called Govind Deva in Kesi Tirtha, Who stands there smiling in thrice bent pose with a peacock feather on His crown and a flute on His lips, and Who casts side-long glances with His big eyes on the onlookers to steal their hearts. If you look at Him once, you will forget their kith and kin and everything else. The two brothers forgot their kith and kin and everything else on seeing Radharamana. They saw Him every morning and evening and wanted to see Him everice became lower and lower till it stopped. He left his physical body and the kunja of the terrestrial. Vrindavana to find himself soon in his transcendental body as sakhi in the loving embrace of Radharani in her kunja in celestial Vrindavana.

The news of his passing away spread like wild fire in every nook and corner of Vrindavana. Crowds of people began to pour in to pay homage to his departed soul. Arrangement began to be made for his funeral according to his own instructions. The sand of Yamuna was laid out on the lanes of Vrindavana. His body was gently dragged over it and taken round the main temples of Vrindavana. Finally it was laid in Samadhi.

On the Jyestha Shukla Panchami of the year 1885 Shah Phundan Lal similarly passed away suddenly in good state of health doing kirtana on a cabutara of Vraja-raja. The Samadhis of both the brothers exist on either side of the gate of Lalita Nikunja.

He used to ease himself and urinate in earthen pots, made from the earth of Agra and had the pots thrown out of Vraja. He had willed that on his death his body must not be carried on the shoulders of people, but dragged through the lanes and bylanes of Vrindavana in such a way that it touched the dust of Vrindavana.

Though engaged in various ways in the service of Radharamana and the Vaishnavas Kundan Lals heart always yearned for the direct service of Radharamana and Radharani. On Kartika Shukla Pratipada in the year 1873 he had a call from Radharani. The same day he took Sannyasa. The next morning he said to his brother, I have a call from Radharani. I must go. Prepare for me a cabutara (small platform) with Vraja- raja (the dust of Vraja) to sit on, pitch round it trunks of banana trees with leaves and decorate them with flowers and pictures of Radharaman so that it looks like a kunja (arbour).

His brother was taken aback. But he started preparing the kunja, as instructed. After a couple of hours, when the kunja was ready, he sat down on the cabutara of Vraja-raja inside the kunja and asked his people to perform kirtana. He also joined the kirtana. At that time his face beamed with happiness. Tears flowed from his eyes. Tremor, horripilation and stupor appeared on his body. After some time he fixed his gaze upon the pictures of Radharamana, his voice became lower and lower till it stopped. He left his physical body and the kunja of the terrestrial Vrindavana to find himself soon in his transcendental body as sakhi in the loving embrace of Radharani in her kunja in celestial Vrindavana.

The news of his passing away spread like wild fire in every nook and corner of Vrindavana. Crowds of people began to pour in to pay homage to his departed soul. Arrangement began to be made for his funeral according to his own instructions. The sand of Yamuna was laid out on the lanes of Vrindavana. His body was gently dragged over it and taken round the main temples of Vrindavana. Finally it was laid in Samadhi.

On the Jyestha Shukla Panchami of the year 1885 Shah Phundan Lal similarly passed away suddenly in good state of health doing kirtana on a cabutara of Vraja-raja The Samadhis of both the brothers exist on either side of the gate of Lalita Nikunja.

But they were not the masters of themselves. How could they stay in Vrindavana forever? They were expected to return after three or four days, but had stayed on for more than a month. They passed a few more days seeing the different places connected with Kṛṣṇa- IiIa, then returned to Lucknow, leaving their hearts in Vrindavana.

They began to pray to Radharani for Her mercy so that She might cut asunder their bonds of Maya and make it possible for them to live and do bhajana in Vrindavana. Both the brothers were also good poets and musicians. They began to pass their time in writing poetry and doing kirtan and thinking and talking always about Vrindavana. Shah Kundan Lals collection of poems published under the title Abhilasam Madhuri reflects the state of mind at this time.

After some time Shah Bihari Lal, Shah Govind Lal and his wife died suddenly one after another. The clouds of Maya that had so far been casting their gloomy shadows upon the two brothers, disappeared and their passage to Vrindavana seemed to have cleared. But the shackles of Maya were still too strong to let them fly atonce to Vrindavana. The responsibility of managing the affairs of the big estate of Shah Bihari Lal had suddenly fallen upon them. The estate was so big that Shah Bihari Lal had to maintain an army for its protection. The title Shah (King), which was conferred upon him by the Navab of Lucknow was not simply an ormily for generations. It seems that the deity now became even more restless than the Shah brothers to go to Vrindavana. He said to Shah Kundan Lal in a dream, Take Me to Vrindavana and build for Me a temple there near Nidhivana.

Shah Kundan Lal was happy to receive the mandate directly from Radharamana. He decided to set aside all other considerations and go to Vrindavana as early as possible. As willed by Radharamana there came at this time from Vrindavana Śrī Radhagovind Gosvami, the guru of Shah Kundan Lal. On his going back, Kundan Lal gave him his deity and said, You take Him with you to Vrindavana and serve Him till l come. I shall also come soon.

They wrote poetry under different names. Shah Kundan Lal under the name Lalit Kishori and Shah Phundan under the name Lalit Madhuri. They are better known by these names.

**Muslim ruler**

Shah Kunan Lal collected a huge amount of wealth needed for the construction of a magnificent temple for his deity Radharamana. The cavalcade consisted of the staff, bullock carts, soldiers and horsemen. Army was necessary because during those days bandits were at large everywhere. The journey was long and he had to pitch his camp for the night after going about 15 or 20 miles each day. The last camp was pitched just outside the border of Vraja. The next morning, as usual, the servant brought the hukka and placed it before Shah Kundan Lal. Kundan Lal kicked it off and never smoked hukka afterwards. Both the brothers also cast away their shoes, because they could not traverse the holy land with shoes on. They entered barefooted into Vraja and never put on shoes again.

On reaching Vrindavana they first stayed in the kunja of. Raja Patanimal near the old temple of Radharamana. Some of the staff and the servants stayed with them. The rest of them and the army lived in camps on the bank of Yamuna.

The arrival of the Shah brothers in Vrindavana with their entourage and the army marked the beginning of a new chapter in Vrindavana. It infused in. the Vrajavasis a new sense of security. Their humility, munificence and their loving attitude towards them made them feel that they had in the two brothers their guardians who would take care of them in their moments of need and adversity.

The Shah brothers arrived in Vrindavana in 1856. The year 1857 was the year of Indian mutiny, which shook the British government at its roots. The whole country was in turmoil.

The government however, succeeded in suppressing the mutiny. After that they started trying those who had helped the rebels and hanging them. Shah Kundan Lal had given shelter to rebel Hirasinha and his followers. A warrant was, therefore, issued against him and a date was fixed for his trial in a court in Mathura.

A gloomy shadow of despair and despondency was cast all over Vrindavana. Everyone was deeply worried But Shah Kundan Lal was calm and quiet and fearless as indicated by a poem, which he composed at this time:

Is there a weapon sharper than the eyebrows of Govind? One whom Govind has made the target of that weapon why should he fear any other weapon or government? Undaunted and unruffled I stand before the government to face the trial and its consequences, for I wear the armour of the protective eye of Govind. Shah Kundan Lal went to Mathura performing kirtan with thousands of his admirers and sympathisers and presented himself before the English magistrate. He was made to take the usual oath. After that the magistrate said, Kundan Lal! Do you know the rebel Hirasinha? Yes sir, Replied Kundan Lal.

Did you at the time of mutiny keep him and his men in your house and entertain them? Yes sir.

Do you know the punishment for giving shelter to a rebel?

Yes sir, it is death.

If you know that why did you support him?

Sir! Hirasingha was a bandit turned rebel. He came to Vraja for looting and plundering the Vrajavasis. I threatened to attack him with my army, but promised to give him shelter if he did not do any harm to Vrajavasis. He promised not to do any harm to anyone in Vraja.
