He lived up tp his promise, I lived up to mine. Thus I saved Vraja from being ravaged and the people of Vraja from being looted and murdered by him. It is the duty of a government to protect its subjects and the duty of the people to serve the government. By giving shelter to Hirasinha I only served the government in protecting its subjects.

You are very clever. You are twisting facts to hide your fault. You must be hanged.

Sir! You can hang me if you want. But before hanging the criminal is asked what he wants. I tell you what I want. I want that you hang me in Vrindavana and let there be kirtana at the time of hanging. If you hang me like this, I shall regard hanging not as punishment, but as reward.

Are you ready to be hanged in this manner?

Yes sir.

The magistrate was impressed by his devotion and fearlessness. He thought that what he said was true. He announced the order for his release. The Vrajavasis, who had gathered in the court and outside shouted, 'Shah Kundan Lal Ki Jai! Rejoicing and singing and dancing in Sankirtan they brought him back to Vrindavana.

Shah Kundan Lal soon applied himself to the task of building a temple for his deity Radharamana. The construction started in the year 1859 and was completed in 1867. Radharamana was installed in the temple with great eclat. The temple was called LaIit-nikunja , but it is generally known by the name Shah-Ji- Ka-Mandir (Shah-Ji s temple). Both the brothers served Radharamana with great devotion. They did not pass a single minute without bhajan and slept only for 3 or 4 hours.

Shah Kundan Lal was very much interested in Rasa-lila. He wrote dialogues and composed songs for the Lila and trained the actors in dance and music. He was also a reputed physician. He used all his talents in the service of Radharamana, the Vaishnavas and the Vrajavasis.

His faith in Vrindavana and the dust of Vrindavan was unique. He determined

## Chapter 37 - Gulab Sakhi

In Barasana there is a place called Pilipokhar. As we go from Pilipokhar to Premasarovar, we see a small and old *chabutra* or platform in the adjoining forest. The platform is called *Gulab Sakhi-ka-chabutra.* Even today people bow down to it as they pass by.

Gulab Sakhi was a poor Mohammadan, who lived in Barasana the abode of Radharani, since his birth. Since his birth he had rolled in the holy dust of Barasana, breathed the air of Barasana, charged with *bhava* and *bhakti* and enjoyed the company of the Vrajavasis of Barasana. He was a Mohammadan only by birth. His life was cast in the same mould in which was cast the life of the Vrajavasis of Barasana.

He was very humble and simple at heart. Though illiterate, he was a good Sarangiplayer. He played Sarangi regularly at the time of *kirtana* in the temple of Radharani. He had a daughter about seven or eight years old whose name was Radha. She danced while he played Sarangi in the temple. Her dance was enchanting. For the service he rendered in the temple, he was paid a small salary of rupees eight or. ten. But he got Radharani s *prasada* both the times, which was enough for him and his daughter. He was happy and contented. There was nothing else that he wanted.

He loved his daughter very much. He was so satisfied with his service to Radharani by his music and her dance at the temple both morning and evening that he felt as if he lived in the highest paradise. He never thought that his daughter would be married one day and his paradise will come to an end.

But the daughter came of age and had to be married. The Gosvami of Barasana all loved his daughter. They after said to him, Gulab! Radha has come of age. Why don t you marry her? Everytime they said so, he replied, Where is the wherewithal required for marriage. The Gosvamis decided to raise money for her marriage and asked him to look for a suitable boy. Fortunately he found a suitable boy Radha was married. She went with her husband and Gulab was left alone.

Gulab was no more the old happy and cheerful Gulab. Cruel fate had cast its darkest shadow upon him. He not only lost his cheerfulness, but also his sleep and appetite. For three days and three nights he had been sitting at the door of Radharani s temple and weeping. He only wept and sighed and cried Radha! Radha! The Gosvamis thought he would go mad. They tried to console him, but in vain.

On the third day at 12 o clock at night when he was lying at the door of the temple with eyes closed, he heard the voice of his daughter. She said, Baba! I have come. Will you not play Sarangi so that I may dance?

It is difficult to say whether Gulab was sleeping or awake. But he saw with his eyes closed that his daughter danced and he played Sarangi. That night her dance was much more enchanting and the jingle of the ornament, she wore round her ankles, much more pleasing to the ear and captivating to the heart than usual. It was found to be so, because it was not really his daughter Radha, but Radharani Herself Who was dancing in the guise of his daughter. Gulab realized this, because the transcendental jingle of the anklets of Radharani had opened his physical eyes as well as spiritual. He looked at Her with eyes wide open and wet with tears and said, LaIi! As he wanted to say something more, moving towards Her with heart full of affection, She ran towards the temple and he ran after Her.

After this Gulab was never seen anywhere. The Gosvamis thought that he could not bear the separation of his daughter. So he died or committed suicide. They constructed, the *chabutra* already mentioned, in his memory.

One day, when a Gosvami who was the Pujari of Radharani s temple, was returning from the temple after performing Arati and putting Radharani to sleep, he heard someone from behind a cluster of trees in the adjoining forest, calling. Gosvami Ji, Gosvami Ji!

He turned round and said, "Who is that?

Your Gulab, came the reply and Gulab came out of the forest.

Gosvami was surprised to see him. He said, Did you not die?

Gulab told him the whole story about Radharani s appearance to him as his daughter and added, She has kindly accepted me as Her Sakhi (girl companion). I have just come after playing Sarangi to Her as She lay down to sleep. Here is Her *prasadi pana*, Have it.

Gulab gave the *pana* to Gosvami Ji. He was surprised to see that it was the same *pana* which he had just offered to Radharani.

From this time Gulab began to be called Gulab Sakhi.

What Sadhana, one might ask, Gulab had practised to deserve such mercy of Radharani? He was not even initiated by a Guru. Don t the Sastras say that initiation by a Vaishnava Guru and the practise of one or more of the nine kinds of Bhakti are essential conditions for the attainment of the mercy of Radha and Kṛṣṇa? The only thing Gulab did to win over Radharani was that he wept at Her door and cried Radha, Radha. But how could that melt Radharani s heart? Was She aware that he was crying for his daughter, not for Her?

It is difficult to answer this question. It may, however, be pointed out that the conditions and the rules and regulations of the Sastras are for us, not for Radharani. Radharani is all merciful. When the ocean of Her mercy swells, it crosses all barriers of scriptural rules and regulations. The rules are for us, for Radharani the exceptions. And as we say, the exceptions prove the rule.

It may also be observed that Gulab was humble, pure in heart and free from all kinds of offences. Radharani s mercy flows more easily towards persons, who are pure in heart, than towards those, who are not pure in heart, howsoever long and arduous be the course of Sadhana they may have practised. Gulab had not practised *japa, tapa* (austerity) or any other kind of Sadhana, that could be noticed from outside. His life itself was a silent Sadhana. He had adopted Sarangi service in the temple of Radharani primarily as his way of life. It was only secondarily a means of his livelihood. He could have gone out of Barasana to earn a better living by displaying his art elsewhere. But he could not even for a moment think of giving up the service to Radharani. He was completely surrendered to Radharani. Everything that belonged to him, including his daughter, was for him a means for the service of Radharani. After Radha was gone he was weeping and crying for her not because of his attachment for her as his daughter but because she was to him an indispensable means for the service of Radharani. How could Radharani prevent Her mercy from flowing freely towards a surrendered soul like him?

## Chapter 38 - Chota Baba

Chota Baba was born. Because he was too young he began to be called Chota Baba (young Baba Ji) and was known by that name till the end.

Chota Baba was completely sold out to Jai Kṛṣṇa Das Baba on account of his sweet and loving behaviour. He accepted him as his Siksha Guru and decided never to leave him. Jai Kṛṣṇa Das deputed him in the service of his deity Radha-madanamohan.

Jai Kṛṣṇa Das was so much pleased by his service and his spontaneous loving attitude towards the deity that he decided to train him in Raganuga way of Bhakti.He asked him one day, Do you have your *guru-pranalika?"*

He said, I do not know what *guru-pranalika* is.

Baba explained to him what it meant and said, You have tp go to Navadvip once to get the *guru-pranalika* from your *guru.* It is necessary for Raganuga *bhajana,* which i want you now to adopt. He also explained to him the importance and sweetness of Raganuga *bhajana.*

But Chota Baba was so much attached to Jai Kṛṣṇa Das Baba that he could not think of leaving him even for a moment. He regarded his company and service more important and enjoyable than any other kind of *bhajana.* He could not say anything in reply and expressed his unwillingness to go to Navadvip by shedding tears. Baba impressed upon him the necessity and importance of Raganuga *bhajana* again and again and somehow made him agree to go to Navadvip to bring his *guru-pranalika.*

During those days there was no train that went to Bengal from Mathura. One had to go to Hatharas to board the train from Bengal. The next day Chota Baba started for Hatharas. Jai Kṛṣṇa Das Baba bade him farewell. Both parted with tears.

While Chota Baba was going to Hatharas tears incessantly flowed from his eyes. As he approached Hatharas station his heart began to sink. He knew that as soon as he would board the train his oul would leave the body. He was in a pitiable situation. Obedience to the command of the *guru* meant death, disobedience also meant death. He began to pray to Radharani and Vrinda Devi and say, O Radhe! O Vrnde! You must be aware of my plight. I have taken shelter under your feet in the expectation that you will be merciful to me and will one day accept me in the service of your lotus feet. Why are you pushing me away from your feet? At least bless me so that I die before the fateful hour of my boarding the train arrives. Radha-rani and Vrinda Devi heard his prayers. As soon as he reached the station, he breathed a sigh of relief to find that the train had already left. He decided to go talk to Jai Krsna Das Baba and tell him how it he was prevented from going to Navadvip by Radha-rani and Vrnda Devi.

Jai Krsna Das Baba was already repentant for having asked Chota Baba to go to Navadvip for bringing his *guru-pranalika.* For Vrnda Devi had scolded him in a dream and said, Why did you send him to Navadvip, when his *guru-pranalika* is already there on the altar of Radha- madanamohan. Jai Krsna Das was surprised to find the *guru-pranali* on the altar. He was also happy to see how Vrnda Devi had showered her mercy upon Chota Baba. But there was no end to his grief, when he thought that Chota Baba must have already gone a long way and would soon reach the station in time for the train. He began to pray to Vrnda Devi to see that he did hot reach in time and was compelled to come back. He was praying to Vrnda Devi and weeping, when in the evening he saw to his surprise Chota Baba coming back to his Asrama. He ran towards him and embraced him, before he could obeisance and apologize for not being able to reach Hatharas in time for the train.

There was no end to the happiness of Chota Baba, when he was told about the mercy of Vrnda Devi and her discovery and presentation of his *guru-pranalika.*

He began to practise Raganuga *bhajana* under the guidance of Jai Krsna Das Baba. After some time Jai Krsna Das Baba left the body. Before leaving he said to Chota Baba, Continue your *bhajana.* Do not leave Vraja even for a day. Radharani will surely be pleased and give *darshan.*

Chota Baba continued his *bhajana.* He became old, but he did not have the *darsana* of Radha or Krsna. The pain of separation became unbearable to him.

One day two children, one boy and the other girl, both about six or seven years old, came to the *kuti* of Baba and said, Baba! Give us water to drink.

Baba came out of his *kuti* and saw them. He was charmed by their beauty. He had never seen such beautiful children before. Baba poured water from his *karava* into the cupped hands of the boy and he drank. Then the boy said, Baba! Give water to my wife as well.

Baba laughed and said, You wife! Both of you are so young. You are married!

No Baba, we are not yet married but we shall be married some day.

Oh! You shall be married. But how can she be your wife until you are married?

The boy said, Baba! You do not know. The girl said, No Baba, he is telling a lie.

I came to the forest to pick flowers. He beguiled me by his talks and brought me here.

Baba then began to pour water into her hands imperfectly cupped. The water fell on the ground and she could not drink. Baba said to the boy, Your wife does not know how to cup her hands. She is too simple, said the boy. He put his own cupped hands close to her mouth to make hwe drink and she drank.

Baba then said to the boy, Lala. It is now getting dark. You reach his home.

The boy took the girl by the hand and began to go. Baba continued to look at them like one spell-bound. They also turned repeatedly and looked at Baba with a tricking smile as they went. They had gone only a small distance when they suddenly disappeared. At once a new consciousness dawned upon Baba. He was filled with a strange feeling of bewilderment, happiness and sorrow. He realised that the two children were no other than the two divinities of his heart, whom he had worshipped so long. They had thus appeared, because he had desired that they once drank water from his hands as Krsna had drunk from the hand of Jai Krsna Das. He was happy at their appearance, but unhappy at their disappearance and his inability to recognize them. Their disappearance after appearing only for a short while made him feel the pangs of separation as he had never felt before. He wept and wept till sleep came and gave him relief.

In sleep Radha-Krsna again appeared to him in a dream. They said, Baba! Continue your *bhajana.* In due course we shall give *saksat darsana* to you. Chota Baba must have been blessed by Their *darsana* again, but who knows when and how.

## Chapter 39 - Karta Krsnadas Baba

Karta Krsnadas. Baba had taken initiation from some saint in the line of disciplic succession from Nityanand. Siddha Krsnadas Baba of Govardhan was his Siksa Guru. He became Siddha like him by practising Sadhana under his guidance. He was called Siddha Baba, because he had become Siddha in the *upasana* of *astakalini-lila.* He was called *Gutikavale* Baba, because he had expanded on Bengali on the Sanskrta *gulika* of Siddha Krsnadas Baba of Govardhan and given it the form in which it is available today. He was called *karta,* because he was, on account of his spiritual stature, the natural head or leader of the vaisnava society of his time. All the Sadhus and Vaisnavas of Vraja took instruction from him. They could not do anything, which was not approved by him.

Karta Krsnadas Baba lived for 104 years. He did not let a single second pass without the service of Radha-Krsna or the vaisnavas. He hardly had anytime for his ownself. He did not have time even for Madhukari. He went for Madhukari only once in two or three days. If he had any leisure after doing his *seva-puja,* he spent it in making *gadadi* or copying *gulika* for the Sadhus. But even then though he seemed outwardly to be occupied with these activities, inwardly he was always absorbed in the meditation of the Lila of Radha-Krsna. Sometimes he go do absorbed that he lost outward consciousness and the external activities remained suspended for hours.

Once a *kirtan* singer came from Bengal to serve the Sadhus of Vrndavana through *kirtan.* The Sadhus said, If you can please Karta Baba of Govardhan through your *kirtan*, you should think that you have done some real service. He went to Karta Baba and requested him to hear his *kirtan.* Baba had at that time taken his bath and was going to do *Tilaka* on his body. He said politely, Baba! Where is the time to do *kirtan?*  On his insistence, however, he said, Very well, while I do *Tilaka,* you do *kirtana."*

He started *kirtana.* Baba poured some water on his left hand and began to rub on it the earth of Radhakunda for *tilaka. Kirtana* was about the Lila relating to the first meeting between Radha and Krsna. As the *Kirtaniya* sang Baba saw the Lila enacted before his mind. He was so absorbed in the Lila that in rubbing the earth his hand sometimes moved, sometimes stopped. Ultimately it stopped altogether. Baba went into a stupor and his mind was totally fixed on the Lila. The *kirtana* began at 8 o clock in the morning and continued till 4 o clock in the afternoon. When the *kirtana* stopped, *tilaka* still remained undone.

The saints of Vraja of that time used to send their disciples to Karta Krsnadas Baba for training in *asta-kalina-lila-smarana*,But Baba did not give training to everyone. He trained only those who had risen above the ordinary level and acquired control over the senses. Therefore before accepting anyone for training he kept him in his Asrama for some time in order to test his eligibility.

Once a Sadhu came to him and requested for Siksa in *Astakalina-lila-smarana.* He received him well and gave him a place to stay in his Asrama. At night he gave him to eat pieces of bread which he had received in Madhukari three days before, after soaking them in water to make them soft. Then he went and sat elsewhere to watch his reaction from a distance. He could understand from his facial expression that he was not eligible for *astakalina-lila-smarana.* He came back and pretending to have given him the stale bread by mistake, said, Oh! What have I done?! I have given you the stale bread. He gave him fresh bread to eat and himself ate the stale.

After sometime he said to him, You are still the slave of your tongue. As long as a man is atached to the world of senses and is given to sense enjoyment, he is not qualified for Raganuga *bhajana.* You should continue to practise Vaidhi Bhakti. By the practise of Vaidhi Bhakti when your heart is purified and your desire for sense enjoyment is completely eliminated, you should come to me for *Raganuga- bhajana.*

So long as Siddha Krishnadas Baba of Govardhan was alive Siddha Karta Krsnadas Baba lived in Govardhan near the *bhajana-kuti* of Sanatan Gosvami in the bank of Manasi ganga. The place, where he lived, is even today called Siddha Baba-ki-thaura, after his name as Siddha Baba. After the passing away of Saddha Krsnadas Baba of Govardhan, Karta Krsnadas Baba shifted to Nutan Ghere in Radhakund.

Once the ownership of Radhakund became a subject of dispute between the Bengal Sadhus of Radhakunda and the Vrajabasis. The Vrajavasis said, We shall not let the Bengali Sadhus touch the water of Radhakund. The Bengali Sadhus went to Karta Baba and asked him what they should do. He replied, We. shall not fight with the Vrajavasis. If Radharani does not want that we should not touch the Kund, we shall not.

The Sadhus obeyed. On the Krsna Astami day of the month of Kartika bath in Radhakund has special spiritual importance. On that day Baba became restless for bath in Radhakund. He sat on the bank of Radhakund near the Samadhi of Raghunatha Das Gosvami and said prayerfully to Radharani, "Merciful Radhey! I know that it must be on account of some offense committed by us that You have deprived us of the privilege of bathing in Your kund. But if today You kindly arrange somehow to send me only a handful of water from the kund I would sprinkle it on my body and be blest.

At that time came from village Kunjar eight or ten Vrajavasis with staff in their hands. While they were taking off their clothes to bathe in Radhakund, they saw Baba sitting aide and shedding tears. They said, Baba! Why are you sitting aside and shedding tears? The moon has come up and it is time to bathe.

Baba told them about the dispute with the Vrajavasis and their resolve not to let the Bengali Sadhus bath in Radhakund. The shouted together, Baba! You must bathe. We shall see who prevents you from bathing.

Baba refused to bathe. But they forcibly took him into the Kund and made him bathe. The Vrajavasis of Radhakund raised much hue and cry, but they could not do anything, because they feared the Vrajavais of Kunjar. After the Vrajavasis of Kunjar had gone the Sadhus apprehended that the Vrajavasis of Radhakund might start violence. But the same night Radharani said to the Vrajavasis in a dream, Radhakund is my Kund. The Sadhus also are My own. Who are you to prevent them from bathing?

Next morning the Vrajavasis spoke to one another about the dream each one had seen.

They understood that they had committed an offense against Radharani and the Bengali Sadhus. They went and fell at the feet of Karta Baba and prayed for pardon.

Karta Krsnadas baba lived in Nutan Ghere for 10 or 12 years. After which he passed away. His Samadhi is there.

## Chapter 40 - Śrī Navalakishor Baba

There exists in Nandagram in Vraja, the temple of Nandalal, the son of Nand.It was in a very dilapidated condition about hundred and fifty years ago. The verandah in front of the temple was uneven and rugged. Nandalal thought that if the floor was reconstructed and made of smooth marble. He could be able easily to move about and play in it.

This was a trifling matter for Nandalal. For He was the Lord and the creator and destroyer of the universe. His *Lila-sakhi* Yogamaya could fulfil His desire as soon as it appeared in His mind But Yogamaya knew that her Lord had appeared in this incarnation not as the Lord of the universe, but as the son of Nand and Yasoda, as the friend of Sudam and Sudam and the other cowherds and as the beloved of *gopis* to revel with them in love. His purpose in this incarnation was only to love and be loved. Therefore in this incarnation His lordliness *(aisvarya)* was completely enveloped by His sweetness *(madhurya)* or loveliness and He depended for the fulfilment of His desires not on Yogamaya, but on the loving service of His devotees. Accordingly He began to wait for a suitable occasion where He could ask or inspire a devotee to do marble flooring in the verandah of the temple.

In village Chandhari-ka-nagala, in district Alavar of Rajasthan there lived a Brahamin devotee, whose name was Navalakishor. He was initiated in Ramanuja Sampradaya and he worshipped Visnu. His mother Srimati Hira Devi was a devotee of Nandalal. Nandalal was very kind to her. Once, when she fell down from a roof, He took her in His lap and saved her from suffering any injury. After this she shifted to Nandagram and began to live there with Navalakishor and his wife. She had lived in Nandagram only for a few months, when Nandalal made her leave her physical body to accept her in His service in His transcendental *dham.*

Navalakishor already had strong *samskaras* of Bhakti, but after the death of his mother his Bhakti and Vairajnya became more intense. He went back to his village for a short while and returned after selling away his property and everything he had. He spent the money he brought in the service of the Sadhus and vaisnavas.

One day he said to his wife, There are many thieves in Nandagram. They have an eye on your ornaments. Give me the ornaments so that I may keep then in safe custody somewhere. She gave the ornaments to him. After sometime she asked him where he had kept the ornaments. He replied, They have gone to the treasury of Nandalal. She understood that the ornaments had also been sold and utilized in the service of vaisnavas. She did not mind, because she was also a devoted lady.

Navalakishor s Vairagna went on increasing. He sent his wife to her fathers house, took vaisnava Sannyas and began to live on Madhukari. No one knew his new name as a Sannyasa. Therefore people began to call him Navalakishor Baba.

Since Navalakishor Baba was a Brahamin, he had strong *samaskaras* of purity-impurity, touchability and untouchability. Therefore he did not accept cooked food in Madhukari. He accepted only food grains or flour. He cooked himself and ate after offering the food to Visnu. While cooking he took special care to see that not even the shadow of any other person fall upon the food he cooked.

It is the habit of Nandalal to regard anyone related to His devotee as His own, whether he has any love or regard for Him or not. Navalakishor was the son of His devotee Hira Devi. Therefore he was as much His own as Hira Devi. He thought of asking him to make the marble floor in the temple. But He felt hesitant for two reasons. One reason was that although He regarded him as His own, he did not recognize any relationship with Him. He often went to His temple for His *darsana* because he lived in Nandagram, but he did not worship Him as his Ista. His Ista was Visnu The other reason was that Navalakishor was too much obsessed by the Brahamamcal rules and regulations of purity and impurity which were repugnant to Raganuga Bhakti of Vraja, in which the governing factor was love and not any rules and regulations of purity or impurity

Therefore He thought of a device to first bring about complete change in Navalakishor in respect of both of these factors, which blocked the way of a loving relationship between Him and Navalakishor.

One day Navalakishor went to the house of a Vrajavasi woman for Madhukari She went in to bring flour Her two sons sitting in the verandah outside, were eating *madheri* from a pot. Navalakishor saw that Krsna and Balarama were also eating with them They were eating with great relish from the same pot and looking jeeringly at him. He was surprised and stunned He stood looking at them like one hypnotized

The woman came with flour and said stretching her hand towards Baba, Take Baba But Baba had lost outward consciousness under the spell of the beauty of Krsna and Balaram He could neither see the woman nor hear her voice She said in a louder tone, Baba! Why are you looking at my children like this? Take *Madhukari* and go .

Baba then looked at her with a start. After thinking for a while he said, Ma! Not this flour, give me a little *maheri* from the pot from which the children are eating.

What? said the lady surprisingly. Have you gone mad? On other days you did not accept anything cooked or touched by my children and today you are asking for the remains of the *maheri* eaten by them!

It was natural for the lady to feel surprised. How could she know that during the short interval between her going and coming out Baba Navalakishor was completely metamorphosed He was no more the staunch Brahamin, who regarded anything touched even by the shadow of another man as impure and did not eat even the *prasada* of a deity if it was cooked by another man. He had now understood that the Thakura (God) of Vraja valued the purity of the heart, not the purity of things offered to Him He was the God of love and could be won over only by love, not by the observance of the rigid rules and regulations of purity and impurity of things.

Baba said, Ma! I have not gone mad. My madness has now disappeared. Now no not hesitate. Give me *maheri.*  As he said this his throat was choked and tears rolled down his eyes.

The lady thought Baba had truly gone mad. She kept standing and looking at him. But he jumped and took a handful of *maheri* out of the pot. He was tastefully eating *maheri* and swimming in the ocean of bliss. Tears of love and joy were coursing down his cheeks and drenching his chest.

After this Baba Navalakishor s upasnatook a new turn. There was a volcanic disturbance in his heart. He began to think, I worshipped Visnu so long, He did not give me *darsana* even once. But this Thakura of Vraja blessed me with His *darsana* even though I never worshipped Him. How kind, simple and merciful is He! In the *upasana* of Visnu there is an element of awe. He wants to be revered and worshipped as God. But Nandalal in not even conscious of His divinity. He wants to love and be loved. He is not God, but Love. Visnu sits on His altar, accepts from a distance the *bhoga* offered by His devotee and leaves His remains for him. But the Thakur of Vraja goes from door to door in Vraja, eats the remains of the Vrajavasis.

Navalakishor found that the seat in his heart, which was so far occupied by Visnu had been captured by Nandalal. Even when he eanted to meditate on Visnu, Nandalal came up in his mind, not Visnu. He now worshipped Visnu only outwardly. Inwardly he always thought of Nandalal.

One day, when Navalakishor was meditating in the courtyard of the temple of Nandalal, he saw that Nandalal was moving about in the verandah of the temple on all flours. From time to time He stopped and began to rub His knees with His hand While meditating Navalakishor fell into a nap. He heard in a dream Nandalal saying to him, Baba! My knees are bruised.

This was unbearable to Navalakishor. He decided that he would beg or borrow and have the floor of the temple paved with smooth marble as soon as possible The next day he went to Makarana in Jodhpur state and begged from a big merchant there for marble stones for the temple of Nandalal. The merchant said, Take as many as you like. Nandalal asked me also in a dream yesterday to give the stones to you

During those days there was no railway line from Jodhpur to Mathura. The stones had to be brought on bullock carts. Very soon the temple of Nandalal including the verandah, were paved with marble On one of the walls of the temple was inscribed a verse describing how Nandalal inspired Navalakishor to pave the floor of the temple with marble

After this Navalakishor Baba became famous as a Siddha Baba Ji, who enjoyed the favour of Nandalal and had the power to fulfil the desires of people by his blessings. He used these powers sometimes for the service of Nandalal Once he went to Mathura with his disciple Jugalakishor. He asked Jugalakishor to go and beg for some *ghee* for the service of his Thakura. Jugalakishor went to a shop-keeper and begged for *ghee* The shopkeeper gave *ghee,* but said, I have no child. Your *gurudeva* is a Siddha Mahatma. Request him on my behalf to bless me with a son

Jugalakishor was my nature too simple and generous. He said, Well, well, I will tell him. You will have a son by his blessings. He reported everything to Navalakishor Navalakishor was angry He struck him with tongs on his back and said, Fool! You trade in my blessings. Go away from here. I do not want to see your face .

Jugalakishor fell at his feet. He begged for forgiveness and said, I shall never do that again I shall go to the shop-keeper, return his *ghee* and say, My *gurudeva* will not do anything for you

Navalakishor s anger subsided, but he said, Now since you have promised Nandala will certainly bless the shop-keeper with a son But the shop-keeper has cheated you He has given a little *ghee* and taken a son. Now go and tell him that he will have to give rose-water to Nandalal everyday

The shop-keeper was happy to hear this. From the same say he started offering rosewater to Nandalal. In due course he had a son.

After the birth of the son that shop-keeper started a new firm for selling stones and other materials, The firm was named Nandalakishor Jugalakishor. The firm exists and is known by this name even today,

Seth Bhagavan Das Berivala of Hariyana was a wealthy merchant. He suffered great loss and his business was ruined. He went to Nandgaon, described his condition to Baba Navalakishor and prayed for his mercy. Baba said, If you render service to Nandalal, your condition will improve. He began to serve Nandalal and his condition began to improve. The members of his family have been rendering service to Nandalal till this day His grandson Ratan Lal Berival renovated the temple of Nandalal and gave it its present form Navalakishor used to prepare for Nandalal ten ladduseveryday with his own hands. Eight of them were kept for Nandalal s breakfast the next day and two were kept with Ma Yasoda to be given to Him by her, if He felt hungry at night. This practise, which was started by Nandakishor continues even today.

Navalakishor had *vatsalya-bhava* (parental attitude) towards Nandalal, but it changed into *madhura-bhava.* This is evident from one of his verses inscribed on the wall of the temple of Nandalal.

Navalakishor Baba s *samadhi* exists at Asesvara in Nandagaon, where he used to do *bhajana.* On the Samadhi its date is inscribed as Vikrama 1937, which means 1880 of the Christian era.

## Chapter 41 - Pandit Jagadanand Das Baba Ji

Śrī Jagadanand Das Baba was a contemporary of Siddha Śrī Jai Krsna Das Baba of Kamyavan and Siddha Śrī Krsnadas Baba of Govardhan. He was born in village of Faridpur District of East Bengal. At the early age of 18 or 19 he took Diksa and Vesa from Śrī Bhagavan Das Baba Ji of the same district and went to Vrndavana. It is not known when, where and from whom he received his education. But he was acknowledged as the biggest Pandit of his time. Many Pandits received education from him. The famous Pandit and *kirtan* singer Śrī Advait Das Baba also studied under him.

He was very intimate with JaiKrsna Das Baba, Krsna Das Baba and Madhusudan Das Baba enjoyed the love wrangles he used to have with him.

Jagadanand Das had absolute faith in the transcendental character of Vraja. The holy *dhama* once gave him a direct experience of this. When living in Radhakund one day in the evening he started for Suryakund to meet Madhusudan Das Baba. Suryakund is three or four miles to the North of Radhakund. The distance can be easily covered within an hour. But he reached Suryakund the next day in the morning. He neither lost his way, nor stopped anywhere. On seeing him Madhusudan Das said, Where are you coming from so early in the morning?

Morning?! Is it morning? I started only a little while ago from Radhakund.

Madhusudan Das Baba said, This *dhama* has mercifully given you a demonstration of its capacity to contract and expand like lotus. You are well versed in the Sastras. You know how space and time here are controlled by Lila-Sakti of Bhagavan and contract or expand according to the requirements of Lila.

## Chapter 42 - Śrī Gulab Das Baba

SriJugal Das Baba Ji was the Mahantof the old temple of Madanamohan, the deity once worshipped by Sanatan Gosvami. He was born in district Gaya of the Bihar province. He was illiterate, but pure and simple at heart. He had dedicated his life to the service of vaisnavas. Early in the morning after taking his bath he made obeisance to all the vaisnavas staying in his Asrama including his own disciples. If some one asked him why he bowed down to his disciples, he replied, I bow down to their vaisnava *svarupa* or form. If I do not do it how will they learn?

The doors of Baba s Asrama were always open to the vaisnavas. Whenever they came they were provided with food and shelter. From morn till night Baba used to be engaged in the service of the vaisnavas because he believed that Krsna was pleased by the service of His devotees more than by His own service. On appropriate occasions feasts and festivals were also held in his Asrama. This involved a lot of expenditure, No one knew where the money came from. The Asrama did not have any fixed source of income Baba purchased provisions and the other things required for the service of the Vaishnavas on credit and paid for them, when the money came. "The shop-keepers were always paid sooner or later Therefore they freely gave him what he needed.

Once, however, the debt to shop-keepers mounted to rupees two thousand five hundred The money did not come from anywhere. Baba fell in anxiety One day a man came and said, SethGovind Das has come from Bombay He is very much interested in the service of the vaisnavas He has come to know that in your Asrama the service of the vaisnavas goes on continuously on account of which you have run into a debt of rupees two thousand five-hundred He has sent this money so that you may clear the debt

The man kept the purse containing two thousand five hundred rupees before Baba. Baba kept looking at him for some time with amazement. Then he asked, Where is that Seth?

The man replied, He is staying near Srngarabat. He will come for your *darsana* in the evening He then made obeisance to Baba and went Baba waited for a long time for the Seth in the evening He did not come Then he sent his man to enquire about him in different places in the neighbourhood of Srngaravat, but he could not be found anywhere.

Who was Seth Govind Das? Who was the man, who brought the purse containing money? The whole thing remained shrouded in' mystery. Jugal Das Baba often thought who could that man be except Seth Govind Das himself? And who could he munificent Seth Govind Das be except Govind Ji Himself. Whenever this thought came in his mind his body shivered and tears trickled down his eyes.

Once a policeman saw a wood-cutter carrying to Vrndavana a lot of fuel-wood on twenty donkeys. He suspected that the wood was stolen. He asked the wood-cutter to drive the donkeys to the police-outpost.

Why? asked the woodcutter.

The wood is stolen.

No, this wood is for Jugl Das Baba. He has purchased it for a festival.

You are telling a lie. I shall inquire from Baba. Come along with me.

He went to Jugal Das Baba with the woodcutter He said, Baba! Is this wood for you? Did you order it?

Baba was actually preparing for a festival, which was to be held the next day. He had purchased everything except wood. The store from which he used to purchase fuel-wood has refused to supply because he had not yet been able to pay for the earlier purchases. He was therefore in anxiety. When the policeman asked him this question, he heard some one whispering into his ear, Say yes. I had ordered. Baba repeated these words. Turning to the woodcutter he pointed out a corner in the Asrama and said, Pile up the wood there.

The policeman was satisfied. After the woodcutter had delivered the wood, Baba said to him, Come after three or four days and take your money.

The wood-cutter said, Baba! You need not bother about money. The wood was stolen. You have saved me from the police That is more than the price of the wood

After the wood-cutter had gone some distance, he disappeared with the donkeys. Who was the wood-cutter? Was He not the same Person, Who had come in the form of Seth Govind Das? He could not be the same, one might think, because He would not lie and steal or do anything immoral. But don t the scriptures say that there is nothing moral or immoral, which He cannot do for His devotees?

Jugal Das Baba served the Vaishnavas all his life. He never himself accepted the service of any vaisnava. He passed away suddenly in 1941 at the age of 90. At that time also he did not accept any one's service But Govind Ji served him whenever he was in difficulty. He could not help it.

## Chapter 43 - Gvariya Baba

During the days of the British rule in India there were a number of independent states in the country, ruled by Rajas. Datiya was one of them. The Raja of Datiya was fond of crocodiles. He had kept a number of crocodiles in a large pond in the state. A watchman kept a watch on the pond and prevented people from getting into it. One day he was surprised to see a young Baba Ji standing inside the pond. He shouted, O Baba! Get out of the pond. Don t you see the crocodiles there. They will devour you. Get out atonce. Baba remained standing there calm and quiet and motionless. He either did not hear the warning or did not heed it. Also he either remained unaware or unmindful of the danger from the crocodiles.

The watchman shouted repeatedly and said, If you do not come out, I shall go and report to the Raja and you will be punished Still the Baba seemed to turn a deaf ear to him

The watchman went to the Raja and told him everything. The Raja came with some of his men. He asked the Baba to come out and threatened to punish him if he did not. His threats also went unheeded. The Raja was surprised at his obduracy and fearlessness. His curiosity was very much aroused to see that the Baba stood there unmoved and undisturbed like a man in Samadhi and the crocodiles swam round him, but did not even touch him. He asked some of his men to remain there and bring him to his palace, when he came out of the pond.

The young Baba became a common subject of talk in the town Some said that he was mad, some said he had for some reason or other become desperate with life and wanted to end it by throwing himself up to the crocodiles. Some, however, said that he was a Mahapurusa, as indicated by the lustre he had on his face.

When Baba came out of the pond he was taken to the Raja The Raja did not take long to recognize that he was actually a Mahapurusha. He treated him well and insisted that he should remain in Datiya at least for some time to bless him and his people Baba stayed on.

The young Baba Ji was born in 1843 in a Brahamin family of Bundel Khand He had strong *Samsakaras* of Bhakti His parents apprehended that he might renounce the world Therefore they married him to a beautiful girl when he was only sixteen years old But marriage could not keep him tied to the world for long He renounced the world, when he was still young and began to roam about here and there in search of Krsna He used to call Him Yara, which means a bosom friend. It is not known whether he had taken initiation from anyone or not. But he had known that Krsna loved to be the friend of a man, who loved Him and no one else and desired his friendship and nothing else Since he had renounced everything with the sole desire of realizing Him as his friend or *Yara* He was confident that Krsna had already accepted him as His. friend and must be as anxious to meet him as he was. Krsna had already entered his mind and sat there. He did not let him think of anything other than Him and did not let him do anything except that which pleased Him He knew that He was pleased by music So he learnt music and became a great singer Though Krsna was always in his mind, he hoped that he would also some day see Him face to face and enjoy His company. He went about from place to place always thinking of Him and chanting His name. He was so much absorbed in His thought that sometimes he forgot all about himself and remained sitting or standing in the same posture for hours in the. state of Samadhi.

In course, of his ramblings he reached Datiya. Hi association brought about a complete change in the mind of the Raja of Datiya. He passed most of his time in *bhajana-kirtana* and the company of Gvariya Baba. Datiya itself was charged with a current of Bhakti, it had never experienced before.

Neither the Raja nor the people of Datiya wanted that Gvariya Baba should ever leave Datiya. But Baba must go to Vrndavana, where he thought his *yara* must be anxiously waiting for him.

He quietly sneaked out of Datiya on night and went to Vrndavana. In Vrndavana he went from forest to forest and *kunja* to *kunja* in search of his *Yara,* but could not find Him. He rolled in the dust of Vrndavana and wept and cried, O Krsna, O my friend, my heart, my soul! Where are you? Sometimes in imagination he had as vivid experience of Him as in actual perception. Then he ran towards Him, but He disappeared and Baba fell senseless on the ground. He could no more bear His separation. In utter desperation and resentment he gave up the search. He said to himself, I have done my best to find Him. He seems to eluding me. I will no more bother about Him. Let Him find me, if He cares for my friendship. Friendship is a two- way traffic, not one.

He gave up the search and began to pass his time in witnessing Rasa-lilaand training the actors of Rasa-lila in musical performances, which are an essential part of Rasa-lila. He hoped that his resentment and show of indifference towards Him would have the desired effect and He would come. But this hope was also belied. There was then no end to his anger. He said to Him in his mind, I had heard that You were kind-hearted and loving. If one advanced even a single step towards You, You readily moved ten steps forward ta meet and embrace him. But this was a lie. I have now discovered that You are most unkind and cruel. Friendship with You means only frustration and suffering. So no more friendship with You If You are proud of being the prince of Nandagram, I am also proud of being a master of my ownself. I vow that if I ever come cross You, I shall not even look at You.

In resentment Gvariya Baba stopped chanting His name. He also stopped going to Rasa-lila and the temples. Not only this, he sometimes stood at the gate of a temple and tried to dissuade people from going in for *darsana.* He said to them, "Bretheren Listen, if you do not want to lose your happiness, do not have anything to do with Krsna He is most untrustworthy and unkind. He revels only with the *gopis* of Vraja and cares little for anyone else.

Śrī Krsna was thus deprived of the *darsana* of His dear friend, who had become unfriendly and stopped going to the temples He feared that He might also be deprived of the *darsana* of His other devotees, if they came under his influence. This was too much for Him to bear. He felt that He must do something about it.

One day at dusk when Gvariya Baba was roaming about in Ramanreti, he saw a group of cows coming out of he forest. Shortly after he saw the environment becoming aglow with the blue lustre of the body of a handsome cowherd and heard the sweet notes of His flute. He wore a peacock feather on His crown. His curly hair were bedecked with flowers and He had a bewitching smile on His lips.

Oh! This is Yara! Shouted Baba and a shiver went through his spine. He forgot his vow not to look at Him. With eyes wide open and swimming in tears of love he kept gazing at Him for some time like one in the state of drunken stupor. Krsna also continued to look at him with eyes full of deep affection. Then both locked each other in long, deep and loving embrace. After sometime Krsna said, Gvariya! Why have you come so late? I have all along been restlessly waiting for you.

This aroused the injured self-conceit of Baba. Stop it, he said atonce. If You were really anxious to meet me could You not come, You could not because You are the prince of Vraj, I a poor cowherd. What have You to do with me? He said this and walked away.

Krsna said, Gvariya! When shall we meet again?

You can come and meet whenever You want.

I do not go anywhere out of Vraja.

This marked the beginning of a new chapter in the friendship between the two cowherds.

They met often and revelled in humourous talks, often full of sarcasm and satire and plays of different kinds. Sometimes they lovingly served each other, sometimes they quarrelled. Frolicsome Krsna sometimes met him in disguise.

Once Gvariya Baba had gone to Vairata, in Jaipur state with Sen Raghunath Das and Śrī Chanthamal his friends They returned to Nandagram at about 10 P. M They were tired so they lay down to sleep without eating anything. At the same time came a boy with a pot full of milk and said, Baba! Ramakali has sent this milk. Baba was surprised How could Ramakali get the information about his arrival so soon?! He took the milk. He and his two friends drank the milk. The milk had a supernatural taste Though small in quantity, it made them feel full to satiety.

The next day Baba went to the house of Ramkali to return the milk pot. He said, Ramakali! Here is your milk pot. The milk you sent was very tasteful.

Ramakeli was stupified. She said, Baba! When did I send the milk? This pot is not mind. It is perhaps the one I often see in the temple of NandaIaI.

Baba then went to Nandalal s temple As soon as Kishan Lal, the pujari of the temple saw the pot in Baba s hand, he said, Baba! Why did you take away this pot? I have been searching it since morning Yesterday night I forgot to take it home and left it after offering milk to Nandalal. Gvariya Baba understood that it was Nandalal Himself Who had gone to him with the milk. He kept looking at the Śrī Vigraha (Image) of Nandalal in the temple for some time while tears of love trickled down his eyes.

Once on the occasion of the Holi festival Baba was staying at the house of Gosvami Kishan Lal in Nandagram. He purchased four kilogram *gulabajamun* At the same time came two Vrajavasi boys, who used to play the part of Krsna and Balaram in Rasa-lila They said, Baba! We are hungry. Give us something to eat. Baba made them both sit in his lap and fed them the *gulabajamuns* with his own hand After the boys had eaten to their fill then said, Baba! No more, we have had our fill.

The next day Baba invited all the actors of the Rasa-lila group to which the boys belonged. Those boys also came. Baba fed everyone with *gulabjamuns,* but he said to these boys, I will not feed you today, because you were fed yesterday.

The boys retorted, Baba! When did we come to you yesterday? We were not even aware of your arrival. If you do not want to feed us don t, but why tell a lie?

Kishan Lal and others who had seen Baba feeding the boys that day said, Boys! It is not Baba, but you who are speaking a lie. We saw Baba feeding you yesterday with our own eyes.

Upon this the Swamiof the Rasa-lila party and the other boys of the group exclaimed, These boys were all along with us yesterday How could they have come here?

Baba understood that not those boys but Krsna-Balaram had on their guise come and sat in his lap and eaten the *gulabjamuns* from his own hand. As Baba realized this, he was overwhelmed with *bhava.* Tears streamed out of his eyes. His body shivered and he became unconscious. On regaining outward consciousness he made those boys sit in his lap and fed them as he had fed Krsna-Balaram.

Gvariya Baba was as frolicsome as his Yara and he sometimes surprised people and made them laugh by what he did. When he had lived in Vrndavana for many years, the Raja of Daliya came to know that he was in Vrndavana He came to Vrndavana and humbly requested him to go to Datiya with him. He refused. But when the Raja insisted too much, he said, Very well, I shall go. The date was fixed. That day in the morning Baba went to the Raja and said, I am ready. Let us go.

The Raja was surprised to see Baba on the back of a donkey with his face painted black He said, Baba! Why have you come in this shape? What has happened to you?

Baba replied, This happens to a man who comes to Vrndavana. the holy land of Krsna and then thinks of going elsewhere.

The Raja fell at Baba s feet and said, Baba!

I will never insist that you go to Daliya. I have committed an offense by insisting that you should go to Daliya with me. Kindly forgive me. Maharaja Madhu Sinha of Jaipur started building the famous Jaipur temple in Vrndavana. Gvariya Baba also began to work with the labourers. Out of the wages he got, he purchased parched gram and distributed it amongst the labourers. Once the Maharaja came to see how the construction was going on. He saw a tall, stout person with lustrous looks working among the labourers with greater enthusiasm than others. On coming near he recognize him as Gvariya Baba. He was surprised. He said, Baba! How are you here? You must not do this work. Baba said, Why? You serve the Lord with money, I serve with my body. Who are you to interfere?

The Maharaja felt ashamed. He said, Baba!

I shall be happy if you kindly visit Jaipur. Yara does not want that I should leave Vrndavana. Replied Baba.

Maharaja said, You have been invited by Govind Ji.He had sent me to invite you.

If He has invited, I shall go. How can I refuse to accept His invitation.

He went to Jaipur with the Maharaja. After a few days he fell ill. The royal physician began to treat him. During this period he wrote a letter to Govardhan Lal Ji, the Tikayalaof Nathadvara, saying, I have captured a lion.

You prepare a cage for him. I will go to Nathadvar with the lion.

He said to the Maharaja, I will now go to Nathadvara. The Maharaja said, You are ill. Go when you get well. But he did not listen. Therefore the physician gave him medicine for a month. He took the entire medicine in a single dose and said, Now I must get well. Surprisingly he became allright the same day.

He reached Nathadvara. The Tikayale said, Baba! Where is the lion?

Baba said, Where is the cage?

The Tikayala showed him the cage. Baba went into the cage sat there. He said, The lion is in the cage.

While in Nathadvara Gvariya Baba was free and fearless like a lion. He did what he liked. No one could say anything to him. One day he saw Damodar Lal Ji, the son of Tikayala wearing coat, pant and tie. He called him. When he came near he gave him a slap and said, Is this the traditional dress of the family to which you belong?

Then he went to the jail and said to the jailer, I have committed an offense. Put me in jail. The jailer was taken aback. He could not obey, but he also did not have the courage to disobey. He stood meekly before him with folded hands, not knowing what to do. But Baba entered into the jail and began to Jive like a prisoner.

When the Tikayala came to know about it, he went to the jail He said to Baba, Baba! You did well. You have taught a good lesson to my son. Now kindly come out of he jail.

Baba came out and left for Vrndavana. In Vrndavana he stayed in the Asrama of Siddha Śrī Ramakrsna Das Pandit Baba. Once he went to Barasana. One day, he kept sitting there in a garden till late at night, lost in the thought of his Yara. He heard some thieves planning to commit theft in the house of a Gosvami. He went to them and said, I am also a thief. I shall go with you and participate in the theft. The thieves agreed. They entered the house of the Gosvami with Baba. They stared collecting the things, which they thought were worth stealing. Baba happened to see the deity Gopala, sleeping in a small temple in the house. He went near the deity and said, Yara! I have come to Your house. You should entertain me, but You are asleep. Now get up and give me something to eat.

Gopal was in a deep sleep. He did not get up. Baba said. You will not get up like this. He picked up the bell in the temple and began to strike it hard. The thieves left everything they had collected and ran away.

For sometime Baba lived in Mathura and taught music to a person, named Mungamal. There was in Mathura a haunted house, called Bhuta-mahal (the haunted castle), which had been lying vacant for number of years. The ghost in it likked everyone else who came to live in it. Baba hired the house for Rs 50/- per month. He asked Mungamal to get the house cleaned by someone. Mungamal tried to find some one, who could do it and promised to pay him handsomely, but no one agreed. With great difficulty Baba made a labourer agree by assuring him that he would also do the cleaning with him. The house was cleaned and Baba began to live in it.

One day Baba said to Mungamal, I am teaching music to the ghost in the house. You may come along with me and hear if you want.

Mungamal went with Baba. As soon as Baba brought out. his harmonium and kept it one the floor of a room it began to be played and the room resounded with its sound. Mungamal was frightened. Therefore Baba asked the ghost to stop playing. The ghost obeyed.

The ghost wanted to be delivered from his birth as a ghost. Baba performed a *yajna* and he was delivered. The haunted house no more remained haunted.

After this episode Baba became famous in Mathura and crowds of people, mostly women, started coming to him. They became a problem. He thought of a strange device to solve it. He went to Panna, the most beautiful prostitute of Mathura and said, If you agree to go out for a drive in the city with me for a few days,

I shall reward you handsomely. She greed. The people of Mathura saw. Baba driving with her in a carriage every evening for a few days. They stopped going to him, because they thought he was licentious. Did this make any difference to Baba? He had risen to a plane which was much above the reach of Maya Praise and blame, lust and licentiousness had no meaning for him. Siddha saints like him live in the world of Maya, without being touched by Maya, just as lotus stands in muddy water without being touched by it.

An example of Gvariya Baba s complete detachment from the world is provided by another episode. He once hired a room in Brahmachari Ji s temple in Vrndavana He decorated it with chandeliers of different colours, hanging from the roof, big glasses fixed to the walls and costly carpets spread on the floor He sat on the carpet, resting against a big velvety pillow and ressed in silk. On day while he thus sat in his room, his friend Giridarval happened to come. He was surprised to see Baba in this shape He said, Baba! What is this?! What has happened to you?

I have become a Raja, replied Baba.

The next day he invited the Vrajavasis and asked them to take away everything in the room. The looted everything and enjoyed the loot. Who can say why Baba performed this Lila? Was it because he wanted to impress upon people the trifling character of everything belonging to this world so that they might practise detachment?

Complete detachment from the world is possible only when one is wholly surrendered and attached only to the Lord. The extent to which Gvariya Baba was attached to the Lord appears from what happened to him once at the time of the *abhiseka* (bathing ceremony) of the deity in Jatipura. The Gosvami first poured *ghee* on the deity, then medicinal water and other things. At the end he poured sugar on Him in such a way that He was completely covered by it. Then he started pouring water drop by drop. As and when water made the sugar melt and some part of the body of the deity became visible the devotees watching the ceremony shouted, Quick, quick! Yara is feeling suffocation! He shouted in a manner which showed that he was himself feeling suffocation and was about to die on account of breathlessness. The Gosvami then poured good quantity of water atonce sugar was instantaneously removed from the face of Gopal. Baba then heaved a sigh of relief. He said to the Gosvami, Do not do this again, for the deity has life and breathes.

One day Baba called any people for a meeting in Ranga Ji s temple in Vrndavana. No one knew what the purpose of the meeting was. When everyone had come, Baba said, Bretheren! I have called you today so that you may have a condolence meeting on my death. Everyone laughed to hear this, because he though that Baba had played a big joke. But it was not a joke. Baba insisted on their having a condolence meeting. He said, Suppose that I am dead and you are here for a meeting to condole on my death. You will condole on my death when I really die. If you do it now, I will also see it. So do it now. Do not do it after my death. On his insistence the condolence meeting was duly held. Several people spoke and expressed their condolences. Baba enjoyed their speeches.

After they had spoken Baba said, Bretheren! You peed not be sorry at my death. Death is only to the body not to me I am under order of transfer. My Yara has transferred me on promotion, from a place where my meeting with Him are casual to a place where the meetings will be eternal So instead of condoling on my death, rejoice at my transfer and bid me farewell on the eve of my departure.

Only a few days after this Gvariya Baba left his physical body and departed from this world in a transcendental body to meet his Yara and remain with Him eternally in His eternal abide, the transcendental Vrndavana.

## Chapter 44 - Durlabh Das Baba Ji

During the days when Manohar Das Baba lived and practised bhajana on the bank of Govind Kunda in Govardhan, there lived on the Northern bank of the Kunda Durlabh Das Baba, who was also engaged in bhajana. He did Nama-japa all day and night and never left it even for a moment except for a short while when he slept. Manohar Das Baba once told his disciples how he conquered death by the power of Harinama

It was the time when plaque broke out in Vrindavana One day at noon, when Durlabh Das was sitting under the shade of a neem tree and doing Harinama-japa, a gigantic, but shadowy looking figure came and began to circumambulate him. He said, Who are you? Why have you come? "

I am Kala-duta (the Regent of Death). I have come to take you, replied the figure.

I am ready Take me. Calmly said Durlabh Das

You set aside the rosary and give up Harinama, I cannot take you while you are doing the japa of Harinama, said the Kala duta in helplessness.

Durlabh Das said I shall not give up Harinama.

The Kaladuta had to go back. In the evening, when Manohar Das Baba came out of the cave, Durlabh Das' told him about it. Manohar Baba said, You were destined to die at this time. Death was inevitable You have been able to ward it off on account of your bhajana.

## Chapter 45 - Anant Das Baba Ji

In Vraja there is a village called Karahala The village is famous because it has the Samadhi of Vajranath, the grandson of Śrī Krsna. On the bank of a pond in the village there is a *kuti* surrounded by a cluster of kadamba trees and covered by creepers all over The *kuti* is so thickly covered by the trees, the bushes and the creepers that the rays of the sun can hardly penetrate into it. It is an ideal place for the birds, snakes and scorpions. It is hard to imagine that a man can live in it. But only a few years before there lived in it a saint, whose name was Anant Das. He lived undisturbed in the *kuti* and practised *bhajana* after taking initiation and Vesa from Advait Das Baba.

Like Durbahl Das Baba, whose mention we have already made, he also provides an excellent example of the conquest of Harinama over death. He was intensely devoted to Harinama. He did not let a moment pass without Harinama. He could not live without Harinama even as long as he could remain without breath. He did Harinama japa in the *kuti* throughout the day. In the evening only for a short while he went out for Madhukari. Day after day, month after month arid year after year passed like this, doing about four or five lac Harinama everyday It is said that he was so much dedicated to Harinam that Harinama had become his life and soul and one could hear the sound of Harinama coming out of every pore of his body

One day, when he was returning from Madhukari and was about to enter his *kuti,* a cobra, who was lying at the door, hissed to prevent him from coming near. But how could he hear the hiss? He had no outward conciousness. He was totally absorbed in chanting Harinama and in the meditation of some Lila of Radha-Krsna, manifested to him by Harinama in his mind. He went on and the cobra bit him. His outward consciousness was then revived.

Was he disturbed by the bite? He was not in the least disturbed. On the other hand he thought that Śrī Anant Deva,had mercifully informed him through the bite that death was near, so that he might collect all his energy and chant Harinama heartily before he died. So he kept aside the bag of Madhukari and sat down cross-legged and began to chant aloud. While chanting he went into Samadhi. For two days and nights he remained in Samadhi. On the third day, when his outward consciousness was revived, he found that he was perfectly healthy There was no effect or sign of the bite by the poisonous cobra.

For the vaisnavas, who are wholly dedicated to Harinama life and death are meaningless In the state in which they live their connection with the body is nominal. Connection and disconnection makes no difference to them. They can, of their own or as desired by the Lord, leave the body at any time, just as the traveller can leave the inn or the bird can leave the nest. It may appear to us that they sometimes die on account of some disease, which is painful. The pain is to the body, not to them. They are perfectly at peace, because there mind rests at the lotus feet of the Lord.

Anant Das Baba died of a disease on account of which his urine was blocked. When this happens to a patient he has excruciatingly painful experience, but Baba did not suffer any pain. The villagers, who loved and respected him immensely insisted on arranging for his treatment. But all his life he had neither accepted anyone's service nor taken any medicine. He turned down their request by saying that where Harinama was present no treatment or service of any kind was necessary and he left the body chanting Harinama in perfect peace and happiness.

## Chapter 46 - Smt. Girija Devi

The gopis of Vraja did not have to do any Sadhana for the attainment of Krsna-prem. Krsna-prem was inherent and selfmanifest (*nitya-siddha*) in them They love Him even when they had neither seen Him, nor heard anything about Him Rupa Gosvami explains this in *Ujjvalanilamani* by referring to what Radha once said to Lalita about her state of mind, when she had not seen or herd anything about Krsna She said to her, Dear Sakhi! I do not know what has happened to me My heart needlessly pines for the company of a youth, who is beautiful beyond description, whose colour is black like the colour of clouds, who wears a garment of bright yellow lightening, who is the repository of all good qualities and who is so loving and loveable. I have neither seen, nor heard about such a person I have never even imagined the possibility of the existence of a person like him. Still I do not know why I love him and shed tears for him.

It is not possible for a jiva of this world to have the *nitya-siddha* Krsna-prem of the *nitya- siddha* gopis. We can perhaps say that Girija Devi had at least a semblance of it. She was blessed with all the best things of this world that one can desire. She was the beloved wife of Śrī Nrityunjaya Prasad Sinha, a rich landlord of Jamira, in district Ara of Bihar. She had two handsome sons and two daughters, who were suitably married She also had a number of servants to serve her all the time She was called the queen of Jamira. But she knew that these things were transitory and the bond of love that tied her to her kith and kin was not pure and altogether free from selfishness. Therefore she always pined for someone, Who was eternally true and eternally her own, Whose love was pure and Who loved her and wanted to be loved by her She did not know Who that person was, but she always remembered Him and shed tears for Him.

Suddenly her older son died, when he was eighteen years old. Shortly after her younger son also died. The death of both of her sons in one year made her turn her back completely against the world and live in a world of her own. She remained oblivious even of her bodily needs. She bathed when her servants made her bathe, ate when they made her eat and did whatever they made hr do. But their activities went on mechanically without her knowledge.

Her husband was greatly worried. He requested his family guru to do something to restore her normal condition. The guru started reading Srimad Bhagavatam to her. On hearing Bhagavata she came to realize that the person, Who had so far been residing in her heart unnoticed and making her weep, was Śrī Krsna. Śrī Krsna also could no more remain unnoticed. He appeared before her. On account of excess of bliss on seeing Him, to which her frail body and mind were not accustomed, she became unconscious.

On regaining consciousness she began to look all around for Krsna Not finding Him anywhere she again became unconscious. It became difficult for her to live without Krsna. She asked her husband to send her to Vrndavana, where she now decided to live for the rest of her life and do Krsna*-bhajan.*

Her husband was in a dilemma. He could not send her to Vrndavana to live there alone, because that would have been against the tradition and honour of the family. How could a lady, of a very respectable Thakur family, who lived strictly in purdah be allowed to go to Vrndavana to live there alone without purdah? How could she be allowed t go and live in Vrndavana in a rented house in austerity, when all her life she had lived in a palace in luxury? At the same time how could she be restrained from going to Vrndavana? Keeping her away from Krsna and Vrndavana would mean her death. He had ultimately agreed to send her to Vrndavana with one or two maid-servants.

She went to Vrndavana and began to live in a rented house, behind the temple of Radharamana. The house belonged to Śrī Nilamani Gosvami, one of the Servants of Radharamana. Since she had now come so close to Radharamana. Radharamana got an opportunity to play hide and seek with her He sometimes appeared before her directly, sometimes through a dream and disappeared. Some times He possessed some person and spoke to her through him.

Girija Devi had accepted Śrī Hari Gosvami, another Servant of Radharamana, as her Tirtha- guru. Many times he was suddenly possessed by Radharamana and in that state he went to Girija Devi, talked with her, gave her certain instructions and came back When dispossessed he did not know why he had gone to Girija Devi and what instructions he had given her. Sometimes while talking with her in the state of possession he would suddenly become dispossessed. He was then surprised and embarrassed to find himseif sitting near her. Girija Devi knew that in that state it was Radharamana Who spoke to her through him and she followed the instructions He gave.

But Girija Devi did not like Radharaman s talking with her through another person instead of appearing directly before her She pretended to be angry with Him. She used to chant His name through Mahamantra. She decided to chant the name of Radha instead of His name The day she took that decision Radharaman possessed Nilamani Gosvami and spoke to her through him. Nilamani Gosvami lived in a house close to her. He was at that time ill and was lying on bed. Possessed suddenly by Radharaman he began to shout in a state of delirium, Call Girija Bibi,Call Girija Bibi. His wife went and brought Girija Devi. As soon as she came Gosvami Ji held her hand and said, Will you forsake me? Tell me.

Girija Devi began to look at him. She did not understand what he meant He said again, Will you now chant only the name of Radha, not my name? Will you certainly forsake me?

Girija Devi then understood Who was speaking through Gosvami Ji. Tears streamed out of her eyes and she said, No, No, How can I forsake You? Immediately Gosvami Ji became dispossessed. He said to Girija Devi, How are you here?

You called me, said Girija Devi

No I didn t.

What else could Girija Devi say? She made obeisance to him and left.

Girija Ji always concealed her *bhakti-bhav.* She could not be easily recognized as a great devotee She smoked *hukka.* Sadhus also used to come to her. When people saw her smoking *hukka* and chatting with the Sadhus, they thought that she was a worlly-minded lady, who only whiled away her time in gossips with the Sadhus, though she did not talk with them about anything except Krsna Ramakanta Gosvmi, another Servant of Radharaman and the present secretary of the managing committee of Radharman temple, also had a similar opinion about her.

But how can a devotee conceal himself, if Krsna wants that he should be known and adored. Radharaman once performed a Lila so that Ramakant Ji might know her well.

The Servant of Radharaman serve Radharaman by turns. It was Ramakant Ji s turn off service, when this Lila took place One day he went to Mathura for some important work, entrusting the service of Radharaman to another Servant. He returned late at midnight. He was surprised to see Girija Devi and a number of Gosvamis waiting for his arrival at his door. As soon as he arrived the Gosvami s said, There has been a serious lapse in the service of Radharaman today.

What lapse? asked Ramakanta with anxiety The *karava* containing drinking water was not placed by the side of His bed at the time of His going to sleep.

It shouldn t be so. How did you come to know? inquired Ramakant. Radharaman Himself went to Girija Bibi and complained about it. Is that so Bibi Ji? Ramakant asked her. Yes, Maharaj, Śrī Jicame to me and said that He was thirsty.

Ramakant Ji did not believe. How could Radharaman be so close to a lady, who only smoked *hukka* and gossiped, that He would go and speak to her about his sorrows and sufferings, he thought. However he bathed immediately and opened the temple. He was surprised to see that the *karava* was not there

Once Nilamani Gosvami thought of asking Girija Devi to vacate his house so that he might let it out to some one else on higher rent. Radharaman did not like this. He devised a Lila, on account of which Nilamani Gosvami was himself ousted from his house for some time with his family. One day he locked his house and went somewhere with all the members of his family On his return he tried to unlock the house, but the lock did not open. He turned the key again and again for a long time, but in vain. His neighbours tried hard to open the lock, but it did not open. No one could understand why the lock, which opened easily on other days baffled all their attempts to open it that day. There lived in the neighbourhood a devoted lady from Rampur. She felt inspired by Radharani to say to Nilamani Gosvami, Maharaj! You have committed some offence against Girija Devi on account of which the lock does not open. If you request her to open the lock, it will open. Nilamani Ji thought that perhaps it was Radharaman, Who was speaking through her, because he had actually committed an offence against her in the morning by thinking of asking her to vacate the house, and that it was perhaps a punishment for the same that Radharaman had kept him out of his house so long. Apologizing in his mind to Radharaman and Girija Devi for the offense committed against her, Nilamani Gosvami went to Girija Devi and said, Bibi Ji! The lock of my house does not open. Every one has tried and failed I think it will open, if you kindly try. Excuse me for requesting you kindly to take this trouble.

Girija Ji laughed. She said, If none of you could open the lock, how can l?

You may at least try Gosvami Ji insisted. Girija Ji went and as soon as she applied the key to the lock, it opened She was surprised. She understood that it was also one of the Lilas of lila-loving Radharaman.

Girija-Devi sometimes enjoyed such close association with Radharaman that some sign of His association remained in her body even when He was gone. Once He left in her sari a supernatural smell, which lasted for several days. Wearing the same sari she went to the temple for the *darshan* of Radharaman Her presence in the temple filled it with a supernatural smell such as no one had experienced before. One of the Gosvamis said, Bibi Ji! Even in your old age you are so fond of scent.

Girija Ji felt embarrassed but there was nothing for her to say She only blamed Radharaman and expressed her anger against Him for trying to manifest her as much as she tried to conceal herself.

Girija Devi s heart was now overfilled with Krsna-prem Therefore it always spilled in the for of tears of her eyes. Even when she sat talking with some people tears flowed from her eyes inspite of all her efforts to control them.

Sometimes she used to be so absorbed in this experience of Krsna-lila, which she saw enacted before her mind s ey that she lost all contact with the external world Though her eyes and ears remained open she could neither see nor hear. If some one wanted to draw her attention, he had to shout loudly Radhey near her ear.

Even then sometimes she heard, sometimes not. When she heard, she responded by saying Radhey in such a way that it appeared as if she was speaking from some far off place Sometimes, when she sat alone in her room, with doors latched from inside, one could hear her talking with some one, sometimes the jingling sound of *nupurs* was heard of someone, wearing *nupurs,* moving about in the room; sometimes a sweet and superb smell came out of the room and filled the house.

Once Girija Ji went to Siddha Śrī Gauranga Das Baba Ji for his *darshan* and *satsang* in his Ashram in Ramanreti in Vrndavana He was at that time surrounded by a number of other devotees. When she went away, Gauranga Das Baba Ji took the dust of the place, where she was sitting, and applied it to his forehead The people said, Baba! What are you doing?

He replied, I am doing what Uddhava and Brahma did and what Śrī Krsna Himself did That lady is not ordinary. She is a gopi

Girija Devi had gone to Vrndavana with determination never to return. But after sometime her husband died and she was compelled to go to Jamira. She began to pray to Radharaman to accept her in His service in the eternal Vrndavana from where there was no return.

At that time a Mahatma came to Jamira who said that he wanted to perform a grand *yajna,* which would last for two months. Girija Devi took upon herself the entire responsibility of the *yajna.* The *yajna* was performed with great eclat. For two months Jamira was charged with spiritual current of *bhava-bhakti* as it has never experienced before.

Immediately after the conclusion of the *yajna* Girija Devi left her physical body to meet her Radharaman in celestial Vrndavan

## Chapter 47 - Śrī Gauranga Das Baba Ji (Madan Thaur)

We have already described the life of siddha Śrī Gauranga Das Baba Ji of Ramanreti, Vrndavana. Another Siddha Mahatma of the same name lived near the Samadhi of Sanatan Gosvami in Madan Thaur in Vrndavana. He was too. old and he could walk with great difficulty It was not necessary for him to move out of his *kuti,* because people respected him as a Siddha Mahatma and wanted to serve him with their heart and soul and cater to all his needs and requirements. But he believed that dependence on any one for anything was an obstacle in *bhajan.* Even though he could not walk easily and he took half an hour to climb the few steps at the door of the Samadhi, he went out for *madhukari* every day. He led a very austere life and never touched money. One night Gauanga Das Baba could not sleep. He passed the whole night turning sides on his bed in a peculiar kind of restlessness In the morning he called a vaisnava, who lived nearby, and said, Look Baba if there are bugs in my bed. They did not let me sleep the whole night, As the vaisnava removed the bedding he saw not bugs but some rupees spread out on the cot. He took out the rupees and shoed them to Baba. As soon as Baba saw them, he was shocked and scared, as if he saw not the rupees, but a snake or scorpion.
