He said, Look, that is why I said there is something underneath my bedding, which did not let me sleep throughout night. Rupees! And the rupees not of an ordinary person. But of a licentious Raja, who came yesterday! Could they let me sleep? The poison of snake or scorpion kills only when they bite, but the poison of rupees kills without biting. Take them away from here atonce. Offer some *bhog* with the money to Madanmohan and distribute *prasad* amongst the vaisnavas.

The previous day a Raja had come to Baba for his *darshan* He saw that Baba did not touch money He had secretly kept some money under his bedding so that he might use it through some one without himself touching it. He did not know that the money could plaque Baba even without his touching it. This reminds one of the experience of Siddha Śrī Jaganath Das Baba Ji, who was offered a rupee by someone. While being carried somewhere in his old age by his disciple Bihari on his shoulder. Bihari put the money in his pocket. Even then the rupee began to bite Baba like a scorpion, because it had come to his knowledge and he had touched it with his mind, if not with his hand Bihari had gone only a few steps forward when Baba asked him to turn back. He went to the house of the donor and said to him, You are a rich man. I could not bear the bite of a single rupee. I wonder how you bear the bite of thousands of them.

A rupee neither stings nor bites, but it is the most powerful agent of Maya, which plagues and bites The bite is not felt by a worldly man, who is accustomed to it. But the Mahatmas, whose heart is pure cannot tolerate it. A dot of dirt cannot be easily detected on a cloth, which is dirty, but it is easily detected on a sheet of cloth, which is spotlessly white. Similarly even the subtlest attack of Maya directly or indirectly is easily detected and repulsed by Siddha Mahatmas like Gauranga Das Baba Ji, whose heart is pure like crystal.

That Gauranga Das Baba had not allowed Maya in any form to come anywhere near him is apparent from the manner in which he passed away from this world. Only an hour before he left the body he called the manager of the temple of Madanmohan and said, You take away everything that you find in my *kuti. "*

The manager said, Baba! There is nothing in your *kuti* that anyone can use.

Baba felt satisfied. Only a short while after he said to Sanatan Das, the pujari of the Samadhi of Sanatan Gosvami, Sanatan! Look, Mahaprabhu has come. Give Him asan, give Him *asan* to sit. As he said this people, who had come to the Samadhi of Sanatan Gosvami for *darshan,* saw a flash of supernatural light appear in his *kuti.* With the disappearance of the light he also disappeared from this world.

## Chapter 48 - Śrī Nityanand Das Baba Ji (Nandgaon)

Nityanand Das Baba Ji was born about the year 1890 as the son of the Raja of Dharakot in Orissa. It was settled at the time of his birth that he would be adopted by the Raja of Parikred and govern the state after him. But he was born with strong Sansakaras of Bhakti. According to a design of the Lord he was to be the Raja not of Parikred, but of the hearts of thousands of people as their spiritual master and preceptor. Even before the adoption ceremony could take place he sneaked out of home. He first went to Alalnath, beyond Jagannath Puri. There he had the *darshan* of the eight feet long Silaon which Śrī Chaitanya Mahaprabhu had once prostrated while making obeisance to the deity Alalnath and left on it the sign of every par of his body that touched it. As Nityanand made obeisance to the Sila his heart was overfilled with *prem* and he became unconscious.

From Alalnath he went to Puri and from Puri to Navadvip and Vrndavana. In Vrndavana he took mantra-diksa from Śrī Madhusudan Gosvami, a Gosvami in the disciplic succession of Gadadhar Pandit, one of the closest associates of Śrī Caitanya Mahaprabhu and Sannyas-diksa from Śrī Shyamcaran Baba Ji, a Baba Ji in the disciplic succession of Gopal guru. Thus dressed as a vaisnava Sannyasi the young Baba Ji, who would have been the Raja of Parikud, began to roam about in the villages and the forests of Vraj, always chanting the name and living on whatever he got in Madhukari He did not live at one place, because he was afraid that his parents might come and take him back He slept on each night under a different tree While sleeping he di. d not lie down, but kept reclining on the trunk of the tree so that he might sleep as little a possible and chant as much as possible. Slowly he conquered sleep to such an extent that he slept only two or three hours a day.

Once he decided to do kirtan continuously for three days and nights In his imagination he wrote a letter to Nidra Devi (the goddess of sleep) praying not to come near him during this period and kept the letter under the feet of the Lord. Thus making Nidra Devi herself sleep comfortably under the lotus feet of the Lord for three days. He did *kirtan* for three days and nights without a wink of sleep. He did not himself go for Madhukari during this period. Another Baba Ji brought Madhukari for him and did *kirtan* in his place for as long as he took in going for the call of nature, bathing and eating

Nityanand Baba moved from place to place for a number of years. Then he settled in Nandgaon. He began to live in the Kadambakhandiof Nandgaon. There he once performed the *purash- charan* of Mahamantra;After *puruash-charan* he became Siddha and his fame as a Siddha Mahatma spread far and wide. In the Kumbha- mela of Vrndavana he was elected as the Mahantof the four vaisnava Sampradayas. He also soon has a large number of disciples. The empire of which the Lord had desired him to be the Raja began to come into shape. How much the Lord was Himself instrumental in building that empire for him is evident from the following example:

A young boy who had renounced the world, was standing before Lord Jagannath in the temple of Jagannath in Puri and saying, Prabhu! I have renounced the world in order that I may attain Your lotus feet by doing *bhajan* under the guidance of a Guru. But the guru can be attained only by Your mercy. I have heard stories of Your causeless mercy upon the helpless and unworthy *jivas* like me. Would you not have mercy on me? He was praying like this and weeping. At that time the figures of Balaram and Subhadra standing on His either side disappeared from his view. He saw only the big round face of Jagannath shedding tears from His eyes. Jagannath was weeping, because He pined for his company as much as he pined for Him. But he could not understand this. He thought that He was weeping because he had committed some offence against Him

He went to the temple of Vimila Devi. Before her also he wept and prayed in the same manner. He saw one hand of the Śrī Murti of Vimila moving. Perhaps by moving her hand she indicated that he had her blessings But this also he could not understand.

He then went to Navadvip in search of the guru. There one day, while he was having the *darshan* of Gauranga Mahaprabhu in the temple of Mahaprabhu and praying to him for the discovery of the guru, he saw a strange scene. He first saw Mahaprabhu the figure of Śrī Krsna, then Radha and then of a blue coloured Sakhi. He was surprised The whole scene remained to him a mystery. But as he had this *darshan* he felt prompted by Mahaprabhu to go to Vrndavana.

On reaching Vrndvana, as soon as he got down from the train, he saw Nityanand Das Baba: He had once met Nityanand Das Baba in Puri. Therefore Baba recognized him. He took him to Nandgram. The boy lived with him and soon recognized him as his guru. He took Mantra- *diksa* fro him and felt relieved of his anxiety.

He requested gurudev to give him his photo so that he might keep it with him and worship. Gurudev sent for Kali Babu, the photographer, who has his studio in Gopinath Bazar in Vrndavana. When Kali Babu had set the camera to take Baba s photo, Baba asked the boy to go and see whether the camera was properly focused on him The boy took the permission of the photographer and flung the black curtain of the camera over his head.

As he fixed his eyes on Gurudev through the lens, he was surprised to see not gurudev, but the Sakhi, whom he had seen in the Vigraha of Śrī Gauranga in Navadvip. On removing the curtain he saw not the Sakhi, but his gurudev Śrī Nityanand Das Baba.

The boy understood that his gurudev was a Sakhi of Radharani, that Gauranga Mahaprabhu had by appearing to him as Krsna, Radha and his Gurudev in the form of Sakhi had tried to convey to him that Krsna and Radha were non- different from Him and that his Guru in the form of Sakhi was also non-different from Radharani as one of Her manifestations. He also understood that Gauranga Mahaprabhu had by prompting him to go to Vrndavana and Nityanand Baba to go to the station just at the time of his arrival there had mercifully helped him in finding his guru.

In this way Shyamsundar Das, Gopinath Das, Gaurgovind Das and many others, who later became famous Mahatmas as well as thousands of householder devotees were made by the Lord to accept him as their guru and add to the glory of the spiritual empire of which he became the Raja.

Ever since Nityanand Das Baba became Siddha, he tried to conceal himself But how can one conceal himself if the Lord wants him to be known by everyone and adored No one knew him in far off Amritsar. There also the Lord made him known through a devotee, who served the Śrī Vigraha of Radha-Shymasundar Shymasundar once said to him in a dream You take us both to Nityanand Das of Nandgaon, whose loving service we now want to enjoy The devotee took them to Nityanand Baba and told him all about the dream. Baba recognized Archan or the service of Śrī Vigraha as one of the important forms' of Bhakti, but he was himself not interested in it He was a free lance He wanted to stay where he liked and go where he liked, living only on Madhukari and doing *kirtan*, which he regarded as the highest form of *bhajan* The service of Śrī Vigraha pinned one down to one place and compelled him to beg for all kinds of things required for the service of the deity or demanded by Him But since Radha-Shyamasundar had now come of their own, he had to say to the devotees, Look, I am incapable of rendering proper service to Radha-Shyamasundar But since They want me to serve Them, you leave Them here. I shall do what I can

The devotee felt very much grieved at heart to notice Nityanand Baba s indifferent and somewhat disregardful attitude towards the divine couple, Who had chosen to be his guests. Any other guest would have frowned at this and turned back. But Radha-Shyamsundar had not come to go back. They were aware that uninvited guests are not only unwelcome, they have sometimes to suffer indignation and admonition of the host.

Still They had come. They were strange guests, Who enjoyed the indignation and admonition of Their devotees even more then their praises.

Baba installed the Śrī Vigrahas of Radha Shyamasundar in a *kuti* near Mukhashodan-kund in Nandgram and deputed his disciples in Their service This *kuti* became a small ashram of Baba. He did not make any special arrangements for the service of Radha-Shyamasundar. They were offered in *bhog* whatever the Baba Ji s got in Madhukari. If a disciple wanted to make any special arrangement for Them he said, We are not going to change ourselves on account of Them We shall remain the beggar Baba Ji s that we are. It is They Who will have to change Themselves. Since They have come to Baba Jis, They will have to live like Baba Jis. They will have to eat what we eat, If any day they desire to eat *malapura* or *laddu* they can go to Nand Baba s place, which is near, and sat.

After the attainment of Siddhi Nityanand Das Baba decided to pass the rest of his life in preaching Harinam Sankirtana. With this purpose he founded a society, called Haibhakti Pracharim Sabha, with its centre in Mathura. He performed AkhandHarinam Sankirtan at Hathara, Aligarh, Jaipur, Delhi, Amritsar, Vrndavana, Jagannathpuri and a number of other places. At each place the Sankirtana lasted for a month, two months or a year. He also performed a month long Akhand kirtan every yea a the time of Rathayatra in Jagannath Puri and at the time of Kumbha in Prayag, Haridwar and other places. He used to say that Nam-kirtan was the most important of all kinds of Bhakti-sadhana. Harinam yielded fruit not only if sung but also if heard, written or seen in the written form

He said that there are two stages of Nam- sadhana. The first is the stage of *nama-grah,* in which the *sadhak* has to make an effort to chant the name a prescribed number of times. The second is the stage of *namashrya,* in which the *sadhak* is completely surrendered to Nam He can t live without chanting the Nam. He knows nothing except the Nam. In this stage the Nam attracts Krsna and He is bound to appear before the *sadhak.*

Baba was not against *ashtayam-lila-smaran.* But he said that Nam-Sankirtan was superior to *lila-smaran.* He said that Nam was a wish- fulfilling tree (*kalpa-tarn*). There is nothing which the *sadhak* could not attain, if he took shelter under it. Continuous watering of the tree through *kirtan* or *japa* made it sprout and grow into leaves and branches of Krsna-lila. In the initial stage of the *sadhak* he discouraged *Lila-smaran.* He said, "*Lila-smaran* is not joke. The *sadhak,* who does *lila-smaran* has to undertake some kind of service of the divine couple in *smaran.* They accept the service regularly done in *smaran.* Failure to render the service at appointed times means offence. Nityanand Das once told his disciples that a Baba Ji used to render the service of *peckadan* in *smaran* to Radha-Krsna in Seva Kunj. Once he could not render this service on account of some other occupation. Next morning, when he went to Sevakunj, he was aghast to see the red marks of spitting our the *peek* here and there in Sevakunj. It was evident from this that the *Sadhak,* who did *lila-smaran* had to be free from all other occupations.

Nityanand Baba also believed in the importance of Sadhu-seva.Wherever he organized *Nam- yajna* he also performed Sadhu-seva in a grand scale. What is surprising is that he never touched money. He went to the place empty handed and returned empty handed. But at the time of Sadhu-seva men and money mysteriously poured in from somewhere. Sometimes he said to a merchant, I have to perform Sadhu-seva after the Nam-yajna. You supply all the provisions.

I shall pay whatever money comes for the *yajna.* If I Cannot pay the full amount, I shall pay what remains on some other occasion with interest. If in the meantime I die, I shall take birth again to clear your debt. The merchant knew that Baba always got sufficient money to pay for the bill and he never ran into debt. So he gladly gave him everything in as much quantity as was needed,

Once he performed Nam-yajna in Vrndavana at the time of Kumbha-mela Seth Hargulal, the richest man of Vrndavana at that time, came to him and said, Baba! I Understand that you are going to perform Nam-yajna, if I can be of service, kindly let me know.

Service! Would you do the service I suggest? Said Baba in a loud tone

Why not Baba? You have only to order Alright. Send one hundred tins of *ghee. "*  What will you do with one tins of *ghee* Baba?

You will see what I do.

Hargulal did not believe. He sent ten tins of *ghee*. Ten tins of *ghee* was meaningless for Baba in view of the grand scale in which he wanted to do Sadhu-seva.

The same night radha-Krsna said in a dream to a big merchant of Kanpur, In Vrndavana at the time of Kumbha-mela MahantNityanand Das Baba is doing Nam-yajna Send him one hundred tins of *ghee* and wheat flour, sugar and other things in correspondingly similar quantity.

The next day the merchant arrived at the camp of Nityanand Das Baba with 100 tins of *ghee* and truck loads of other provisions. He said to Baba, Baba! Are you Mahant Nityanand Das Baba? Last night Radha-Krsna asked me in a dream to supply these provisions to you for the *bhandara*, you are going to hold. Kindly accept.

Baba said, I am not a Mahant. I also do not know anything about the *bhandara.* Mahant and Mahant are They, who appeared to you in the dream. They will do the *bhandara.* I shall only watch. You can leave the provisions.

After the conclusion of the Nam-yajna Baba performed the *bhandara,* in which all the Sadhus of all the Sampradayas were fed. The size of the *bhandara* can be imagined from the fact that 200 cooks had to be engaged for it.

Baba organized a similar one year-long *kirtan- yajna* and *bhandara* at Hatharas. A specialty of the *yajna* was that the Śrī Vigraha of Mahaprabhu of Gopinath Bazar and Siddha Śrī Gauranga Das Baba Ji of Ramanreti, Vrndavana also graced the occasion with their visit to Hatharas. When the devotees of Hatharas requested Nityanand Das Baba that the Śrī Vigraha of Mahaprabhu should also be carried to Hatharas, Baba said, How is that possible? Śrī Vigraha should not be carried from one place to another except in time of emergency. You do not know how much the big Śrī Vigraha of Mahaprabhu would be inconvenienced, if He is carried from Vrndavana to Hatharas. If you insist on taking Him there, I shall not go. " But on seeing the loving insistence of the devotees Mahaprabhu Himself became restless to go. He said to Nityanand Das Baba, I shall go. Nityanand Das Baba then said, I shall go only if Mahaprabhu goes. The Servant of Mahaprabhu, Śrī Krsna Caitanya Gosvami did not know what to do. He could neither disregard the insistence of the Mahapurusha and the devotees, nor tolerate the inconvenience and the hardship Mahaprabhu would have to go through in being carried so far. When the devotees requested him for permission to take Mahaprabhu to Hatharas he said, What can I say? Ask Mahaprabhu Himself by drawing lots before Him. Two slips of paper, on one of which was written I shall go on the other I shall not go, were folded and placed before Mahaprabhu. A child was asked to pick one of them. He picked the one, on which was written, I shall go, The devotees sprang up with joy and shouted Mahaprabhu Ki Jai. They took Him to Hatharas with utmost care to see that no inconvenience was caused to Him.

For one year Akhand Harinam kirtan and *katha* went on in Hatharas. *Kirtan* was held round the clock, *katha* everyday in the evening. At the time of *katha,* discourse were delivered first by Nityanand Das Baba, then by Gauranga Das Baba Nityanand Das Baba spoke on *Sadhan,* Gauranga Das Baba spoke on *Rasa.* Nityanand used to say to the devotees, I clean your hearts by speaking on Sadhan, Gauranga Das Baba makes it attractive for Krsna by filling it with *rasa. "* Even today when the devotees of Hatharas think of the Rasa-ganga which flowed in Hatharas during those days and in which they freely bathed, their eyes are filled with tears and the hairs of their body stand on end.

After Hatharas Baba organized a similar one year-long *kirtana-yajna* in Aligarh. Thousands of men and women participated in the *yajna.* The women participated during the day, the men at night. In the *yajna* there was shortage of *ghee.* Baba asked each woman to bring one spoonful of *ghee.* So much *ghee* was collected that not spoons, but streams of it were constantly poured into the *yajna.* After the conclusion of the *yajna* sweets were left over in such huge quantity that a packet of sweets was sent as *prasad* to the house of each Hindu of Aligarh. Besides so much money was received in donation that two halls were constructed for *kirtan* and *satsang.*

In the *yajna* the people got an opportunity to have a glimpse of Nityanand Baba s many supernatural powers or Siddhis, including his *Asan-siddhi.* He used to be busy throughout the day in *katha* and *kirtan.* At 6 o clock in the evening he took *prasad.* From 6 o clock to 8 o clock he rested. From 8 o clock to 4 o clock in the morning he remained sitting on the same *Asan* and did Harinam-japa.

Baba also preached Harinam-kirtan in Orissa. There he introduced *kirtan-saptah* or one week long *kirtan* like the *bhagavat-saptah,* prevalent in Northern India.

Baba did not normally use his supernatural powers. Once while Akhand-kirtan was going on in Hatharas, a man pitifully requested him to cure his only son, who was about to die on account of an incurable disease. He expressed his inability to do anything. One of his disciples said, Baba! If you had only lied and said that the boy would be allright the man would have at least temporarily felt relieved. Baba replied, Fool! Even if I had so lied, the boy would have been saved. But what good would have come out of. that. He would have still been in the dirty ditch of Maya and suffered birth and death. I am the Baba, who pulls people out of the ditch instead of seeing them rot in it.

But Baba some times used his power to instill in the hearts of people faith in the divine or to strengthen it. Once Gopinath Das Baba, one of his disciples, who had been professor of English in the Training College, Barahanipur, said to him, Baba! I have heard about the benedictory hand of the guru. I do not understand what exactly it means. Would you kindly explain?

Baba said, Benedictory hand means this as he said this he stretched his benedictory hand so as to place it about a foot above his head. Immediately he felt that a soothing current passed through his body and filled him with bliss. Also he was attacked with such intense horripilation that he could not control it.

## Chapter 49 - Raghunath Das Gosvami

We know that the rivers Ganga, Yamuna and Sarasvatimet at Prayaga and the confluence of the three rivers was called Triveni. The river Sarasvati branched off a short way down and, flowing in a semicircular course, met the Ganga again at Alipur.A new Triveni was thus formed in Bengal. It is near this Triveni that in Puranic times the seven sons of Raj Priyavrata practised *sadhana* after accepting *sannyas.* Since then the place has been called Saptagram.

During the days of the Mohammedan rulers Saptagram was a flourishing town. It was the residence of a large number of Europeans, whose ships lay anchored on the river Sarasvati, laden with merchandise.

It was the most important port for the seagoing vessels in Bengal. The Emperor of Gauda had leased the town and its adjoining villages to two influential Kayastha Chiefs Hiranya and Govardhan. The condition was that they would pay a revenue of 12 *lacs of* rupees a year and the Emperor would not interfere with the internal administration of the district. Under this new arrangement his annual income from the land alone rose to 20 *lacs,* besides which the taxes on the exports.

One practising religious discipline for the attainment of a religious end.

Practice of religious discipline for the realisation of the Lord.

A present due to the guru tor siksa given by him.

Srngara means decoration, vata means banyan tree. Srngaravata is so called because here under a banyan tree Krsna once decorated Radharani.

A delicious food prepared from pulse and wheat flour.

Seeing

Repeating a mantra and counting the repetitions silently over the beads

A form of begging specially prevalent in Vraja The saint begs for pieces of bread from several houses like the bee that collects honey from several flowers. He refuses to accept the whole meal from a particular house

Suffering in separation from Krsna.

Kanhaiya and Baladau are nicknames of Krsna and Baladeva. These are also most common names of boys in Vraja.

An earthen pot with a nozzle generally used by the saints in Vraja.

In madhura-rasa the sadhaka develops the conceit of a gopi and regards himself as the beloved of Krsna or a companion of Radha.

A loose garment for women hanging from the waist downwards.

A cloth worn by women from the head downwards up to waist.

Discussion on the true intent of the sastras.

World conquerer in sastrartha.

Burning of a chaste wife on the funeral pyre of her husband.

The strand or principle of worldly activity.

Term of endearment for a girl.

External signs of internal emotion attendant upon Krsna-prema They are Stambha (stupor), Sveda (perspiration), Romanca (thrilling of the body), Svara-bhanga (break of voice), Vepathu (trembling), Vaivarunya (change of colour), Asru (tears), and Pralaya (loss of consciousness).

Sanskrta Grammar compiled by Śrī Jiva Gosvami.

Lila pertaining to eight parts of the day, each part consisting of three hours.

mane nija siddha deha kariya bhavana, ratri dine kare vraje krsnera sevena.

Śrī Visvanatha Cakravarti interprets the text to mean that Bhagavan imparts to the devotee a transcendental body exactly like the one, which he imagines himself to possess and which is essential for the particular mode of bhakti practised by him, because He is bound to do so on account of His always being subservient to the devotee.

When any effect of the divine lila on the transcendental body appears on the physical body that is regarded as a sign of siddhi or accomplishment in bhajana.

A sakhi, who in her loving service is inclined towards Radha more than towards Krsna. Her bhava is purer than that of other sakhis. On account of the purity of her bhava she is entitled to the service of Radha-Krsna even in their esoteric lila.

An earthen pot with a nozzle.

A girdle set with tiny bells producing a sweet jingling sound

A tinkling ornament worn round the ankles.

A lake In a village of the same name near Govardhana.

A lake in Govardhana.

Spontaneous, flowing naturally from Raga or attachment to the Lord.

The water in which the foot of the deity, guru, or a brahmin is washed.

Three curved horizontal marks made on the forehead by the worshippers of Siva or Kail. (Gaudiya Vaisnava Jivana, vol. II, P. 154).

An oval shaped drum generally used in samkirtana.

Continuous repetition of a mantra for a specified period under specified rules and regulations with the object of attaining a particular end.

Flour cake baked at home.

Mat, carpet or anything to sit on.

Earthen vessel for keeping water

A hall in front of a temple.

Remnants of their food.

A follower of tantra books prescribing a code of religious ceremonies particularly for the worship of Siva and Durga.

Mahasaya means a respectable person or a mahatma.

Naubata-Khana is a place above the entrance-gate of a temple or palace reserved for the musicians, who beat the drum and play on sahanai (a musical instrument like flute) The naubata-khana Is usually infested by pigeons, who continue to live there happily, as if totally unconcerned with the disturbing sound of the drum.

Accepting alms or daksina for Bhagavata-patha is regarded as an offence Also the vaisnavas are not supposed to accept alms from a king or queen or any other visayi (worldly minded) person.

Balarama or Baladeva.

A yellow sheet worn as upper garment by Krsna.

A book describing the daily activities (lila) of Krsna as performed during the eight parts of the day, each part consisting of three hours. In each part of the day the devotee meditates upon the lila pertaining to that particular part.

Lanile Sarakara was the deity Balagopala, presented to Narayana Bhatta Gosvami by Śrī Krsna Himself. Lanile Gopala, like Lokanatha Gosvami's deity Gokulananda, helped Narayana Bhatta Gosvami in the discovery of lila- sthalies of Vraja.

A place near Radharamana temple in Vrndavana, which abounds in thick cluster of low-lying trees and is supposed to be connected with amorous pastimes of Radha and Krsna
